# Gap costing: which drawn affordances the CLI can be given cheaply

**Status:** DRAFT for Teddy (2026-09-08 03:28 UTC; revised 2026-09-08 03:45 UTC against `BRIEF.md`, `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` and the running loop's `GAPS.md` / `FIDELITY.md` — the revision re-framed the header, §0, the calls and §8 and annotated superseded premises; no costed row or verdict was changed or removed). Not a spec. Answers Teddy's directive verbatim: "investigate the lower cost gaps that we can very simply build into it. This should include most of the gaps." Written by the spec-track session; the tabular sections are generated deterministically from the reconciled batch files by `assemble_doc.py` (scratchpad `costing/`), so no row is retyped; the prose sections are written by hand.
**Decision context — CLOSED, not an open question (Teddy, 2026-09-07; `design/terum-skills/.review/2026-09-08-overnight/BRIEF.md:1` marks the file FINAL and "never re-ask"):** the question this costing was originally framed against — flag the gaps, or hold the app spec-clean until the CLI catches up — is **decided**, and nothing below re-opens it. **`BRIEF.md:6`:** every screen is built as drawn; a capability flag exists ONLY for the four hard, non-integral gaps — per-machine disable, the Inbox event log and the `offtarget` kind, a machine registry, per-case eval tables — and **those four are the only affordances behind a flag**. Everything else is built as if real (the mock says yes, no flag), mock data is fine everywhere, and every drawn affordance the CLI on `main` lacks is one line in `GAPS.md` at the app repo root: screen, affordance, what the CLI would need. (The app realises the four as **five** named `Capabilities` keys, because the Inbox event log and `offtarget` are flagged separately — `GAPS.md:28-32` in the app repo — five bullets under the capability-flag heading (`GAPS.md:26`, which names no number), against the four gaps `BRIEF.md:6` names; the mock reports every one `true`. Prose that says four and prose that says five are counting the same set.) Printed command strings stay as drawn — the boards say `share` — while the seam method is `connect`, and every printed-string or verb/hint change is a **design-side call Teddy makes, never patched overnight** (`BRIEF.md:28`, `:33`, `:35`). Under that decision this document's job is exact and is stated in §0: for every drawn affordance the CLI lacks, name the cheapest honest way to make it real — a CLI change (an ask to Ryan or Ajay, or `codex-implement` work), an app-side derivation the D20 rule allows, or a design-side string — and name the few that are genuinely not cheap. ## 0. What this document answers now

Teddy's decision (`BRIEF.md:6`) settles *whether* to flag; what is left is *price and routing*. This document answers, for the app that is being built as drawn: **which `GAPS.md` lines and which drawn-as-real affordances can be made real cheaply in the CLI, and by whom.** It produces three things.

1. **The SIMPLE CLI-side set — 62 rows** (31 recommended `cli`, 31 `both`; recounted over `costing/cost-*.final.json` at revision time). By approver: **45 Ryan, 9 Ajay, 8 with no approver or a mixed one**. Each is either an **ask to Ryan or Ajay** (§2.1 has the two bundles as written) or **`codex-implement` work** in a worktree of the CLI repo (spec delta → `codex-spec` → `codex-implement` → `hybrid-review`, never a push to main, and only after the loop). §2 groups them by owner file with a paste-ready one-paragraph spec delta **for the 54 rows that need one**; eight rows carry no delta — **AC-07** and **CP-14** because the CLI route is refused by the bar, and **CP-13**, **EV-11**, **IB-08**, **RM-27**, **RM-45**, **RM-47** because none is required (recounted over `costing/cost-*.final.json`). Which route to take is call 1 below — **the only unanswered CLI-routing call this document carries**; call 2 is not a fork this document opens (see "The calls for Teddy").
2. **A line-by-line map from the loop's `GAPS.md`, and from its seed, to the costed rows** — §8: all 37 `- ` lines with row ids and verdicts, plus the **146 costed rows `GAPS.md` does not carry**, listed by verdict and side so the loop's file can be extended from this costing afterwards (47 of them are SIMPLE CLI-side and are the ones worth appending). Where a `GAPS.md` line and a costing row disagree, the `GAPS.md` line's own text is the authority.
3. **The flag boundary restated, priced, not re-decided.** The four gaps at `BRIEF.md:6` are the only affordances behind a capability flag, and **nothing below proposes a flag for anything else**. What the costing adds is what each costs and how much of each drawn surface survives without the mechanism: per-machine disable **MC-05 — HARD**; the `offtarget` kind **IB-03 — HARD** (recommended cut); per-case eval tables **EV-03 — MEDIUM**, additive because the receipt is `.passthrough()`; the **machine registry** has no single row, because the drawn surfaces do not need the mechanism — MC-04 ("your other machines") is **CLOSED** (the drawn row is an em-dash plus a sentence already true at `b5c0507`) and MC-03 (footer machine label) is **SIMPLE · app** chrome; the **Inbox event log**'s drawn consequences are IB-01, IB-02 and MC-02, all **SIMPLE** (a recomputed-at-sync derivation and unread state, not a log). Each flag stays because the *mechanism* is absent, not because the screen is.

Everything else — **166 of 196 rows are SIMPLE** — is built as if real, and where the CLI cannot back it, it is one `GAPS.md` line. One correction the decision forces on this document's own prose: **PF-08 (opt out of publishing install records) is HARD but is *not* on Teddy's flag list**; it is built as drawn and recorded as a `GAPS.md` line like any other unflagged gap (§8.1 — the loop's file does not carry it yet).

## The bar (verbatim policy every verdict was judged against)

SIMPLE means ALL of: (1) at most one new field in ONE owner file — `people/<handle>.json` (guard row (b), the actor's own file) or `config.json` (via `ConfigStore`) — or no new field at all; (2) at most one new verb OR one new flag on an existing verb; (3) at most one guard change, and only of the shape "add an existing `GuardAction` to `PEOPLE_ACTIONS`" or "add one action string to `GuardAction` for a row-(b)-shaped self-write" — never a new `team.json` row (the §6.0 table is closed: rows c/d/e only); (4) tests that fit the existing vitest layout; (5) a one-paragraph spec delta to ONE section; (6) exactly one approver — Ryan (phase-1 spec, ledger, guard, schema) or Ajay (eval-engine spec, receipts, hygiene). ALSO SIMPLE: a purely app-side derivation over committed files that passes the D20 honesty test; a design-side string fix; an app rule. NOT SIMPLE: anything needing a collector, an event log, a machine registry, a change to WHAT the eval engine computes, a `team.json` schema change, a privacy call, a hook write path that commits on every session start, a new Prompter member threaded through every verb, or a mechanism that does not exist. MEDIUM = a bounded additive change that breaks exactly one SIMPLE condition (stated per row). HARD = a new subsystem or mechanism. CLOSED = already true at the pin (the closing line or commit is cited). DEFERRED = decided elsewhere (the decision is cited). D20 honesty test for the app side: a value presented as TEAM-WIDE truth may not be app-local; per-machine chrome is app-side by nature. Anything touching sync respects D-R4 (`gap-4.md` §12).

Of the two remaining flagged gaps, per-case eval tables (**EV-03**) is MEDIUM because the receipt is `.passthrough()` and the engine change is additive, and the machine registry splits into **MC-04** (CLOSED) and **MC-03** (SIMPLE · app) — see §0 item 3.

Two entries below. **Only call 1 is a question this document opens**; call 2 is the standing design-side call `BRIEF.md:28` and `:33` already leave with Teddy, restated here so the routing is visible in one place. 1. **Build the SIMPLE CLI set now via spec delta → `codex-spec` → `codex-implement` in a worktree → `hybrid-review`, or hand it to Ryan and Ajay as asks?** 62 rows recommend a CLI change (45 under Ryan's approval, 9 under Ajay's, 8 needing no approver or a mixed one; recounted at revision time). Building means one spec delta per owner file (§2 has the paste-ready paragraphs — for 54 of the 62 rows; the eight named in §0 item 1 have none), then the CLI repo's gates and a PR Ryan reviews — never a push to main, and **not while the loop runs**: `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:17` bars every other writer from `~/Projects/SSM/terum-skills`, and `git worktree add` writes into that checkout, so the worktree route starts after the loop. Sending as asks means §2.1's two bundles go to Ryan and Ajay as written; **neither ask has been sent** — no send is recorded in `BRIEF.md`, `TEAM-HANDOFF.md` or the teammate-handoff doc (`2026-09-07-desktop-app-teammate-handoff.md` §0 item 3, decision 1). ## 2. The SIMPLE set — CLI-side (62 rows), grouped by owner file

Every field below is verbatim from the reconciled row. `field` is the exact zod line; `verb_or_flag` the exact commander registration; `guard` the exact `src/lib/guard.ts` change; `tests` the vitest files and cases; `spec_delta` the one paragraph to paste.

### Owner file: `config.json` (3 rows)

#### CP-D1 — The team is drawn as a (display name, key) pair; the CLI has one string, and a joiner's is the repository name  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** SettingsTeams, SettingsLeave, OnboardingBoot, OnboardingTeam, OnboardingWelcome, OnboardingError
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** There is exactly ONE string. teamConfigSchema is `{ remote, handle }` with no display field (src/lib/schema.ts:72 @ b5c0507) and the map is keyed by the team name (:90, :96); teamClone(team) is `<root>/teams/<that same key>` (src/lib/config.ts:91-93 @ b5c0507). `team create` asks one question, 'Team name' (src/commands/team.ts:214 @ b5c0507), binds it as the config key (:285-290) and writes THE SAME STRING into team.json's `name` (:523; teamSchema.name is `z.string().min(1)`, src/lib/schema.ts:45 @ b5c0507) — so a create cannot produce the drawn pair; and teamNameSchema accepts uppercase (`/^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,99}$/`, src/lib/schema.ts:15 @ b5c0507), so "Terum" would key the clone at `teams/Terum` and the command would be `team leave Terum`. `team join` derives the local name as `existing?.[0] ?? args.as ?? remoteName(target.remote)` (src/commands/team.ts:313 @ b5c0507; remoteName takes the last path segment less .git, src/lib/remote.ts:252 @ b5c0507) — so joining github.com/terum/team-skills names the team `team-skills`. `team leave <name>` is an exact-key lookup with no fuzzy fallback (src/commands/leave.ts:21-25 @ b5c0507). CORRECTION TO THE COSTER: team.json's `name` is NOT unread — src/lib/readme.ts:71 @ b5c0507 renders it as the generated README's heading, `` `## ${inlineText(data.team.name)} skills` ``, fed from :139 and committed at :168; README.md is guard row f (src/lib/guard.ts:48 @ b5c0507). So team.json.name is already the team's published display name on GitHub. Also: configSchema ALREADY carries `display_name: z.string().min(1).optional()` at src/lib/schema.ts:94 @ b5c0507 — the PERSON's name, read at src/commands/setup.ts:161 @ b5c0507 for git config user.name.
- **Field:** `label: z.string().min(1).optional()` inside teamConfigSchema — i.e. src/lib/schema.ts:72 becomes `const teamConfigSchema = z.object({ remote: z.string().min(1), handle: handleSchema, label: z.string().min(1).optional() }).passthrough();`. It must NOT be called `display_name`: src/lib/schema.ts:94 @ b5c0507 already carries a person-scoped `display_name` in the same file.
- **Verb or flag:** `.option('--label <name>')` on BOTH `team create` (src/cli.ts:61-66 @ b5c0507) and `team join` (src/cli.ts:68-71 @ b5c0507) — two separate commander subcommands with their own option registrations, so two flags
- **Guard:** none — config.json is machine-local and never enters a staged diff, so src/lib/guard.ts (GuardAction :10, PEOPLE_ACTIONS :36, row b :49) is untouched
- **Tests:** src/lib/__tests__/config.test.ts: a team entry round-trips label and an absent label reads as undefined; src/commands/__tests__/team.test.ts: --label is stored on create and on join, and the clone still keys on the team name
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §5.4 (the config.json block and the handles paragraph, :263-275)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** §5.4 — a per-team entry may carry an optional `label`: a human name shown by a UI, never an identifier. The team NAME remains the config key, the clone directory name (`teams/<name>`), and the operand every verb takes; `label` is never resolved, matched, or written into the team repository, and its absence means the UI shows the key. It is machine-local like the rest of config.json and is not part of the §6.0 staged diff. It is not `display_name`, which in this file is the person's.
- **Why SIMPLE:** Breaks SIMPLE condition (2): `team create` and `team join` are separate commander subcommands with their own `.option()` registrations (src/cli.ts:61-66 and :68-71 @ b5c0507), so a stored label needs TWO new flags, not one. The coster's escape hatch — "or nothing at all if the value is only ever read" — does not exist: a field no verb writes is dead unless the app writes config.json itself, and an app writing it outside ConfigStore races the lockfile (src/lib/config.ts:70-79 @ b5c0507). A one-flag variant (join only) would satisfy the letter of condition (2) but leaves a creator's entry permanently unlabelled, which is incoherent for a field the UI reads on every team. AND IT SHOULD NOT BE BUILT AT ALL: team.json already carries `name`, written from the same string at create (src/commands/team.ts:523 @ b5c0507) and already published as the README's heading (src/lib/readme.ts:71 @ b5c0507), so a config-local label is a third spelling that nothing keeps in sync and that would disagree with the README the whole team reads.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The app renders the display name from team.json's `name` IN THE TEAM'S CLONE — committed team truth, already on disk, and the exact string the generated README publishes (src/lib/readme.ts:71 @ b5c0507) — and renders the CONFIG KEY everywhere a command, a path or an identifier appears (`team leave <key>`, `teams/<key>`, the boot row). Where the two differ (the joiner case) it shows the repository's own name for the team with the local key beside it, rather than pretending they are one value. When describeClone is not `ok` (src/lib/teamRepo.ts:445-451 @ b5c0507) it falls back to the key — a state the design does not draw.
- **Cites:** `src/lib/schema.ts:15 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:45 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:72 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:90 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:94 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/config.ts:70-79 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/config.ts:91-93 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/readme.ts:71 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/readme.ts:168 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/remote.ts:252 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/guard.ts:10 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/guard.ts:36 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/guard.ts:48 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:61-66 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:68-71 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:70 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:214 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:285-290 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:313 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:523 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/leave.ts:21-25 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/setup.ts:139 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/invite.ts:51 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:263 @ b5c0507`

#### MC-01 — Project -> checkout-path map on this machine  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** MarketplaceProject, MarketplaceProjectNotInstalled, MarketplaceProjectInstall, MarketplacePerson, SettingsMachine, SettingsTeams
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** team.json projects carry `remotes`, never a path (src/lib/schema.ts:48 @ b5c0507), and the spec says so verbatim: 'The committed record names the *project*, never a filesystem path... The machine-local `placements` map in 5.4 is where a placement's actual path lives' (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:189 @ b5c0507). Project context is resolved from the worktree the command stands in, everywhere: matchingProject -> currentRepoRoot -> `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (src/commands/install.ts:219-229 @ b5c0507, currentRepoRoot at :226) and matchingProjectRoot in sync (src/commands/sync.ts:349-357 @ b5c0507); spec:191 'the answer always comes from where the command is standing'. configSchema has no such map (src/lib/schema.ts:91-101 @ b5c0507). The cwd seam already exists on every verb that needs it (InstallArgs.cwd src/commands/install.ts:26; SyncArgs.cwd src/commands/sync.ts:26; LsArgs.cwd src/commands/ls.ts:17 @ b5c0507) and the runner consumes it verbatim (src/lib/runner.ts:18 @ b5c0507; install.ts:227 passes `cwd ? { cwd } : undefined`). The bin fills cwd for setup (src/cli.ts:57), ls and both sub-commands (src/cli.ts:94, :95, :96), publish (:112) and connect (:119) and NOT for install (:122), uninstall-skill (:123) or sync (:125) @ b5c0507 - those three ride the process cwd. `install project <p>` run outside a matching checkout THROWS: `if (scope.kind === 'project' && (!project // matchedProject !== scope.project)) throw new Error('Install project <p> from a checkout registered for that project; no matching project context was found.')` (src/commands/install.ts:88 @ b5c0507); sync skips a project placement whose checkout this run is not standing in (src/commands/sync.ts:164, :167 @ b5c0507). A --cwd flag is precedented twice at b5c0507 - `validate --cwd <team-checkout>` (src/cli.ts:114, b5f441f) and `receipt-check --cwd` (src/cli.ts:115, 4149287) - but both name a TEAM checkout, not a project worktree. The read side is narrower without cwd: localSkillRoots returns the global root alone when cwd is undefined (src/lib/local-skills.ts:29-32 @ b5c0507).
- **Field:** none recommended. The smallest field that would work is `projects: z.record(z.string(), z.object({ team: z.string(), path: z.string() }).passthrough()).optional(),` inserted after src/lib/schema.ts:100 @ b5c0507 - but see reason; it is not recommended.
- **Verb or flag:** none recommended. The smallest honest flag set is `.option('--cwd <path>', 'resolve project context from this directory')` added to install (src/cli.ts:122), uninstall-skill (src/cli.ts:123) and sync (src/cli.ts:125) @ b5c0507, forwarded into the existing args.cwd. That is three flags on three verbs, not one.
- **Guard:** none - machine-local; nothing reaches the team repo (src/lib/guard.ts:43-55 @ b5c0507 is not entered).
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts: '--cwd resolves the project from the named checkout, not the process cwd'; src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: '--cwd selects the project checkout for a project-scoped placement'; src/commands/__tests__/uninstall.test.ts: '--cwd resolves the project scope for uninstall-skill project <name>'
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 5.2 (:189-191) and section 6 `install` (:348)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** Project resolution stays by worktree (5.2). For an embedded caller that cannot chdir, `install`, `uninstall-skill` and `sync` accept `--cwd <path>`, which supplies the same directory the CLI would otherwise take from `process.cwd()`; the resolution procedure itself is unchanged - the named directory is walked to its nearest `.git`, its `origin` is normalized (5.1) and matched against `team.json projects[].remotes`, and a non-matching directory is no project context, exactly as today. No path is recorded anywhere: `config.json` gains no project map, because a recorded path is a second answer to a question the worktree already answers, and it goes stale the moment a checkout moves.
- **Why SIMPLE:** Bar condition (2) breaks: the flag is useless on fewer than three verbs, because the app drives install, uninstall-skill and sync and none of those three is given a cwd by the bin (src/cli.ts:122, :123, :125 @ b5c0507). Refuter A's correction is taken: spec:343 bans the LIBRARY from calling process.cwd() ('cwd passed only from the CLI (the library never calls process.cwd())'), it does not ban a --cwd flag, and two verbs already ship one (src/cli.ts:114, :115) - so MEDIUM rests on the arithmetic, not on a spec prohibition. The config-map variant is worse: it would make install prefer a recorded path over the worktree, rewriting the 5.2 contract in two files (src/commands/install.ts:85-88, src/commands/sync.ts:349-357 @ b5c0507) against spec:191. Neither is needed - the app can set the child process's cwd, which is exactly what the existing seam consumes (src/lib/runner.ts:18).
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — Four things, no CLI change. (a) DISPLAY: derive each project's checkout path app-side from config.placements - every key is an absolute placed path and every entry carries scope {kind:'project', project} (src/lib/schema.ts:100 @ b5c0507; spec:253-257), so the checkout root is the key with /.claude/skills/<name> stripped (spec:141-142; src/lib/placer/agent-paths.ts:9-13 @ b5c0507). A project with no placement has no path - exactly the not-installed branch already drawn (build.py:2840). (b) COUNT: bind build.py:2840's number to count(config.placements where scope.project === key), not to the team list, so a declined skill cannot make the sentence false. (c) ACTING: spawn every verb with the child cwd set to that checkout; the seam consumes it verbatim (src/lib/runner.ts:18). (d) FIRST INSTALL INTO AN UNSEEN CHECKOUT: there is no path to derive, so the app must ask - a native folder picker, an affordance drawn on none of the 98 boards - and spawn `install project <key>` there. D-R4 CONSTRAINT ON (c), verified not assumed: sync reconciles a project placement only when standing in that project's checkout (src/commands/sync.ts:164, :167 @ b5c0507) and records the skipped ones as NOT undone work ('A project placement outside its checkout is not undone work: that placement belongs to another session.', src/commands/sync.ts:285-286 @ b5c0507), yet it still stamps the team (src/commands/sync.ts:288-291, writeStamp :347 @ b5c0507). Because `sync --hook` short-circuits on a stamp under an hour old (src/commands/sync.ts:106; STAMP_FRESH_MS src/lib/hook.ts:149; stampIsFresh :165-168 @ b5c0507), an app sync run in project A's checkout silences for an hour the hook run in project B's checkout that would have placed B's skills. So the app rule is: N project checkouts need N sync spawns, all user-intent only (gap-4 D-R4), and the freshness chip must not read the stamp as evidence that project B was reconciled.
- **Cites:** `src/lib/schema.ts:48 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:91-101 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/install.ts:26 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/install.ts:85-88 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/install.ts:219-229 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:26 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:164 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:167 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:349-357 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:17 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:28 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/local-skills.ts:29-32 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/runner.ts:18 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:57 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:94-96 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:112 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:114 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:115 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:119 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:122 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:123 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:125 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:189 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:191 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:253-257 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:343 @ b5c0507`, `build.py:2840`, `build.py:2348-2352`, `build.py:2875`, `build.py:2928-2930`, `build.py:3370-3377`, `build.py:3416`, `build.py:3578`, `build.py:3586-3610`

#### MC-11 — config.json identity fields (name, email, default handle) are drawn as editable; only the login wizard writes them  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** Settings, SettingsLight, SettingsStates
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** THE FIELDS EXIST; NOTHING SETS ONE. src/lib/schema.ts:91-101 is `configSchema`: `default_handle: handleSchema.optional()` (:92), `email: emailSchema.optional()` (:93), `display_name: z.string().min(1).optional()` (:94), `github: z.string().optional()` (:95), closed with `.passthrough()` (:101). ConfigStore is the only writer — src/lib/config.ts:14 "Read-modify-write under a lock, written atomically. The only way to change the file" — implemented at :70-79 inside `underLock` (:51-65; lock :53-59, `assertHeld` :60-62 and :75), atomic write at :97-112. There is no `config` verb: src/cli.ts:49-130 registers none. The only writer of the identity block is `setIdentity` (src/lib/auth.ts:174-179), which assigns all four fields at once; its callers are the wizards — `login` (src/commands/login.ts:22-23, registered src/cli.ts:50-52 with the description "Check the GitHub CLI and record your identity (name, email, GitHub login, default handle); writes no team entry" at :51) and the team paths `authenticateCreator` (src/lib/auth.ts:145-155) and `identityForJoiner` (:165-170), both via `collectIdentity` (:66), which asks the whole block (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:265). So changing one field today means re-answering the identity block through a terminal wizard. THE DESIGN'S TWO CLAIMS BOTH CHECK OUT: "Handle on Terum" locked is right (spec :263 "immutable for that team once its people file exists", :265 "Only `team create` and `team join` bind"; single writer `bindTeam`, src/lib/auth.ts:186); and "rewrites that line at the next sync" is literally true — src/commands/connect.ts:178 builds `const author = `${config.display_name} <${config.email}>`` and the reconcile path recomputes it from a fresh config at :244, committed with `previousAuthor` at :289, :307, :357 and :359, which the guard explicitly admits (src/lib/guard.ts:18-19). NOTE the coster cited `src/commands/share.ts:135/:179/:247`: THAT FILE DOES NOT EXIST at b5c0507 — `share` was renamed to `connect` in 71e7a0a (hidden pointer at src/cli.ts:121; `SKILL_ACTIONS` is now `['connect','sync']`, src/lib/guard.ts:37). Also load-bearing and unnamed by the coster: src/commands/connect.ts:165 refuses to connect without `config.email` and `config.display_name`, so the verb's non-empty validation is not cosmetic. And the spec already intends the edit: .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:238 `"default_handle": "ajay", // the suggestion offered at each join, editable any time`.
- **Field:** none — the cheapest shape in the family. All three editable keys already exist in the zod object: src/lib/schema.ts:92 `default_handle: handleSchema.optional(),`, :93 `email: emailSchema.optional(),`, :94 `display_name: z.string().min(1).optional(),`. Nothing is added to configSchema.
- **Verb or flag:** ONE new flag on an existing verb — appended to the `login` registration at src/cli.ts:50-52: ```ts .option('--set <key=value>', 'set one identity field without re-answering the block: name, email or default-handle (repeatable)', (value: string, previous: string[] = []) => [...previous, value]) ``` With `--set` present, `login` skips `detectOrOfferGh` and `collectIdentity` entirely: it parses each `key=value`, allowlists exactly `name` → `display_name`, `email`, `default-handle` → `default_handle`, validates each with the schema configSchema already uses (`z.string().min(1)`, `emailSchema`, `handleSchema` — src/lib/schema.ts:92-94), then performs one `store.update((c) => { … })` (src/lib/config.ts:70-79) and prints the new values. It refuses `github` (evidence collected from `gh api user` inside collectIdentity, src/lib/auth.ts:68-72 — not a preference) and every other key, so `.passthrough()` (src/lib/schema.ts:101) cannot become a write-anything hole; it cannot reach `teams.<team>.handle`, bound only by `bindTeam` (src/lib/auth.ts:186) and immutable per spec :263. WHY THIS SHAPE AND NOT THE COSTER'S: a bare `config` reader plus `config set <key> <value>` is TWO commander registrations, which breaks condition (2); the flag reuses `login`'s existing bullet and its existing store handle. The reader half the coster wanted is not needed either: config.json is machine-local and never committed (spec §4.2, header :102), so the adapter reads it directly — only the WRITE has to go through the CLI.
- **Guard:** none. src/lib/guard.ts:6 scopes the guard to team-repo diffs and `guard()` at :43-55 walks `tree.changedPaths`; config.json is never in a tree. No PEOPLE_ACTIONS change (:36), no GuardAction addition (:10), no team.json row. The downstream author-line rewrite is ALREADY guarded — action `sync`, SKILL_ACTIONS at :37, row (a) at :52, `previousAuthor` at :18-19 — so no new guard surface appears.
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/login.test.ts: '--set display_name writes only that key and leaves every other config field byte-identical'; src/commands/__tests__/login.test.ts: '--set refuses an invalid email and an out-of-syntax default handle, and writes nothing'; src/commands/__tests__/login.test.ts: '--set refuses github and every unknown key, so passthrough never becomes a write-anything hole'; src/commands/__tests__/login.test.ts: '--set runs no gh detection, asks no question, and never touches teams.<team>.handle'; src/lib/__tests__/config.test.ts: 'a --set racing a sync serializes under the lock so neither write is lost' (extends the existing 'serializes concurrent updates so neither is lost' at src/lib/__tests__/config.test.ts:61); src/commands/__tests__/connect.test.ts: 'a changed display_name reaches metadata.author at the next sync' (the coster named src/commands/__tests__/share.test.ts, which does not exist at b5c0507 — 71e7a0a)
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6, the `login` bullet at :332 — one section. §5.4 needs no change: :238 already calls default_handle "editable any time".
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** `login --set <key>=<value>` is the field-level write §5.4 has always implied by calling `default_handle` "editable any time". It is the one form of `login` that asks nothing: no gh detection, no identity collection. It accepts exactly three keys — `name` (`display_name`), `email` and `default-handle` — validates each with the schema `configSchema` already uses (non-empty string, `emailSchema`, `handleSchema`), writes through `ConfigStore.update`, which remains the only writer of the file, and prints the new value; repeating the flag sets several keys under one lock. It refuses every other key, including `github`: that value is evidence collected from `gh api user` during identity, not a preference, and the schema's `passthrough` must not become a way to write arbitrary keys. It cannot set `teams.<team>.handle`, which only `team create` and `team join` bind and which is immutable once your people file exists. Changing `name` or `email` changes `metadata.author` on every skill you author at the next `sync`, through the §5.3 managed-author refresh that guard row (a) already admits — and the command prints that consequence before it writes, including the half it does not fix: your committed `people/<handle>.json` keeps the `display_name` and `email` recorded when you joined (only `team create`/`team join` write them), so `ls member <handle>` matches your authored skills against the old pair until a people-file refresh ships. `login` with no `--set` is unchanged.
- **Why SIMPLE:** (1) NO new field at all — src/lib/schema.ts:92-94 already carries all three; (2) exactly one new flag on an existing verb; (3) no guard change; (4) every test lands in a file that already exists (src/commands/__tests__/login.test.ts, src/lib/__tests__/config.test.ts, src/commands/__tests__/connect.test.ts); (5) one paragraph to one section, §6's `login` bullet at :332; (6) one approver, Ryan. The write path already exists and is already correct: ConfigStore.update (src/lib/config.ts:70-79) takes proper-lockfile before reading (:53-59), aborts if the lock was compromised (:60-62, :75), and writes temp+fsync+rename at 0600 (:97-112). No D-R4 interaction: nothing here writes run/ or <team>.stamp. THE CAVEAT REFUTER A IS RIGHT TO RAISE, STATED RATHER THAN BURIED: `display_name` and `email` are REQUIRED fields of the committed people file (spec §5.2 :174-175; src/lib/schema.ts:56-57), written only by `joinMutation` (src/commands/team.ts:407-411), and `ls member` computes "Authored" by matching `metadata.author` against that file's pair (src/commands/ls.ts:68); `sync`'s people writes touch only `installed`/`declined` (src/commands/sync.ts:320-341). So a changed name makes a member's own skills stop being attributed to them on every teammate's machine. It does NOT break a SIMPLE condition here, because the divergence is ALREADY reachable at b5c0507 by re-running `login`: src/commands/login.ts:23 calls `setIdentity` (src/lib/auth.ts:174-179) with no people refresh anywhere. This row adds an entry point to a pre-existing gap; closing the gap (refreshing people/<handle>.json in every configured team at the next sync) is a SEPARATE MEDIUM — a committed write to a second owner file, needing §5.2/§5.3 as well as §6. Distinct from PF-07, which is that committed people file under guard row (b).
- **App side:** FORBIDDEN (D20 ok) — The gap-closing app-alone option is: the app writes ~/.terum/skills/config.json itself when a field loses focus. That is FORBIDDEN. The app's actual job here is read + spawn: read config.json in the adapter to fill the three drawn fields, and spawn `login --set <key>=<value>` to write.

It is written only by its owner, through the `profile` verb under guard row (b), and it is **not an authorization**: repo admin remains the only authority predicate (SS6.0 rows c-e, D11), no verb reads `role` to decide anything, and the roster's Admin/Member chip is a separate, host-derived value. The two chips are already different components (build.py:2656 vs :3011-3020), so there is no code path in which one could be mistaken for the other.

The verb READS team.json inside the mutate to validate the project name, which is not I/O and has an exact precedent: `uninstall` parses team.json from `tree.before('team.json')` inside its own safeWrite mutate (src/commands/uninstall.ts:97-98), so SS6.0's "`mutate` performing any I/O of its own is a bug" (phase-1-build.md:304) is untouched.

- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** "`projects` is an array of `team.json` project names, defaulted to `[]`, recording which team projects this person considers themselves on. It is self-declared and written only by its owner, through `profile --project` under guard row (b); the verb validates each name against the `team.json` project registry, reading it from the pre-image tree inside the mutation, so the roster can never show a chip for a project that does not exist. It is deliberately **not** authorization and **not** placement: which skills land where is still decided by `team.json projects[].skills` and by worktree resolution (SS5.2 project resolution), and a name that later disappears from the registry is ignored by readers rather than pruned. Project member counts and facepiles are derived across all `people/*.json`, never stored, so `team.json` gains no members array and the SS6.0 table is unchanged."
- **Why SIMPLE:** One field in the actor's own file, one flag on one verb, one guard change of the permitted shape, tests in the existing layout, one SS5.2 paragraph, one approver. Critically it avoids the trap the row is shaped to walk into: the obvious home for "members" is team.json.projects[], which the bar names NOT SIMPLE and which src/lib/guard.ts:150-156 would refuse at runtime anyway. Putting it in the people file keeps the write inside row (b) and transcribes the design's own model (build.py:2365-2370), where the card count is derived FROM the per-person record (build.py:2482-2485).
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — the app derives, per project P, the set of people whose `installed[]` holds an entry with scope.kind === 'project' && scope.project === P (src/lib/schema.ts:26-29, :36-41), renders that set as the facepile and its size as the count - and the design relabels the number to say what it is.

#### PF-07 — Edit own profile after join (the committed display_name, and bio)  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** Settings, SettingsLight, Share, MarketplacePerson, MarketplacePeople
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** The propagation half-works and the half that does not is the defect. `login` collects identity and writes it to config.json only (src/commands/login.ts:22-23 -> `setIdentity`, src/lib/auth.ts:174-179). `connect`/`sync` refresh `metadata.author` from fresh config under `{ action: 'sync' }` (src/commands/connect.ts:289, :307, :357, :359), so the design's note at build.py:3558 is TRUE for the author line. But `display_name` reaches people/<handle>.json only through join: `joinMutation` sets `display_name: identity.displayName` unconditionally at src/commands/team.ts:410 (and the scaffold at :524), and NO verb whose purpose is editing a profile exists. `bio` is worse - `bio: existing?.bio ?? ''` at src/commands/team.ts:414 preserves whatever is there and nothing ever sets it, while `personSchema` requires the key (src/lib/schema.ts:61). So after join, change your name in the Account pane and every teammate's roster row (src/commands/ls.ts:34, build.py:3053) and the generated README roster line (src/lib/readme.ts:73) keep the old one until you happen to re-join.
- **Field:** none new for this row - `display_name` (src/lib/schema.ts:56) and `bio` (:61) already exist. (Bundling PF-03 adds `role`; bundling PF-04 adds `projects`; those are those rows' fields.)
- **Verb or flag:** RECOMMENDED: one verb in the M2 block (src/cli.ts:118-130): `program.command('profile').description('Update your team profile in people/<you>.json (name, bio, role, projects)').option('--name <name>').option('--bio <bio>').option('--role <role>').option('--project <name>').option('--team <team>').action(async (options: { name?: string; bio?: string; role?: string; project?: string; team?: string }) => execute((io) => active.profile(options, io), { verb: 'profile', notices: true }));` - with no flags it prompts through the Prompter like every other verb, so the app calls the same function (library-first, phase-1-build.md:59). CHEAPER ALTERNATIVE, costed honestly below: no verb at all - have `sync` refresh people/<you>.json.display_name from config.display_name when the two differ, reusing the managed-fields idiom at src/commands/connect.ts:289.
- **Guard:** RECOMMENDED path: src/lib/guard.ts:10 gains `/ 'profile'`; :36 becomes ['join','install','uninstall','sync','profile']. One change of the permitted shape, SHARED with PF-03 and PF-04. CHEAPER path: none at all - row (b) already admits `sync` (src/lib/guard.ts:36, :49; spec table row b at phase-1-build.md:320 lists it; src/lib/__tests__/guard.test.ts:27 exercises it) and that permission is DORMANT - `grep -rn "action: 'sync'" src/` at b5c0507 hits only src/commands/connect.ts:289/:307/:357/:359, all SKILL.md.
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/profile.test.ts: profile --name rewrites display_name in the actor's own people file and leaves installed, declined, github and email untouched; src/commands/__tests__/profile.test.ts: profile --bio sets and clears the bio; src/commands/__tests__/profile.test.ts: profile refuses --email, --github and --handle with the reason; src/commands/__tests__/profile.test.ts: profile also refreshes config.display_name so the author line and the roster agree; src/commands/__tests__/profile.test.ts: profile on a team where this machine has no binding fails before any write; src/lib/__tests__/guard.test.ts: the profile action may write only the actor's own people file, never team.json or a skill folder; src/lib/__tests__/readme.test.ts: the generated roster line reflects a renamed member; src/commands/__tests__/join.test.ts: a later join still overwrites display_name from config identity, so a people-file-only rename does not survive a reclaim (the regression the config refresh prevents)
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md SS5.2 (:169-193)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** "`display_name` and `bio` are set at join and, from this revision, editable afterwards by their owner through one verb: `profile [--name] [--bio] [--role] [--project]` writes `people/<you>.json` via `safeWrite` under guard row (b), which gains the `profile` action. It writes **only** self-describing fields: `email`, `github` and `handle` are refused, because `email` and `github` are the identity evidence the SS5.4 reclaim rule matches a second machine against and `handle` is the binding identity itself, immutable once the people file exists - the Account pane's locked handle row already draws that boundary. `profile --name` also refreshes `config.display_name`, because `team join` rewrites the committed `display_name` from config identity on every join and reclaim: without the refresh, a people-file-only rename is silently reverted the next time its owner joins from a second laptop. This closes the defect that a name changed after join reaches every SKILL.md at the next sync but never reaches the roster or the generated README."
- **Why SIMPLE:** Zero new fields, one new verb, one guard change of the permitted shape, tests in the existing layout, one SS5.2 paragraph, one approver. It is the cheapest row in the batch and the only one that fixes an existing inconsistency rather than adding a feature: the design's Account note (build.py:3558) over-promises against src/commands/team.ts:410. The cheaper zero-cost alternative is real but carries a hazard the verb does not: a sync that rewrites the people file from config.display_name whenever they differ will PING-PONG forever between two laptops whose configs disagree (config.json is machine-local, src/lib/schema.ts:94, and nothing reconciles two machines), committing a rename at every sync on both. It must at minimum be scoped to the interactive path using the existing `if (!io)` deferral idiom (src/commands/sync.ts:316). When PF-03 and PF-04 are also taken - and they both need a writer - one `profile` verb amortizes across three rows and has no such failure mode, so it is the better engineering even though it is not the cheaper row.
- **App side:** FORBIDDEN (D20 no) — the app stores an edited display name (and bio) in an app-owned file.

The READERS exist: `src/lib/skills.ts:58 @ b5c0507` (`endorsedCandidates` filters `!person.declined.includes(id)`), `src/commands/sync.ts:171 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:314 @ b5c0507`; `src/commands/install.ts:135 @ b5c0507` clears the entry on explicit install, closing the round trip. - **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md — §6.0 only (the section spans :299–:372 @ b5c0507; §6.1 starts :373): one new verb bullet inserted directly after the `uninstall-skill` bullet at :351, plus row (b)'s “Permitted on” cell in the permitted-writes table at :320. §5.2 (:183, :193) needs NO edit — the opt-out contract is unchanged; this adds a second writer of the list it already defines.
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** **`decline <ref>`** — records `<ref>`'s skill ID in your own `people/<you>.json` `declined` list and does nothing else: no placement is touched, no `pending` entry is written, and no local file moves, because a decline suppresses a *future* automatic placement rather than undoing a past one. It exists because `uninstall-skill <ref>` resolves its targets from your `installed` records and the machine-local `placements` ledger, so on a team-endorsed skill you were offered but never installed it resolves zero targets, returns before its write, and is a silent exit-0 no-op — leaving the one case the §5.2 opt-out contract is *for* with no verb. It records under the same predicate the `uninstall-skill` bullet above already uses: only a skill an automatic path would otherwise place (team-endorsed global *or* project-list) may be declined, so one field keeps one recording rule; a ref nothing endorses is refused in one line rather than committing a permanent, team-readable refusal of a skill nobody offered. `declined` is scope-free (§5.2), so `decline` takes a skill ref only, never `member` or `project`. It is idempotent (an ID already in the list produces no tree change and therefore no commit) and reversible only the documented way — an explicit `install` of that ID clears the entry (§5.2). Authorization is unchanged: the commit is a `people/<you>.json`-only change and the durable guard action remains `uninstall`, the same action `sync` already uses when its orphan pass records a decline; row (b) of the table above gains `decline` in its “Permitted on” cell (`/ b / `people/<you>.json` / `join`, `install`, `uninstall-skill`, `decline`, `sync` /`), and no other row, no `team.json` write, and no stamp are involved.
- **Why SIMPLE:** Every SIMPLE condition is met, two of them without spending the allowance. (1) ZERO new fields: `declined` already exists at `src/lib/schema.ts:63 @ b5c0507` and `.passthrough()` (`:64`) is not even needed. (2) Exactly one new verb (or, on Ryan's alternative, one new flag on `uninstall-skill`) — never both. (3) Guard: zero code changes on the recommended path, since `src/commands/sync.ts:339 @ b5c0507` already commits a decline-only people-file change under the existing `uninstall` action admitted by row (b) at `src/lib/guard.ts:49 @ b5c0507`; the honest-label alternative is one action string added to `GuardAction`/`PEOPLE_ACTIONS`, which the bar names as an allowed single guard change. (4) Tests land as one new file in the existing `src/commands/__tests__` layout plus one regression pin in `uninstall.test.ts`; `src/lib/__tests__/guard.test.ts:61-62 @ b5c0507` already asserts row (b) for the four actions and needs no edit on the recommended path. (5) One paragraph plus one table cell, both inside §6.0 — the section spans `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:299 @ b5c0507` ('### 6.0 Every write goes through `safeWrite()`') to `:372`, with §6.1 starting at `:373`, and the table (`:320`) and the verb bullets (`:341` connect, `:351` uninstall-skill, `:352` uninstall) are both inside it. (6) One approver, Ryan. Nothing is hidden underneath: no `team.json` write (the mutation reads `tree.before('team.json')` only, the same way `src/commands/uninstall.ts:97-99 @ b5c0507` does, and §6.0 rows c/d/e are untouched); no new privacy call (the opt-out call is already made and ratified three times — `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:193 @ b5c0507`, `:515`, and D14 at `.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:122 @ b5c0507`, which positively REQUIRES the recording the design draws — so this adds a second writer, not a new disclosure); no eval-engine change and no §12 UI-contract rule (no receipt is read or written); no hook write path (`.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:365 @ b5c0507` already spells out that `--hook` writes nothing to `declined` and skips a declined ID silently — the verb makes the hook quieter through an EXISTING reader); no collector, no event log, no machine registry; no Prompter change (`io` is already supplied by `execute`, and the verb needs it for one output line); and NO D-R4 interaction — `writeStamp` is a module-private function at `src/commands/sync.ts:347 @ b5c0507` whose only call site is `src/commands/sync.ts:291 @ b5c0507`, verified by grep, so a `decline` run never writes `run/<team>.stamp` and never suppresses the SessionStart hook's sync.
- **App side:** FORBIDDEN (D20 no) — The app would keep a local 'dismissed inbox items' set keyed by skill id, hide the Inbox share row on Decline, and never write the team repo.

#### CP-14 — 'Edit' opens the authored source or is hidden; project-page Edit is ambiguous  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** SkillDetail, SkillDetailLight, SkillDetailRemove, SkillDetailStates, MarketplaceProject
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** The placed copy is not an authoring surface, by decision: "nothing is ever written inside a placed folder. A hand-edited placed copy (local-changed) is overwritten on sync and moved to quarantine, with one printed line — placed copies are generated output, not an authoring surface" (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:545, §13 default 33), mechanism at :371. The one authoring surface is the connected source: config.shared is z.record(z.string(), z.object({ source: z.string(), team: z.string(), baseline: z.string().optional() }).passthrough()) at src/lib/schema.ts:97, written by connect (fresh.shared[id!] = { source, team, baseline }, src/commands/connect.ts:216), and thereafter "sync diffs each shared source against the repo copy and auto-commits `ajay: update single-fix` — you never re-connect" (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:341). config.shared lives in machine-local config.json, so it holds only what THIS machine connected. The project page's Edit splits in two. Project METADATA (key, remotes, description) has no path at all: remotes appears in src/ only in the schema (src/lib/schema.ts:48), publish refuses an unknown project (src/commands/publish.ts:171), guard row c permits global and projects[].skills only (src/lib/guard.ts:174-183, dispatched at :153, guardTeam throwing at :156), and the raw-push guard permits the same three shapes and refuses at :90 (teamChangeOpenToRawPush, :101-107) — so the only route is a hand edit plus git push --no-verify. The project's SKILL LIST, by contrast, is reachable today with an existing verb and no guard change: publish <ref> --project <project> is registered at src/cli.ts:108-112 and endorse() pushes the id into fresh.projects[<key>].skills (src/commands/publish.ts:166-176, the push at :175) — exactly what row c permits. The complement is a gap, not a wall: no verb removes an id from a project list, though the guard would permit it.
- **Field:** none
- **Verb or flag:** For the rejected project-METADATA route only: something like program.command('team project <name>').option('--remote <url>').option('--rename <new>') in src/cli.ts beside the existing team sub-commands (src/cli.ts:59-86). Not recommended. The project SKILL-LIST route needs no new verb at all — publish --project already exists at src/cli.ts:108-112.
- **Guard:** For the rejected route: a NEW row in the §6.0 permitted-writes table (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:317-326) plus a matching branch in guardTeam (src/lib/guard.ts:150-156), a new predicate beside onlySkillListsChanged / archivedAppendedOnly / archivedRemovedOnly (src/lib/guard.ts:174-193), and a parallel change in teamChangeOpenToRawPush (src/lib/guard.ts:101-107) or every such push is refused by the pre-push hook.
- **Tests:** src/lib/__tests__/guard.test.ts: a project rename is permitted for team-project and refused for every other action; src/commands/__tests__/team.test.ts: team project --rename rewrites the key and leaves the skill lists byte-identical
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6.0 (the permitted-writes table at :317-326) and §6 team
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** not written — the bar states the §6.0 table is closed (rows c/d/e only), so drafting a paragraph that opens it would misstate the cost. Project administration is a spec amendment with its own decision, not a delta.
- **Why SIMPLE:** Read as project metadata, the project-page Edit breaks bar condition (3) outright: it needs a new team.json row and the §6.0 table is closed. Forcing lines: src/lib/guard.ts:174-183 (row c is skill-lists-only — onlySkillListsChanged compares projects with skills stripped, so a new or renamed project key fails) and src/lib/guard.ts:150-156 (guardTeam throws for anything else). The skill half of the row needs no CLI change: the app reads config.shared[<id>].source itself, and "Open in editor" (build.py:1198) already covers the read-only case.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — One app rule with one disclosure. Edit is shown only when config.shared[<skill id>] exists on this machine, and it opens that entry's source path; otherwise it is hidden — not disabled, because a disabled control still advertises that editing a generated folder might be allowed. The read-only case stays covered by the existing "Open in editor" (build.py:1198). The disclosure: an edit to a connected source is auto-committed to the team repository by the next sync with no further confirmation (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:341), so the surface must say that — otherwise CP-13's disclosure standard is applied to removal and not to authoring. One consequence to specify with it: the rail's provenance line reads source = f'~/.claude/skills/{s["name"]}' if installed else the clone (build.py:1197) and hangs the opener off it (:1198); for a connected skill the honest "read from" is the connected source.
- **Cites:** `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:545 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:371 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:341 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:317-326 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:97 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:48 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/connect.ts:216 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/guard.ts:150-156 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/guard.ts:174-183 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/guard.ts:101-107 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/guard.ts:90 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/publish.ts:171 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/publish.ts:166-176 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:108-112 @ b5c0507`

### Owner file: `none (a Result field, an export, a flag, a verb payload, or a library read)` (53 rows)

*(Grouping correction applied by hand, 2026-09-08: **CP-24** was printed under `people/<handle>.json` by the generator and is moved here, where its own reconciled `owner_file` puts it — `none (documentation; README.md is not people/<handle>.json, config.json or team.json)`, which is also what its **Field:** line and its §2.1 bundle line say. Cause: `owner_bucket()` in `costing/assemble_doc.py` tests `if 'people' in o` before `o.startswith('none')`, so the substring inside that disclaimer captured the row. The fix in the generator is to run the `none` test first, or to match on the leading token rather than a substring. Consequence corrected: the people-file group is 4 rows, this group is 53, and nobody assembling "one spec delta per owner file" folds a product-README documentation ask into the people-schema ask to Ryan. The 62-row total and every count in §0, §2 and §8.1 are unaffected.)*

#### AC-06 — Window chrome: the decided three-mode header has no written Tauri config or platform specimens  *(recommended: both; approver: none)*

- **ANSWERED BY `BRIEF.md:15` (FINAL, never re-ask):** "a three-mode header component (macOS OS-drawn lights with a reserved gutter, Windows/Linux drawn controls, browser/mock the cosmetic drawn lights). The macOS config, when src-tauri exists later, is titleBarStyle Overlay + decorations true + trafficLightPosition. src-tauri is NOT created tonight." The D-W2 paragraph below is the **written-down form of that decision**, not a fork awaiting a choice. The remaining work is verification, and `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:49-51` already assigns it to Mac teammates (prove the window with the probe; walk C1-C17). One reconciliation for whoever runs that walk: the geometry in the spec delta below (12px dots, left edges at x 16/36/56, the mark at x 76) and `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:51`'s "centres at x 22 / 42 / 62" with a 16px left inset are **the same drawing** — left edge + 6px radius = centre.
- **Boards:** every board (top bar), OnboardingBoot, SettingsAppearance, SettingsMachine
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** Nothing - a tauri.conf decision, verified against the Tauri source of truth rather than assumed. traffic_light_position is Option<LogicalPosition>, default None, documented 'The position of the window controls on macOS. Requires titleBarStyle: Overlay and decorations: true.' (tauri-feasibility.md:608, restated as the easily-missed rule at :617, risk R13 at :886). So macOS is a DECORATED window with an overlaid bar and repositioned lights, while Windows and Linux are the opposite - decorations: false plus hand-drawn controls (tauri-feasibility.md:617, :639). startResizeDragging(), the manual edge-drag hatch for an undecorated window, is confirmed present on the Tauri v2 JS Window class (https://v2.tauri.app/reference/javascript/api/namespacewindow/, fetched 2026-09-07). No CLI file, verb, schema, guard row or receipt is implied.
- **Field:** none
- **Verb or flag:** none
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** none named
- **Spec section:** No CLI spec section. NOT a CLI spec change - the paragraph below goes in the frontend spec's window/chrome section and is gap-8.md:270-290 (D-W2) adopted verbatim.
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** NOT a CLI spec change - paste into the frontend spec (window chrome): "D-W2. The top bar is one component with three platform modes (gap-8.md:274, T-B recommendation (c); CONFIRMED by `BRIEF.md:15`, which is FINAL and never re-asked). macOS: decorations: true, titleBarStyle 'Overlay', hiddenTitle: true, and trafficLightPosition { x: 16, y: 14 } - coordinates, not a reserve, because traffic_light_position is a LOGICAL position, so the OS lights land on exactly the pixels the design drew (12px dots at x 16/36/56 in #ff5f57 / #febc2e / #28c840, build.py:224) and the macOS build matches the mock instead of approximating it; the design's own dots are not rendered, and the mark stays at x 76, i.e. a 76px reserve, not the ~78px of tauri-feasibility.md:639. Windows/Linux: decorations: false; no lights; the Terum mark moves to x 16 with back/forward at 48 and 80; the app draws minimise/maximise/close top-right and RESERVES 138px (3 x 46, the Windows convention), which means the bell (build.py:233) shifts left and the right block grows 240 -> ~378 (gap-8.md:276) - both end blocks are re-laid, not one inset moved. Browser/mock keeps the drawn cosmetic dots; this is what the fidelity baselines contain. Dragging is data-tauri-drag-region on the bar's empty regions only (it does not inherit to children, tauri-feasibility.md:974) or startDragging() on mousedown, with double-click-to-maximise hand-built. With decorations: false the WM gives NO resize border, so 4-6px edge and corner hit targets calling startResizeDragging() are a BUILD ITEM, not a test note (gap-8.md:278; tauri #8519 closed, #9053; 2.11.4 improved undecorated resize per tauri-feasibility.md:50) - test on a real Windows host. SHORTCUTS (build.py:3403) needs a Ctrl rendering. Gate carve-out, required: the fidelity gate runs the browser mode, so it validates the DRAWN chrome and can never see either Tauri mode - both platform headers ship untested by the gate (gap-8.md:288-290). Either mask the top-bar left block in the gate and test chrome separately, or state that the gate's coverage stops at the panel edge."
- **Why SIMPLE:** Window decoration is a Tauri config plus a header component. No CLI field, verb, guard row or receipt is implied. DECIDED, not a fork: `BRIEF.md:15` fixes the three-mode header (macOS OS-drawn lights with a reserved gutter, Windows/Linux drawn controls, browser/mock the cosmetic drawn lights) and the macOS config (titleBarStyle Overlay + decorations true + trafficLightPosition), with src-tauri deliberately not created by the loop. What is left is verification, and it is already assigned: the C1-C17 chrome walk and the probe window, `TEAM-HANDOFF.md:49-51`.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — Adopt gap-8's D-W2 table verbatim as one paragraph plus one platform-branched tauri.conf block, and build the top bar as a three-mode component. The decision is the deliverable; the component and the edge hit targets are ordinary frontend work the decision makes cheap. Tests (app repo): chrome.test.ts: the macOS header reserves 76px at the left and draws no dots; chrome.test.ts: the Windows header reserves 138px at the right and the bell sits left of the caption buttons; chrome.test.ts: the drag region is on the bar's empty regions and not on its buttons; chrome.test.ts: edge and corner hit targets call startResizeDragging; gate.test.ts: the fidelity gate's top-bar carve-out is asserted, not assumed.
- **Cites:** `tauri-feasibility.md:608`, `tauri-feasibility.md:617`, `tauri-feasibility.md:639`, `tauri-feasibility.md:886`, `tauri-feasibility.md:974`, `tauri-feasibility.md:50`

(1) Phase 2 may open a browser where phase 1 would not: :384 rules on the CLI's setup step 6, and the same spec anticipates the successor at :386 ('This is the line phase 2 replaces with opening the local UI', restated as default 41 at :556) with ui gated out at :24 - so the app opening COMMUNITY from Settings > About (build.py:3781) is a deliberate, scoped departure, not a contradiction. (2) The opener path scope separates CLI-owned local state from a placed copy: revealing ~/.terum/skills (build.py:3756) and the eval run trees (build.py:3710) is safe, but 'Open in editor' on a placed skill (build.py:1198, :2137-2138) opens generated output, and a hand-edited placed copy is overwritten and quarantined at sync (:371) - so the warning the design already writes on Settings > Sync (build.py:3650) must also sit next to that button. - **Spec delta (paste-ready):** The `eval` verb's one-line help must not claim the command never writes the team repository. Without `--commit` a run writes only its local run tree; with `--commit` it lands exactly one new receipt file through `safeWrite` under guard action `eval`, after redaction, and it does so whenever the run reaches the end - including a run whose `execution_status` is `partial`. A receipt is not written when hygiene reports an error finding, when the built receipt fails validation, or for a `--working` run. Every surface that quotes the verb - CLI help, the desktop Run-eval dialog, the onboarding Basics hint - states the same two-clause fact and the same exceptions.
- **Why SIMPLE:** Zero new fields, zero new verbs or flags, zero guard changes, tests in the existing src/__tests__ and src/commands/__tests__ layout, one paragraph to one spec section, one approver. It is a help string that contradicts the flag registered beside it, and the design copy is the correct statement of the two. Approver is Ajay alone because eval is out of phase-1 scope (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:24 @ b5c0507 lists eval under 'Out (gated/deferred)'), so the eval-engine spec is the single owner.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The Commit-receipts toggle is an app-side default in the same class as the k / model / judge selects two rows above it (build.py:3700-3705): the app remembers whether to pass --commit on the next run and passes it. Nothing is stored in config.json and nothing is presented as team-wide truth.

#### CP-11 — Filter facets with no predicate; AND/OR within a group; badge vs footer count  *(recommended: both; approver: Ajay)*

- **Boards:** MarketplaceFilters, MarketplaceStates, Marketplace
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** Three of the five inert facets already have exact CLI predicates the app must match rather than reinvent. src/cli.ts:126 registers search <term> with --category, --author, --project; src/commands/search.ts:38-44 implements them: category.toLowerCase().includes(args.category.toLowerCase()) (case-insensitive substring over metadata['terum-category'], :43), author.toLowerCase().includes(args.author.toLowerCase()) over metadata.author, which is the full "Name <email>" string (:44; src/lib/schema.ts:111), and project as Object.hasOwn(teamJson.projects, args.project) && teamJson.projects[args.project]!.skills.includes(skill.id) — exact, case-sensitive key lookup plus list membership (:41). The three flags AND together and each takes one value: there is no OR-within-a-group anywhere in the CLI. "Hide skills I have installed" has no flag but its data is committed (person.installed, src/lib/schema.ts:62). "Updated" has NO producer at all: I re-ran the grep at the pin — %cI, %ci, committerdate, 'log' and --format= appear nowhere in src/ outside __tests__ — and the only git read of a skill is latestTree, which returns a tree hash (src/lib/readme.ts:119-122). The only committed date is installed[].since (src/lib/schema.ts:40), a per-person install date, not a skill's last change. search is read-only by construction (src/commands/search.ts:21) and already batches its per-skill git reads eight at a time (src/commands/search.ts:50-51).
- **Field:** none
- **Verb or flag:** none. (A CLI route would need repeatable --category/--author on search for OR-within-a-group plus a new --updated-since; one new flag is inside the bar, three is not, and none of it is needed because the UI reads the clone under D21.)
- **Guard:** none — every read is read-only over the clone; search is documented as "Read-only clone search: git reads only ... no prompts, no placement, no safeWrite" (src/commands/search.ts:21).
- **Tests:** none named
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md §12 "UI contract (Teddy)", appended after the hard-rules paragraph at :495-498
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** A viewer-set filter over a receipt field is permitted and is not a derived statistic. The Verdict facet admits or excludes a skill by its latest receipt's `verdict` for the version on display (§12:481-482); the Net-lift facet applies a floor to `comparisons.*.net_lift` of that same receipt. Neither reorders a list and neither computes a new number: the counts a facet shows are cardinalities of the filtered set, never an average, a rate, or a score. Two constraints follow from §5.4 and are binding on any filtering surface. First, comparability: a lift threshold may only be applied within one (`provenance.model`, `provenance.cc_version`) pair, so the surface either scopes itself to one pair or shows provenance beside the facet and excludes receipts from another pair rather than silently comparing them. Second, no ranking: a filtered list is still ordered by install count (D38), never by the facet's own number. Facets over non-receipt data — install counts, context size, list, category, author, last-updated — are unconstrained by this section.
- **Why SIMPLE:** No code, no schema, no guard, one section, one approver — SIMPLE on every bar condition. It is nonetheless required: the coster's "spec_section: none, approver: none" is wrong, because two of the nine facets read receipt numbers and §12 opens with "The receipt JSON is the API. The UI never runs evals and never derives new statistics" (.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:480), scopes rendering to the latest receipt per version (:481-482), and §5.4 forbids merging or comparing numbers across model/cc_version by any surface (:253-254). A catalog-wide lift threshold is exactly such a surface.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — Eight new predicates, not nine — "Not evaluated" already has one (build.py:2432-2433). Specified once in the desktop spec's filter section: Verdict — the latest receipt's verdict for the displayed version (OR within the group; all-unchecked means no constraint); Net lift ≥ — that receipt's comparisons net lift, within one model/cc_version pair; Context ≤ — the skill's size; Installed by ≥ — installCounts over people/*.json (src/lib/readme.ts:40, D38); Updated — the one facet with no CLI producer (see design_consequence); List — membership in team.json global or projects[<key>].skills, OR within the group, each membership test matching src/commands/search.ts:41; Category — metadata['terum-category'] matched the way src/commands/search.ts:43 matches it (case-insensitive substring), OR within the group; Author — the app resolves the selected handle to people/<handle>.json display_name/email and matches metadata.author the way src/commands/search.ts:44 does; Hide installed — the id is absent from this viewer's people/<me>.json installed[]. Groups AND, values within a group OR — stated once, because nothing drawn says it. Name the two counts differently in the component API (activeFacetCount vs matchingSkillCount) so an implementation cannot swap them, and label the Verdict checkbox counts as catalog-wide so "PASS 9" above "Show 6" reads as intended.
- **Cites:** `src/cli.ts:126 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/search.ts:21 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/search.ts:38-44 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/search.ts:50-51 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:62 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:111 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/readme.ts:119-122 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:480 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:481-482 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:253-254 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:134 @ b5c0507`

- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** The suppression is written by the shared uninstallMany path, so it is not confined to the bulk forms. src/commands/uninstall.ts:99 defines isAuto(id) as "the id is on team.json global or on any project's skills"; :105 is the write — for (const target of input.targets) if (isAuto(target.id) && !installed.some((entry) => entry.id === target.id) && !declined.includes(target.id)) declined.push(target.id) — committed into people/<you>.json at :106; the comment at :102-104 gives the one exemption (a scope-targeted removal is skipped only while another scope's install record survives). What declined then does is total: .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:183 ("suppresses ALL automatic placement of that ID"), :193 ("never placed by any automatic path — not the join offer, not a later global publish, not project auto-placement ... Three code paths honour it — endorsedCandidates (src/lib/skills.ts:54-58) consumed at src/commands/sync.ts:257 and src/commands/team.ts:367, sync's placement skip (src/commands/sync.ts:171) and the orphan pass (:314) — and install clears it (src/commands/install.ts:135), so the drawn "you can install it again any time" is TRUE and should survive the rewrite.

#### CP-15 — Marketplace filter defaults (PASS-only, lift ≥ +20 %, installs ≥ 3) shipped off  *(recommended: both; approver: Ajay)*

- **Boards:** MarketplaceFilters, MarketplaceStates
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** §12's hard rules are at .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:495-498: "no skill list is ever sorted or ranked by any receipt number; no lift-style decimal appears at card level; a skill without a receipt shows '—' ...; numbers from different model/cc_version never appear in one comparison surface". §2's out-of-scope list bans "any leaderboard or cross-skill comparison surface (banned by the D29 resolution, not merely deferred)" at :52-53. Filtering is named in neither: the hard rule says sorted or ranked, §2 says leaderboard or comparison surface. A PASS-only default is neither a sort nor a leaderboard, but it is an editorial stance built on the numbers §12 says do not rank — a reader who sees a 17-skill catalog render as 6 has been handed a ranking by another name. The empirical basis is two separate studies and they must not be conflated: .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:31 carries SkillEvaluator's ρ = −0.11, and probe #5's pair lives at .planning/research/2026-09-04-eval-determinism-probe.md:191-193 ("Spearman ρ −0.07 vs SkillsBench all-harness, −0.22 vs its claude-code harness column (n=9) — sitting right beside SkillEvaluator's ρ −0.11").
- **Field:** none
- **Verb or flag:** none
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** none named
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md §12 "UI contract (Teddy)", appended after the hard-rules paragraph at :495-498
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** Filtering is not ranking, and the distinction is deliberate. A viewer-chosen filter over a receipt field — verdict, net lift — narrows a list without ordering it, and is permitted: it states a threshold the viewer set, not a claim that one skill beats another, and the reader can see and clear it. What is not permitted is a DEFAULT filter over a receipt field. A default that arrives with PASS pre-selected, or with a net-lift floor already applied, is the product ranking on the viewer's behalf using precisely the numbers our own probe shows do not rank (Spearman ρ −0.07 against SkillsBench all-harness and −0.22 against its claude-code column, .planning/research/2026-09-04-eval-determinism-probe.md:191-193; §1:31 carries the earlier SkillEvaluator figure, ρ −0.11). Every receipt-derived facet therefore ships off, the filter control shows a zero badge on first run, and the unfiltered catalog is what a new viewer sees. Facets over non-receipt data — install counts from the people files (D38), context size, last-updated — are subject to the same default-off rule for consistency of the control, but only the receipt-derived ones are load-bearing here.
- **Why SIMPLE:** One paragraph into one section, no code, no schema, no guard, one approver — SIMPLE on every bar condition. Worth doing whichever way Teddy decides, because the current position is unwritten and the next reader of §12 will re-litigate it. The paragraph above is drafted for the defaults-off answer, which both reader reports recommend (spec-delta.v2 §7 T10, eval-delta.v2 §8 #5); if Teddy keeps the defaults, it inverts at the same size.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The shipped default is every facet off — all four verdict buckets admitted (including "Not evaluated"), no lift floor, no size ceiling, no install floor, no list/category/author selection, hide-installed off — so the badge reads 0 and the footer reads the full catalog size. Encode "no bound" as a sentinel (None, or ±inf), not as a magic number. The viewer's own selections are per-viewer chrome and may be remembered locally.
- **Cites:** `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:495-498 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:52-53 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:31 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:491 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/research/2026-09-04-eval-determinism-probe.md:191-193 @ b5c0507`

- **Boards:** SkillDetailRemove

ALTERNATIVE, also SIMPLE but not recommended: `.option('--scope <scope>', 'global, or project:<name>; default: every recorded scope')` appended to the `uninstall-skill` registration at src/cli.ts:123 @ b5c0507.

- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** This bullet now states what `uninstall-skill <ref>` has always done. The targets are not one scope: the verb unions the acting handle's people-file `installed` scopes for that id with every `placements` entry for that id in that team, de-duplicates, and clears all of them -- which is what makes it the exact inverse of an install whose scope the user no longer remembers. A people-file scope with no local placement is still cleared (the run says the skill is not placed on this machine and drops the record). Placements belonging to another configured team are never touched. The union rule is exported as `ledgerScopes` so a caller that must disclose the targets before asking for confirmation reads the same rule rather than reimplementing it. No field is added to any committed file, no guard action and no `team.json` row: the durable pending operation and the guard action remain `uninstall` (section 6.0 row b), and the `declined` rule already stated in this bullet is unchanged.
- **Why SIMPLE:** The recommended CLI change is one `export` keyword plus a spec paragraph that documents behaviour already shipped: no new field, no new verb, no new flag, no guard change, tests in the existing src/commands/__tests__ layout, one section, one approver. The `--scope` flag also clears the bar (one flag on one existing verb, no field, no guard) but buys a shipped verb's contract change to fix a dialog title that the design fix corrects for free -- so it is recorded as the alternative, not the recommendation.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The dialog drops the scope from its title and lists the exact targets about to be cleared -- computed from BOTH halves of the union: the acting handle's `installed` entries in `people/<handle>.json` in the team clone (a committed file) and the machine-local `placements` ledger (src/lib/schema.ts:100 @ b5c0507). With `ledgerScopes` exported, the app calls it instead of restating the rule.

- **Verb or flag:** none -- two edited lines in one file: src/lib/config.ts:1 @ b5c0507 becomes `import { invocation, getStartedLines, type InvocationForm } from './invocation.js';` (the module already imports from './invocation.js' on that line) and src/lib/config.ts:28 @ b5c0507 becomes ``throw new Error(getStartedLines(form).join('\n'));``

- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** One route, one spelling. The zero-team message is `getStartedLines` (`src/lib/invocation.ts`) and it names `setup`, per this default: `setup` is the single onboarding entry point for a fresh machine, and `team join` is the explicit second-team flow a first-run user has no reason to reach. `selectTeam`'s zero-team throw and the root help's Get-started block render from that one source, so a verb that fails for want of a team tells the user exactly what `status` does. Nothing else about `selectTeam` changes: the one-team, unknown-`--team` and ambiguous-bare-ref answers are unchanged. `install`'s not-joined message keeps `team join`, because it names a SECOND team on an already-configured machine, which is that flow.

No field, no verb, no flag, no guard row, no team.json write; two of the three tests already exist in the right files and only their expected strings move; one spec paragraph in one section; one approver. Two costs I looked for and did not find: no spec pins the config.ts wording (a repo-wide grep for 'No team is configured' over .planning/specs returns nothing -- only source, tests and a review document), and src/lib/config.ts:28 carries no `terum-skills` literal, so src/lib/__tests__/invocation-tripwire.test.ts:19 @ b5c0507 never scans it and the 72-entry catalogue needs no edit. Ajay is not a second approver: `eval` reaches `selectTeam` (src/commands/eval.ts:63 @ b5c0507) but the eval-engine spec's section 12 UI contract pins receipt-card slots and hard rules only, not CLI error strings.
- **App side:** MEDIUM (D20 ok) — The app pattern-matches src/lib/config.ts:28's text before rendering and substitutes the `setup` copy.
