#### CP-S3 — "The clone is safe to delete; the next sync clones it again" — sync never clones, and an absent clone fails the whole run  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** SettingsTeams, SettingsLeave
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `sync` calls refreshClone for each team (src/commands/sync.ts:117 @ b5c0507), which takes the clone writer lock and runs `git fetch origin` then `git reset --hard origin/main` with `cwd: clone` — it operates INSIDE an existing directory and never clones (src/lib/teamRepo.ts:462-484 @ b5c0507; the reset at :472). WITH THE DIRECTORY GONE THE WHOLE RUN FAILS — it is not the classified per-team skip the coster described. `RemoteAccessError` is constructed only after `git remote get-url origin` SUCCEEDS inside the clone (src/lib/teamRepo.ts:477-479 @ b5c0507), which a missing directory cannot do; the fetch itself is `runner.run('git', ['fetch','origin'], { cwd: clone })`, whose spawn cannot enter a missing cwd and whose error event REJECTS rather than resolving a non-zero code (src/lib/runner.ts:54 @ b5c0507) — and if the lock fails first (`lockfile.lock` on the clone path, src/lib/teamRepo.ts:503 @ b5c0507) the same route is taken. refreshClone rethrows anything that is not ELOCKED (src/lib/teamRepo.ts:486-487 @ b5c0507); sync's per-team catch rethrows anything that is not CloneBusy (src/commands/sync.ts:123 @ b5c0507); the outer catch returns a failed Result (src/commands/sync.ts:295 @ b5c0507). So every other team dies with it and `sync --hook` fails at session start. `describeClone` reports `absent` (src/lib/teamRepo.ts:445-446 @ b5c0507) and `status` prints the real remedy: `  Clone: <path> is missing.` and `  Restore it: <invocation> team join <remote>` (src/commands/status.ts:57-58 @ b5c0507; the prefix is launch-aware since 1d5e3f9 — bare `terum-skills` when the PATH bin is this install, `npx -y terum-skills@latest` otherwise). Two verbs really do re-clone and neither is sync: `team join` via ensureClone (src/commands/team.ts:433 @ b5c0507) and `setup`, but setup's re-clone is GUARDED by `steps.team === 'skipped'` (src/commands/setup.ts:155 @ b5c0507) and applies to the one team the wizard resolved to — with no argument that is `Object.entries(before.teams)[0]`, the FIRST team (src/commands/setup.ts:96 @ b5c0507) — so on a two-team machine bare setup does not repair the second team's clone (src/commands/setup.ts:159-160 @ b5c0507). Spec §6's sync bullet defines the contract as skip-and-report (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:353 @ b5c0507).
- **Field:** none
- **Verb or flag:** none — no new verb and no new flag; one branch inside sync's existing per-team catch (src/commands/sync.ts:120-126 @ b5c0507)
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: a team whose clone directory was deleted is skipped with the restore remedy, and the other teams still complete; src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: sync --hook with an absent clone exits 1 with the notice and never aborts the run
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6, the `sync [--hook]` bullet (:353)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** §6 `sync` — a team whose clone directory is ABSENT is classed with the unreachable teams, not with the run-aborting failures: sync reports `Skipping <team>: <clone> is missing. Restore it: <invocation> team join <remote>` (the pair `status` already prints), skips that team, leaves its placements, shares, orphans and old stamp untouched, and finishes every other team, exiting 1 with the existing `Sync finished with <n> team(s) skipped` line. sync still never creates a clone: restoring one remains `team join` or `setup`. Unclassified fetch failures and reset failures still abort the whole run.
- **Why SIMPLE:** The cheaper fork refuter A found, and it is genuinely SIMPLE: no new field in any owner file, no new verb and no new flag, no guard change, tests that fit the existing sync.test.ts, one paragraph to one spec section, one approver. It does not teach sync to clone — it stops an absent clone from taking down every other team and the session hook, and it reuses the message status.ts:57-58 already composes. The coster costed only the MEDIUM alternative (teaching sync to re-clone through ensureClone), which is correctly MEDIUM — it breaks condition (2) by adding a second behaviour the verb's own contract at spec :353 denies, inside the unattended hook path — and should not be built.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The app corrects the string, and where it wants to be useful it renders the remedy `status` already prints — `Clone: <path> is missing.` / `Restore it: <invocation> team join <remote>` (src/commands/status.ts:57-58 @ b5c0507) — from the StatusResult rather than composing its own advice.
- **Cites:** `src/lib/teamRepo.ts:462-484 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/teamRepo.ts:477-479 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/teamRepo.ts:486-487 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/teamRepo.ts:503 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/teamRepo.ts:445-446 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/runner.ts:54 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:117 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:120-126 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:295 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/status.ts:57-58 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/setup.ts:96 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/setup.ts:155 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/setup.ts:159-160 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:433 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:353 @ b5c0507`

#### EV-07 — Hygiene findings as data (ValidateResult carries a count only)  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ajay)*

- **Boards:** SkillDetailQuality, SkillDetailStates
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** The typed value exists and is exported; what does not cross the verb boundary is the value itself. HygieneFinding = { code: HygieneCode; path: string; line?: number; message: string } (src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:7, codes at :6); inspectHygiene(input): HygieneAssessment is exported and pure - no I/O, the caller supplies the byte map and the executable set (src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:38, contract at .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:420-433); assessHygiene is 'the single pure gate shared by every hygiene caller' (:119-120, :123); formatHygieneFindings (:101), formatHygieneWarnings (:105), HygieneRefused carrying the assessment (:110-117) and reportHygieneWarnings (:128) are all exported. But ValidateResult is TWO COUNTS and no findings: export interface ValidateResult { name: string; findings: number; warnings: number; } (src/commands/validate.ts:12); the failure carries { name, findings: assessment.errors.length, warnings: assessment.warnings.length } (:46) and success { name, findings: 0, warnings } (:50); the findings themselves are only printed (:42 warnings, :44-45 errors). eval's hygiene refusal carries no payload at all - a bare return failure(...) at src/commands/eval.ts:94, even though failure(error, value?) accepts one (src/lib/result.ts:6). There is no --json flag anywhere in src/cli.ts and createExecute (src/lib/execute.ts:21-39) writes only an error line and an exit code, so a verb's Result is meaningful ONLY to an in-process library caller - which is exactly the D21/D23 phase-2 UI (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:59; ledger D23 DECIDED at .planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:136).
- **Field:** In src/commands/validate.ts:12, additive so no existing reader of .findings or .warnings breaks - and carrying the ASSESSMENT, not an array of errors, because a HygieneFinding[] would surface errors as data while leaving the warning tier (the tier the design's warn row now depends on) invisible:   export interface ValidateResult { name: string; findings: number; warnings: number; assessment: HygieneAssessment; } with import type { HygieneAssessment } from '../lib/evals/hygiene.js'; added to the existing hygiene.js import at src/commands/validate.ts:5, then return failure(..., { name, findings: assessment.errors.length, warnings: assessment.warnings.length, assessment }) at :46 and return success({ name, findings: 0, warnings, assessment }) at :50. Optionally the same one-line payload on eval's refusal - return failure(`Hygiene failed for ${record.name}: ${error.message}`, { assessment: error.assessment }) at src/commands/eval.ts:94 - but that changes EvalResult's shape and is better folded into EV-08's single Result change.
- **Verb or flag:** none - validate <path/name> already exists (src/cli.ts:114)
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/validate.test.ts: 'a failing validate returns every error finding as data with its code, path and line, not only a count' (inside describe('validate (§9)') at validate.test.ts:12, which already asserts on printed HYG codes at :21-22 and can assert on the payload instead); src/commands/__tests__/validate.test.ts: 'a clean skill with an oversized SKILL.md returns findings: 0 with the HYG6 size finding in assessment.warnings' (pairs with the existing size-warning case at validate.test.ts:39)
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md section 6.0, the validate bullet at :280-281
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** Rev 17 (Ajay, 2026-09-__), section 6.0: validate's result carries the findings themselves, not only how many there were - { name, findings: number, warnings: number, assessment: HygieneAssessment }, where assessment is the value inspectHygiene returned, verbatim and in order, both buckets. The counts are retained so existing callers are unaffected. This is a payload change only: the exit-code gate, the printed lines and the errors-only fail-closed predicate are unchanged, and no caller may re-derive a finding it was not given. It exists so that a surface rendering the section-9 tier shows the check that failed or warned, its file and its line, rather than a number - the same values formatHygieneFindings and formatHygieneWarnings already print, carried as data instead of text. (This bullet still says 'share'; the verb is 'connect' since 0.1.4 - correct it in the same edit.)
- **Why SIMPLE:** One additive field on one return type in one file, no new verb, no new flag, no guard change, no owner file, no schema field, tests inside the existing describe block, a one-paragraph delta to one section, one approver. Every SIMPLE condition met. It is recommended over the in-process library route because the route the specs bless is the VERB seam: phase-1 section 3 authorises the phase-2 UI to supply a Prompter and 'call the same functions' - meaning commands/<verb>.ts's run(args, io) (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:59) - not deep-importing lib internals, and src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts is exactly the module that already moved once (out of src/lib/ at 3b26647). NAMED PREREQUISITE, shared with EV-02 and EV-08 and belonging to the RM family rather than to this row: package.json at b5c0507 declares bin only - no main, no types, no exports - and tsconfig.build.json emits no declarations, so ANY in-process consumption of a Result or a type needs a packaging surface. That prerequisite is identical on both routes, so it does not choose between them; D23 does.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — Available but not recommended: the app imports assessHygiene / inspectHygiene from the library directly and calls it over the clone's skills/<name>/ bytes, getting { errors, warnings } typed with no CLI change. inspectHygiene is pure and takes its input as a Map<string, Buffer> plus an executable set (src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:38, spec :426-433), so the app supplies them from its own lstat walk exactly as validate does (src/commands/validate.ts:36-37).

#### EV-08 — Eval aggregate on the Result / persisted to the local run tree  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ajay)*

- **Boards:** SettingsEvals
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** Two of the three things the app needs already cross the boundary: EvalResult { team; id; name; runDir; ccVersion; executionStatus: 'complete'/'partial'/'failed'; receiptPath?: string } (src/commands/eval.ts:43-51), returned at src/commands/eval.ts:202. The gap is narrower than the seed's 'EvalResult is a pointer' framing and is real: summary = aggregate(rows, arms, expectedRows, environmentSkips) is computed at src/commands/eval.ts:150, used to build the receipt when --commit (:165-192), PRINTED via renderReport (:157), and then dropped. For a run WITHOUT --commit - including every --working run, where --commit is refused outright (src/commands/eval.ts:56) - the whole Aggregate (verdict, attribution, execution_status, expected_rows, scored_rows, comparisons, arm_scores, efficiency, environment_skips - src/lib/evals/results.ts:40-51) exists only as printed text. run.jsonl's _meta line (src/commands/eval.ts:151-156, written by writeRunTree at src/lib/evals/results.ts:18-24) carries only { team, skill_id, skill_name, run_id, cc_version, model, judge_model, expected_rows, arm_skill_lists } - no verdict, comparisons, arm scores, efficiency, environment_skips, k, cases, runner_handle, engine_version or engine_commit.
- **Field:** In src/commands/eval.ts, one member on the existing interface at :43-51:   /** The aggregate the report printed - already computed at run time, now returned rather than dropped. */   summary: Aggregate; with import type { Aggregate } from '../lib/evals/results.js'; added to the existing results.js import at src/commands/eval.ts:13, and summary spread into the success value at :202:   return success({ team: teamName, id: record.id, name: record.name, runDir, ccVersion: preflight.value.ccVersion, executionStatus: summary.execution_status, summary, ...(committedPath === undefined ? {} : { receiptPath: committedPath }) }); The optional _meta enrichment at src/commands/eval.ts:151-156 (verdict, k, cases, runner_handle, engine_version, comparisons, arm_scores, efficiency, environment_skips) is a SECOND artifact change and is explicitly OUT of the costed scope: the in-process caller holds the Result, so it is not needed.
- **Verb or flag:** none - no new verb, no new flag; --commit and every existing option untouched
- **Guard:** none - nothing new is written to the team repo; runDir is join(store.root, 'evals', ...) (src/commands/eval.ts:103) and writeRunTree writes there (src/lib/evals/results.ts:18-24)
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/eval.test.ts: 'the result carries the same aggregate the report printed, for a run with no --commit' (inside describe('eval (§6 / IE2)') at eval.test.ts:17, alongside the run-tree test at :29 and the receipt test at :49); src/commands/__tests__/eval.test.ts: 'a partial run returns execution_status partial with its scored and expected row counts on the summary'; src/commands/__tests__/eval.test.ts: 'the returned summary.attribution carries no credential-shaped text' (section 8's boundary is asserted rather than left implicit now that a free-text field crosses a process boundary: redact() runs only inside buildReceipt at src/lib/evals/receipt.ts:100, and attributionLine returns a closed vocabulary at src/lib/evals/results.ts:105-120)
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md section 6.0, the eval bullet at :266-275
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** Rev 17 (Ajay, 2026-09-__), section 6.0: eval's result carries the aggregate it printed. EvalResult gains summary: Aggregate - the same object aggregate() returned and renderReport() rendered, byte-for-byte, with no recomputation and no additional field. Nothing new is measured: this only stops an already-computed value from being discarded at the process boundary, and it is what makes a run without --commit (a --working author-loop run in particular, where --commit is refused) legible to a library caller without parsing printed text or re-aggregating raw rows - the latter being the derivation section 12 forbids. attribution is the aggregate's only free text; it is generated from a closed vocabulary and remains subject to section 8 redaction at the point it enters a receipt. The committed receipt remains the only shareable artifact and the only thing any surface may show about someone else's run.
- **Why SIMPLE:** This is the family brief's own SIMPLE archetype - 'returning an already-computed value on the Result'. One field, one file, no new verb, no flag, no guard, no committed file, no schema change, no change to what the engine computes (aggregate() is untouched), tests inside the existing describe block, one paragraph to one section, one approver. Same named prerequisite as EV-02/EV-07 and belonging to the RM family, not to this row: the app must import the Aggregate TYPE from a package that declares bin only with no exports/types (package.json) and emits no declarations (tsconfig.build.json).
- **App side:** FORBIDDEN (D20 no) — The app would parse run.jsonl's raw ComparisonRow / ArmSample lines (src/lib/evals/execution.ts:161-191) and re-run the aggregation itself, or scrape the printed report text.

- **Boards:** SkillDetailEvals, SkillDetailEvalsReport, SkillDetailNoReceipt, SkillDetailPartial, SkillCard, Library
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** No reusable reader is exported, and at b5c0507 there are now FOUR private ones, not three — this is the coster's most materially stale paragraph and both refuters caught it. src/lib/evals/receipt.ts exports only CREDENTIAL_PATTERNS (:16), redact (:26), RUN_ID_PATTERN (:47), receiptSchema (:50), the Receipt type (:88), receiptPath (:91-93) and buildReceipt (:99), and its header declares the module 'Pure (ME1): no I/O' (src/lib/evals/receipt.ts:4 @ b5c0507), so a filesystem reader may not live there. The private readers: latestReceiptVerdict (src/lib/readme.ts:172-181 @ b5c0507), latestReceiptVerdictInTree (src/lib/readme.ts:184-190 @ b5c0507) and receiptVerdict (src/lib/readme.ts:192-201 @ b5c0507) each return a display STRING for the `latest` version only; and commit 4149287 (IE4 key-free CI) added a fourth, src/commands/receiptCheck.ts, behind the hidden verb `receipt-check` (src/cli.ts:115 @ b5c0507) — receiptFiles (src/commands/receiptCheck.ts:92-97 @ b5c0507), newestReceiptAt (:100-109), hasSchemaValidReceipt (:111-119) — and newestReceiptAt is the only one that returns a parsed Receipt. A cross-version walker exists twice: latestReceiptedTree (src/commands/eval.ts:252-267 @ b5c0507) and validReceiptsOutsideVersion (src/commands/receiptCheck.ts:121-129 @ b5c0507). The four copies deliberately DIFFER: receiptVerdict catches a schema-invalid newest file and returns undefined (src/lib/readme.ts:194-200 @ b5c0507 — fail-open, the README prints a dash), while newestReceiptAt THROWS on one (src/commands/receiptCheck.ts:105 and :107 @ b5c0507), with the reason stated at :91 ('A malformed newest file fails closed rather than hiding behind an older run'); src/commands/receiptCheck.ts:94 filters entry.isFile(), src/lib/readme.ts:177 does not; src/commands/eval.ts:262 admits only /^\d{8}T\d{6}Z\.json$/ while the other two take any *.json. So the rule is written four times and exported zero times, and the copies are not interchangeable.

- **Verb or flag:** One new read-only verb, D32's shape (.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:173 @ b5c0507). It must be a sibling, not `eval show`, for two independent reasons: `eval <skill>` already binds its positional (src/cli.ts:116 @ b5c0507), so a subcommand would be swallowed as the skill name — the parent-plus-subcommand pattern works for `ls` only because `ls` takes no argument (src/cli.ts:93-96 @ b5c0507) — and `eval show` is named in the Out (gated/deferred) list at .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:24 @ b5c0507. Register beside eval at src/cli.ts:116 @ b5c0507: `program.command('receipt <ref>').description('Print the latest committed eval receipt for a skill version as JSON; reads the local clone, never fetches').option('--skill-version <tree>', 'a 40-char skill tree hash (default: the clone HEAD version)').option('--runs', 'list the committed run ids for that version instead of the receipt').option('--team <team>', 'configured team (required when more than one exists)').action(async (ref: string, options: { skillVersion?: string; runs?: boolean; team?: string }) => execute((io) => active.receipt({ ref, ...options }, io), { verb: 'receipt', notices: false }));` plus `receipt?: typeof runReceipt` on CliVerbs (src/cli.ts:33 @ b5c0507) and in `active` (src/cli.ts:36 @ b5c0507). New src/commands/receipt.ts exporting run(args, io): selectTeam (src/lib/config.ts:23 @ b5c0507) + store.teamClone (src/lib/config.ts:91 @ b5c0507), findSkill, resolveVersion (src/lib/version.ts:9-28 @ b5c0507), then a NEW module src/lib/evals/receipts.ts exporting `latestReceipt(clone, id, version, { onInvalid: 'undefined' / 'throw' }): Promise<Receipt / undefined>` and `runIds(clone, id, version): Promise<string[]>`. CRITICAL SCOPE RULE: do NOT rewire src/lib/readme.ts or src/commands/receiptCheck.ts onto it in this change — their fail-open vs fail-closed behaviours differ on purpose and re-pointing either one would change the README column or the publish gate, dragging in a second spec section (.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md section 6.1 :289 and section 11 :470-476 @ b5c0507) and breaking condition (5). CRITICAL: the verb must NOT call refreshClone (src/commands/eval.ts:66 @ b5c0507); refreshClone runs `fetch origin` AND `reset --hard origin/main` inside withCloneLock (src/lib/teamRepo.ts:462 @ b5c0507), so a UI read that called it would take the clone WRITER lock and hard-reset the working tree on every render, exactly what D-R4 exists to prevent (the stamp machinery it would bypass is src/lib/hook.ts:157 and :165-166 @ b5c0507).

- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** Section 12 gains one paragraph after the card table: "The UI obtains receipts through `terum-skills receipt <ref> [--skill-version <tree>] [--runs]`, which prints the latest schema-valid receipt for that version as JSON, or that version's committed run ids. The verb reads the local team clone only — it never fetches, never writes and never recomputes — and a receipt whose `skill_id` or `version` disagrees with its path is treated as absent, exactly as the README column already does (section 5.4). It is a third reader of the same rule and is deliberately fail-open like the README column, not fail-closed like the publish receipt check (section 11): a malformed newest receipt makes the UI show no receipt, while CI still blocks the publish. Where the UI and CI can therefore disagree, CI is authoritative."
- **Why SIMPLE:** One new verb, no new field, no guard change, no team.json row, tests in the existing src/commands/__tests__ and src/lib/evals/__tests__ layout, one paragraph into one section, one approver. The new library module carries no field and no guard change, which the bar does not count against it. SIMPLE holds only with the scope rule above: the coster's 'extract once and have readme.ts call it' is NOT a free refactor at b5c0507, because src/commands/receiptCheck.ts:105/:107 fails closed where src/lib/readme.ts:194-200 fails open, so re-pointing either caller would change the publish gate or the README column and pull in section 6.1/section 11 as a second spec section.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The app reads ~/.terum/skills/teams/<team>/evals/<id>/<version>/*.json itself (path from src/lib/config.ts:91 @ b5c0507 plus src/lib/evals/receipt.ts:91-93 @ b5c0507), sorts the filenames, parses the last one and cross-checks skill_id/version against the path.

#### EV-17 — No share-time hygiene result is recorded, so “passed on share, 12 days ago” and “hygiene 6 of 6” have no source  *(recommended: both; approver: Ajay)*

- **Boards:** SkillDetailQuality, SkillDetailStates, Inbox, InboxLight, InboxPanes, InboxStates, SkillDetailEvalsReport
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** Three of the row's load-bearing CLI facts flipped between 8bf12a8 and b5c0507. (a) inspectHygiene no longer returns an array: `export function inspectHygiene(input: HygieneInput): HygieneAssessment` at src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:38 @ b5c0507, where HygieneAssessment = { errors, warnings } (hygiene.ts:9), returned at hygiene.ts:98 (commit 914ebb0, spec rev 16). Callers go through assessHygiene (hygiene.ts:120-125), which throws HygieneRefused (hygiene.ts:110-117); each prints warnings through a caller-owned print callback, reportHygieneWarnings (hygiene.ts:128-130). HYG6 is SPLIT across the two tiers under one code: size over 20,000 UTF-16 code units pushes a WARNING (hygiene.ts:93-95, message 'Size alone does not block this operation'), an empty description pushes an ERROR (hygiene.ts:97). Spec: eval-engine.md:418, :420-426, :446. (b) The CI producer now exists: WORKFLOW is src/commands/team.ts:550-628 with four jobs — hygiene: (team.ts:559), which runs `npx -y terum-skills@latest validate "skills/$name" --cwd .` per changed skill on every PR touching skills/ (team.ts:576), receipt-check: (team.ts:578), readme: (team.ts:588), publish-comment: (team.ts:604); spec eval-engine.md:468-469 (commit 4149287). (c) validate reaches any checkout: `--cwd <team-checkout>` at src/cli.ts:114, implemented at src/commands/validate.ts:19-29 ('The Action runs in an unconfigured team checkout'), so a PR worktree IS addressable. What has NOT changed: nothing writes a hygiene outcome anywhere. receiptSchema (src/lib/evals/receipt.ts:50-87) has no hygiene member, and the §6.0 permitted-writes table (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:317-324, rows a-f) plus row g (.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:450-458) admit no path a hygiene stamp could occupy. validate's Result already carries the two counts — ValidateResult = { name: string; findings: number; warnings: number } at src/commands/validate.ts:12, returned at validate.ts:46 and :50 — and its stdout already carries the finding lines (validate.ts:42, :44-45, :49) in the shapes formatHygieneFindings / formatHygieneWarnings print (hygiene.ts:101-103, :105-107), pinned by src/commands/__tests__/validate.test.ts:39-54. But a Result's value never leaves the bin: createExecute writes only sink.stderr(outcome.error) and the exit code (src/lib/execute.ts:21-39), wired at src/index.ts:41-46. Finally the drawn verb is dead: 'share' was renamed to 'connect' (src/cli.ts:119; hidden refusing 'share' at src/cli.ts:120-121, commit 71e7a0a), and §9's authoritative caller list is validate, connect, publish, sync's reconcile path, eval and CI (eval-engine.md:412) — five recurring local occasions, so there is no single past event a date could name.
- **Field:** none — nothing is committed and no schema changes
- **Verb or flag:** One new flag on the existing verb. src/cli.ts:114 @ b5c0507: append `.option('--json', 'print one JSON line: { name, errors, warnings }, each finding { code, path, line?, message }')` to the existing `program.command('validate <path/name>')` chain (which already carries --team and --cwd) and widen the action's options type to `{ team?: string; cwd?: string; json?: boolean }`. src/commands/validate.ts:11: `export interface ValidateArgs extends WithForm { target: string; team?: string; cwd?: string; json?: boolean; config?: ConfigStore; }`. src/commands/validate.ts:42-50: when args.json is set, emit one line — `io.print(JSON.stringify({ name, errors: assessment.errors, warnings: assessment.warnings }))` — in place of the human lines at :42, :44-45 and :49, leaving both returns (:46 failure, :50 success), the errors/warnings split and the exit code exactly as they are. That is the whole diff: one flag, one optional arg, one branch. Rejected alternative A (zero CLI change): parse validate's stdout, which is already machine-shaped (`HYG3 SKILL.md:41: ...`, `warning HYG6 ...`) and whose ordering is pinned by validate.test.ts:49-51 — it works, but it makes a human surface a contract. Rejected alternative B (the coster's): widen ValidateResult with `details` — unreachable to any consumer that runs the published bin, because execute.ts:21-39 never serializes outcome.value; it works only under the D21/D23 in-process seam (2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:134, :136), which the desktop app has not committed to. A single `details` array would also have to be two (`details`, `warningDetails`) or collapse the tiers §9 forbids collapsing (eval-engine.md:424-425).
- **Guard:** none — no repo write of any kind is added; src/lib/guard.ts is untouched
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/validate.test.ts: --json prints exactly one line of JSON and no human lines, on both the passing and the refusing path; src/commands/__tests__/validate.test.ts: --json keeps errors and warnings in separate arrays, so a HYG6 size warning never lands beside a HYG6 empty-description error; src/commands/__tests__/validate.test.ts: --json carries every finding's code, path, line and message, in the order the human formatter prints them; src/commands/__tests__/validate.test.ts: --json leaves the exit-code contract alone — 0 with warnings only, 1 with any error (mirrors the createExecute assertion at validate.test.ts:52-53); src/commands/__tests__/validate.test.ts: --json under --cwd reports the same findings as the configured-team path for the same tree
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md §9 — one paragraph appended after the rev-16 API block at :420-426
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** **Caller surface (rev 17, §9).** `validate` gains `--json`: one line of JSON on stdout, `{ name, errors, warnings }`, each entry a `HygieneFinding` verbatim (`code`, `path`, `line?`, `message`). It is a print-format option only — `inspectHygiene`'s frozen rev-16 API, `assessHygiene`'s fail-closed gate, the errors/warnings separation and the exit code are unchanged, and the two arrays stay separate so no consumer can gate on a combined count. It exists so a UI can render the six §9 screens without parsing human output. A consumer keys on the finding, not on the code alone: `HYG6` carries both an error (empty `description`) and a warning (SKILL.md over the 20,000-code-unit guideline). `validate` records nothing. Hygiene remains a gate, never a stored artifact, and no committed hygiene result exists anywhere in the team repo, so every surface that shows hygiene shows a check run **now**, against the tree it can currently read, and must be worded that way. The one hygiene statement that survives about the past is implicit in a committed receipt: `eval` runs the §9 tier on the pinned tree before preflight, run-tree creation or any agent call and refuses on any error finding, with HYG4's executable-form half waived (`allowExecutable = !--working`, true on every committable run) and HYG6's size half a non-blocking warning — so a receipt at version `V` attests that `V` produced **no hygiene errors** at that run's timestamp, not that all six screens passed.
- **Why SIMPLE:** The gap is entirely in the tense, and every drawn hygiene OUTCOME is computable on demand, free, with no model call, by a verb that already exists and whose button the design already draws (build.py:1433). Only the pastness — 'on share', '12 days ago', 'on PR #42' — has no source, and after 71e7a0a there is not even a single event to name: §9's caller list (eval-engine.md:412) puts hygiene on five recurring local occasions. Persisting a share-time result is NOT the MEDIUM branch: a committed hygiene artifact needs a new path row in a table the bar declares closed (phase-1-build.md:317-324 rows a-f plus row g at eval-engine.md:450-458), a new schema and a new GuardAction with a new predicate in src/lib/guard.ts — a subsystem, i.e. HARD, approver Ryan. The narrower per-PR branch is now genuinely MEDIUM and half-built: the CI job exists (team.ts:559, :576), so what remains is a `gh` check-run read the app makes nowhere else — and it buys a boolean, not '6 of 6'.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — On opening the Quality tab, and on the Validate button (build.py:1433), the app runs `terum-skills validate <name> --json` and renders the six drawn rows from the returned assessment, keyed by (code, clause) rather than by code alone — HYG6 needs the split because hygiene.ts:95 (size, warning) and hygiene.ts:97 (empty description, error) share one code. A code with no finding renders 'passed'; a warning finding renders the warn branch the design already draws (build.py:1423-1424); an error finding renders 'failed' with the finding's message and line as the row detail. The header states what actually happened — 'Hygiene checks · checked now · free, no model calls', with 'not checked yet' before the first run and 'could not check' when the verb fails. No date, no 'on share'. The review report and the Inbox row lose their hygiene counts outright: the local clone is hard-reset to origin/main on every write (phase-1-build.md:303, :309), so what the reviewer's machine can read is the CURRENTLY PUBLISHED version, not the tree PR #42 proposes — printing a present-tense count there would be a new false claim, not a fix.

A surface may state that a skill's latest receipt carries a different verdict from the latest receipt of a PREVIOUS VERSION, provided each receipt's own provenance line sits beside its own numbers and the two are never merged into one figure, table or derived number; §12's 'never displayed side by side or merged' governs multiple receipts of one version and does not reach across versions. When the two receipts differ in `model` or `cc_version` the surface suppresses the comparison entirely rather than qualifying it, per the hard rule at :495-498. Card-level rendering still carries no lift-style decimal.
- **Why SIMPLE:** Six of the seven kinds are pure derivations over files b5c0507 already writes, using predicates it already implements; no owner file, verb, flag, guard row or spec section moves. Two real constraints, neither of which makes the row non-SIMPLE. (1) §12 and the regression alert. The Inbox row's fact line as drawn — '−56% on d4c3b2a1f0e9 · ryan · was NEUTRAL +11%' (build.py:1825) — puts lift decimals on a list row, which .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:495-496 @ b5c0507 forbids outright ('no lift-style decimal appears at card level'); that is a design fix costing nothing. report_regression's figure (build.py:2039-2056: three lift_number calls at :2044, :2045, :2046 — the coster counted two — plus fig_record and sign_p at :2049) sits in a report body, which §12:493 assigns `comparisons` (W/L/T, net lift, sign p) to, so the CURRENT receipt's numbers are permitted there; only the PREVIOUS VERSION's numbers on the same surface are contested, and §12:481-482's clause is scoped 'For a given version'. Either reading is available: take the design trim and nothing moves, or take the one-paragraph §12 amendment above with Ajay — which SATISFIES bar conditions (5) and (6) rather than breaking them. (2) The fingerprint. `alert·local` compares the placed copy against `config.placements[].fingerprint`, and that fingerprint is skillhub's algorithm copied verbatim (phase-1:397 @ b5c0507); an app re-implementation that differs by one byte reports every placement as edited-locally and is quarantine-bait. The app must therefore NOT re-derive it: `update` is honestly derivable by comparing the clone tree against the placed copy's bytes (the app needs 'differs / same', not the fingerprint string), while `alert·local` is a stated dependency on the CLI's own read-only classification (RM-39), not an app re-implementation.

Dates come from one read-only `git log -1 --format=%aI -- <path>` per item against the clone. The sidebar rows become a named grouping over the derived kinds, not a fourth count. The app never re-implements snapshotSkillDirectory.

- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** HYG7 (rev 17, warning tier): any text file in the map contains a relative path token that resolves to no key of the file map. A token qualifies only when it matches [A-Za-z0-9_./-]+\.[A-Za-z0-9]{1,5}, is not absolute, carries no URI scheme, and does not sit inside a fenced code block or an inline code span; a qualifying token is resolved against the skill folder's own keys, case-sensitively. The finding carries the referencing file's path and the 1-based line number. HYG7 is a WARNING finding only — it is printed by every caller before the gate and never blocks, exactly as the HYG6 size cap does since rev 16 — because a heuristic path matcher in the error tier would refuse a `connect`, a `publish` or a `sync` write on a false positive.
- **Why SIMPLE:** Mechanically additive and SIMPLE by every condition of the bar: no owner-file field, no new verb, no new flag, no guard change, tests that fit src/lib/evals/__tests__/hygiene.test.ts as it stands, ONE one-paragraph delta to ONE section (§9), ONE approver (Ajay). Conditions (5) and (6) are satisfied, not broken — that is the coster's inversion, corrected. The two reasons the coster gave for keeping the predicate out of hygiene are both false at b5c0507: hygiene is no longer uniformly fail-closed (src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:9, :122 @ b5c0507; .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:418, :425-426 @ b5c0507), and the 'predicates frozen' clause is gone from §9 (:420 @ b5c0507). ONE CONDITION IS LOAD-BEARING and must be written into the change list or this becomes a regression: the finding MUST be pushed to `warnings`, never `errors` (src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:40, :98 @ b5c0507) — an error-tier HYG7 would gate connect/publish/sync's first write on a heuristic, which is exactly the failure the coster feared and the only real risk left.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — A read-only scanner run against the placed copy for an installed skill's Library flag and against skills/<name>/ in the clone for a Marketplace flag. One written predicate, identical to the HYG7 text above. Never gates any write.

#### IB-06 — Open endorsement PR list (endorsement opened / merged events)  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan (phase-1 spec))*

- **Boards:** InboxPanes, Inbox, InboxStates, SettingsInbox
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** One function knows about endorsement PRs and it is module-private: `openEndorsements(runner, clone, name, remote)` at src/commands/publish.ts:189 @ b5c0507 — one `git ls-remote --heads origin refs/heads/publish/<name> refs/heads/publish/<name>-*` at :190 (throwing a named error on non-zero at :191), branch parsing at :192, a gh-availability gate at :193, then per branch one `gh pr list -R <owner/repo> --head <branch> --state open --json url -q .[0].url` at :200 with the leftover-branch drop at :201. Its result type `OpenEndorsement { branch; by; url }` is a private interface at src/commands/publish.ts:179 @ b5c0507; the `by` decode is name-anchored at :196-197 (`branch.startsWith(\`publish/${name}-\`) && /-[0-9a-f]{8}$/`, then `branch.slice(...)`), the pre-R2 legacy forms are documented at :182-183, and the branch convention is minted at :70. It has exactly one caller, src/commands/publish.ts:72 @ b5c0507, feeding a note and a y/N at :74-78. `publish <ref>` takes a REQUIRED operand (src/cli.ts:108-112 @ b5c0507). Nothing else in the CLI lists PRs, nothing merges one (there is no `gh pr merge` anywhere at b5c0507), and `ls` says so out loud: its mandatory footer is 'Team status is from local clones and may be stale; open endorsement requests are not checked.' (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:343 @ b5c0507). The PR gate itself is spec'd at :342 ('merge is the review'). DECISIVE for the KIND as drawn: `gh pr create` at src/commands/publish.ts:128-134 @ b5c0507 passes -R/--base/--head/--title/--body and NO --reviewer, so no pull request the CLI opens ever carries a review request — the design's 'Someone asked you to review a skill' has no producer.
- **Field:** none
- **Verb or flag:** ONE new read-only verb (not a flag on `publish`, which the coster got wrong): in src/cli.ts, beside the `publish` registration at src/cli.ts:108-112 @ b5c0507, add `program.command('endorsements', { hidden: true }).description('List the open endorsement pull requests on the team remote').option('--team <team>', 'configured team (required when more than one exists)').action(async (options: { team?: string }) => execute((io) => active.endorsements({ form: context.form, ...options }, io), { verb: 'endorsements', notices: false }));` with `src/commands/endorsements.ts` exporting `run(args, io): Promise<Result<{ open: OpenEndorsement[]; prsChecked: boolean }>>`, reusing one `git ls-remote --heads origin 'refs/heads/publish/*'` plus ONE `gh pr list -R <owner/repo> --state open --json url,headRefName,author,title` (not N per-branch `--head` lookups), so `author` comes from GitHub directly and the name-anchored `by` decode at src/commands/publish.ts:196-197 @ b5c0507 is not needed at all. A flag on `publish` is the WRONG door: `publish <ref>`'s operand is required (src/cli.ts:108-112 @ b5c0507) and the refspec pair (:190) and `by` decode (:196-197) are both name-anchored, so a repo-wide mode is a second code path plus an operand-contract change, not 'one optional parameter'.
- **Guard:** none — the path is read-only and never enters safeWrite; src/lib/guard.ts is untouched.
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/endorsements.test.ts: endorsements lists every publish/* head with its open pull-request URL and author; src/commands/__tests__/endorsements.test.ts: endorsements drops a branch whose pull request is no longer open; src/commands/__tests__/endorsements.test.ts: endorsements reports the ls-remote failure instead of an empty list; src/commands/__tests__/endorsements.test.ts: without gh, endorsements lists the branches and states that pull-request state was not checked; src/commands/__tests__/publish.test.ts: publish still notes an already-open endorsement and asks the y/N (openEndorsements unchanged)
- **Spec section:** /tmp/.../cli-pin/.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6 — one paragraph after the `publish` bullet at :342, reusing the honesty rule the `ls` footer states at :343.
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** `endorsements [--team <team>]` (hidden, read-only, no write, no prompt) lists the endorsement branches on the team remote — `publish/<name>-<handle>-<id8>` plus the pre-R2 forms `publish/<name>` and `publish/<name>-2` — with each branch's open pull-request URL, title and author where `gh` is authenticated on a GitHub remote, and pushes nothing. It is the same remote probe `publish` already runs before opening a branch, exposed for a caller that needs the list rather than the y/N, and it keeps `publish`'s leftover rule: a `publish/`-prefixed head whose pull request is not open is dropped. Without `gh`, or on a generic-git remote, it lists the branches and states that pull-request state was not checked; a failed `ls-remote` is an error, never an empty list — the same honesty `ls`'s footer states. It does not make an endorsement addressed to anyone: `publish` requests no reviewers, so an open endorsement is open to the whole team.
- **Why SIMPLE:** SIMPLE by every condition: no owner file, no schema field, no guard row, no team.json write, no privacy call, no engine change; exactly ONE new verb (condition (2)'s other door); tests that fit src/commands/__tests__ as it stands; one paragraph into one spec section; one approver. It belongs in the CLI rather than the app because the branch-naming convention (src/commands/publish.ts:70, :196-197 @ b5c0507), the pre-R2 legacy forms (:182-183) and the leftover-branch drop rule (:201) are CLI rules that move with R2-style rulings, and two implementations of a moving rule is the drift failure mode. Refuter B's counter is recorded and partly conceded: with `--json author` the `by` decode disappears, so the shared surface shrinks to one predicate ('keep publish/-prefixed heads, drop the ones with no open PR'), which makes the app route defensible if Ryan's time is the binding constraint.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The app shells `gh pr list -R <owner/repo> --state open --json url,headRefName,author,title` itself and keeps only the 'publish/-prefixed heads with an open PR' predicate; it caches nothing.

- **Boards:** Library, LibraryEmpty, Inbox, InboxEmpty, InboxLoading, SettingsInbox
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** The premise 'no counter exists for any of the three' is now HALF FALSE at b5c0507. 'failing evals' / 'not evaluated': IE4 (4149287) landed src/commands/receiptCheck.ts, registered hidden at src/cli.ts:115 @ b5c0507, which computes per skill and deterministically 'missing receipt at current tree hash', 'stale receipt: receipts exist ... but none are at current tree hash' (both src/commands/receiptCheck.ts:69 @ b5c0507) and 'candidate-vs-incumbent comparison bands FAIL' (:80), via `newestReceiptAt` (:100-109), `receiptFiles` (:92-97) and `validReceiptsOutsideVersion` (:121-129), with `checkSkill` at :60-89 and the result type `ReceiptCheckResult { endorsed; checked }` at :13. It is not yet a cross-skill scan only because run() gates on a publish PR: `git show <base>:team.json` at :23, the `addedEndorsements(...).length === 0` failure at :27-28, then iterating `endorsed` at :32 rather than the `records` it already fetched at :30. The older readers remain module-private (`latestReceiptVerdict` src/lib/readme.ts:172-181, `latestReceiptVerdictInTree` :184-190, `receiptVerdict` :192-201 @ b5c0507, called once from :166; the export list at :12,13,15,26,40,46,51,55,60,102,119,126,142,148,210,224 contains none), and `status` still prints exactly 'Evaluated skills: not yet available' (src/commands/status.ts:84 @ b5c0507, spec .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:345 @ b5c0507). 'updates available': unchanged — the classification lives only inside sync's writing loop (src/commands/sync.ts:207-208 @ b5c0507 with the will-it-place gate at :186 / `approved()` at :299-301), vocabulary at .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:353 @ b5c0507; there is no read-only query (RM-39).
- **Field:** none
- **Verb or flag:** none FOR THIS ROW — the two scans it consumes are EV-19 and RM-39 and are costed there. Recorded for those rows, not claimed here: EV-19's dependency is now SIMPLE-shaped rather than open, because a repo-wide mode of `receipt-check` is one flag on an existing verb plus one branch — `--all` skipping the `git show <base>:team.json` read at src/commands/receiptCheck.ts:23 @ b5c0507 and the endorsement gate at :27-28, and iterating `records` (already fetched at :30) through the unchanged `checkSkill` at :60-89.

- **Tests:** none named

- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** none required.
- **Why SIMPLE:** IB-08 is not a missing capability, it is a missing definition. Three numbers on one screen are drawn from a hardcoded dict and a hand-written composite that disagree with each other and with the list they summarise. Fixing that costs one rule and zero CLI surface. The row inherits dependencies rather than breaking a condition: the numbers cannot be computed until EV-19 (a read-only cross-skill receipt scan) and RM-39 (a read-only update classification) exist. If either comes back non-SIMPLE, IB-08's DEFINITION is still SIMPLE — the composite renders with the sets that are available and suppresses the rest, rather than inventing them.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — One rule, four consequences. (1) ONE DEFINITION PER SET, computed once per refresh and shared by every surface. `Shared with you` = the endorsedCandidates predicate (in team.json.global, not in installed[], not in declined[] — src/lib/skills.ts:57-58 @ b5c0507). `Updates` = placements the clone has moved past AND whose grant hash still matches, i.e. the ones sync will actually place (src/commands/sync.ts:207-208 with approved() at :299-301 @ b5c0507); a placement held back by a widened grant needs a decision, so it belongs to Alerts, not Updates. `Alerts` = the alert kind only — local, missing, regression (and offtarget if IB-03 were ever built). (2) `Needs attention` = /Alerts/, exactly, with its sub-lines becoming the alert sub-kinds, so the tile and the sidebar row are the same number by construction and 'Open alerts' lands where the number came from. If Teddy keeps a composite, it must be the UNION of the kinds the link opens, de-duplicated, with 'not evaluated' excluded because it is not an Inbox kind and a young skill with no receipt is a normal state, not a fault (.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:495-498 @ b5c0507: a skill without a receipt shows an em dash). (3) EVERY NUMBER RENDERS FROM THE SAME DERIVATION CALL, so no two can disagree. (4) THE RULE MUST STATE WHOSE FACTS IT COUNTS: regression and missing are team truth (from the clone), local and updates are facts about THIS machine's placements — a single 'Needs attention' number over both populations is still a mixture, so either the tile is scoped per-machine and says so, or it splits.

#### MC-10 — Session-start hook toggle: no verb toggles it, an installed entry is never refreshed, and the design contradicts itself  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** SettingsSync, SettingsAdvanced, SettingsStates
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** NO VERB TOGGLES THE HOOK. src/cli.ts:49-130 registers the complete verb set at b5c0507 — login :50, setup :55, team create/join/remove/leave/workflow-update :60-86, invite :89, ls :93-96, status :97, readme :99, guard-push :104, publish :108, validate :114, receipt-check :115, eval :116, connect :119, share (hidden pointer) :121, install :122, uninstall-skill :123, uninstall :124, sync :125, search :126, update :128 — and there is no `hook`. The two functions a toggle needs are already exported and already do the hard parts: `installHook` (src/lib/hook.ts:89-114 — read+validate :90, entry-level replace :93-110, backup once :111, mode-preserving atomic rename :112) and `removeHook` (:116-128 — strips only our commands :122, prunes empty containers :123-124, backs up :125, writes atomically :126). `hookInstalled` (:57-61) is the only read and it returns a BARE BOOLEAN. Callers today are lifecycle-only: `offerHook` from src/commands/team.ts:459-461 (`offerHookAfterDurableWork`, from create :294 and join :375) and src/commands/setup.ts:209; `removeHook` from src/commands/uninstallMachine.ts:68 and src/commands/leave.ts:46 — and in `leave` ONLY when the team left was the last configured one (src/commands/leave.ts:42 `const lastTeam = Object.keys((await store.read()).teams).length === 0;`, the guarded call at :43-48). Applying corrections.settings.json MC-10: on a two-team machine no `leave` removes the hook, so `uninstall` is the only unconditional remover — which makes build.py:3765 the one string in this row that is exactly right today, and build.py:3648's toggle the one that is not. HONESTY PROBLEM BEHIND THE DRAWN ROW: `offerHook` short-circuits at src/lib/hook.ts:130-131 (`if (await hookInstalled(...)) { io.print('Session hook already installed in ...'); return 'present'; }`), and the spec says so on purpose — .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:403 "An existing matching hook entry is not refreshed by `offerHook`; it returns `present`. A hook-command change needs an explicit migration." The command the row DESCRIBES comes from src/lib/hook.ts:12 (`HOOK_COMMAND = 'npx -y terum-skills@latest sync --hook'`, entry at :13), but the command ARMED is whatever bytes are in ~/.claude/settings.json, and `matchingEntry` (:23-31) matches the loose predicate `command.includes('terum-skills')` at :29 — so a machine can be armed with a materially different command and still read as "installed". The drawn row must come from the file, never from the constant. The one thing the design gets exactly right: "at most once an hour per team" is `STAMP_FRESH_MS = 60 * 60_000` (src/lib/hook.ts:149), consumed at src/commands/sync.ts:106.
- **Field:** none
- **Verb or flag:** One new verb, registered with the M2 block after src/cli.ts:118 (the comment there asks for it, so the M1/M3 commander edits stay mechanically mergeable): ```ts program.command('hook [state]')   .description('Show, install, or remove the Claude Code session-start hook in ~/.claude/settings.json (on/off; no argument reports what is installed)')   .action(async (state: string / undefined) => execute((io) => active.hook({ form: context.form, state }, io), { verb: 'hook', notices: true })); ``` plus `hook?: typeof runHook` on CliVerbs (src/cli.ts:33) and its default in `active` (src/cli.ts:36). New file src/commands/hook.ts exporting `run(args, io)`: bare → read ~/.claude/settings.json and print the entry's ACTUAL `command` string (or "not installed"); `on` → `installHook(defaultHookOptions(store.root))` (src/lib/hook.ts:89, :16-18); `off` → `removeHook(...)` (:116). It must call `installHook`, NOT `offerHook` — `offerHook` (:130) is the y/N wrapper for a terminal wizard and short-circuits on `present` (:131), which is exactly the behaviour a switch must not inherit.
- **Guard:** none. src/lib/guard.ts:6 scopes the guard to team-repo diffs and `guard()` at :43-55 walks `tree.changedPaths`; ~/.claude/settings.json is never in a tree. No PEOPLE_ACTIONS change (:36), no GuardAction addition (:10), no team.json row.
- **Tests:** src/lib/__tests__/hook.test.ts: 'reports the command actually present in settings.json, not HOOK_COMMAND'; src/commands/__tests__/hook.test.ts (new file in the existing directory): 'hook on installs the canonical entry into an absent settings file and backs up nothing'; src/commands/__tests__/hook.test.ts: 'hook on over a stale terum-skills command replaces it in place and reports replaced'; src/commands/__tests__/hook.test.ts: 'hook off removes only our command, preserves a mixed group, and is idempotent'; src/commands/__tests__/hook.test.ts: 'hook with no argument writes nothing and reports the installed command verbatim'; src/commands/__tests__/leave.test.ts: 'leaving the last configured team still removes the hook and says so' (regression on the leave.ts:42-48 coupling the new verb does not change); src/lib/__tests__/invocation-catalog.ts + src/lib/__tests__/invocation-tripwire.test.ts: every new source line containing the literal `terum-skills` (the verb description, any routed hint) must be added to the catalog, or the tripwire at invocation-tripwire.test.ts:7-23 fails; any routed hint is tested in omitted and bare form per .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:467
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §8 "Session-start hook" (:401-436) — ONE section, but three sentences move: :403, :405 and :435.
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** `hook [on/off]` is the explicit switch for the session-start entry, and it is the migration this section already requires. With no argument it prints the command actually found in `~/.claude/settings.json` — read from the file, never from `HOOK_COMMAND` — or reports that no entry is installed. `on` calls `installHook` directly rather than `offerHook`: it does not short-circuit on an existing entry, so it is how a stale hook command is corrected on a machine that has one, and it asks no y/N because a user who asks for the switch has given the consent. The rule above — installed only after y/N at create/join or at the wizard's step 7, exactly one offer per run, never silent, never two — governs the OFFER, which is unchanged; an explicit `hook on` is not an offer. `off` calls `removeHook`, which strips only this tool's commands and leaves a mixed group's other hooks intact, and it joins `team leave` of your last configured team and `uninstall` in the removal list above. Both take the same parse-before-write, backup-once and atomic-rename contract as every other write to that file. The lifecycle is otherwise unchanged: a machine whose user turned the hook on and then leaves their last configured team loses the entry, because an armed hook with no configured team syncs nothing.
- **Why SIMPLE:** (1) no new field anywhere — the hook lives in ~/.claude/settings.json, which is neither a people file nor config.json nor team truth; (2) exactly one new verb; (3) no guard change; (4) tests extend the existing src/lib/__tests__/hook.test.ts suite plus one new file in the existing src/commands/__tests__/ directory (the layout condition names that directory); (5) one section, §8; (6) one approver, Ryan. The implementation is a thin wrapper: installHook (src/lib/hook.ts:89-114) and removeHook (:116-128) are already exported, already atomic, already backed up, already targeted, and already called from four places. NO D-R4 INTERACTION, re-verified: neither function touches run/; `stampPath` is src/lib/hook.ts:157 and `writeStamp` is src/commands/sync.ts:347, called from sync alone, so the hook's own hourly suppression (src/lib/hook.ts:149, consumed src/commands/sync.ts:106) is unaffected. WHAT REFUTER A GOT RIGHT AND WHY IT DOES NOT FLIP THE VERDICT: adding a user-facing verb to this product does NOT force §2 or §6. §2's In-list at :22 already names "the session-start hook" as an in-scope deliverable, and §6's per-verb bullet list is provably not a closed registry — `validate` is a visible registered verb (src/cli.ts:114) with no §6 bullet and no §2:22 entry, specced instead in .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:280. EXPLICITLY NOT INCLUDED and a separate row: AUTOMATICALLY refreshing a stale entry across a fleet remains what :403 calls an explicit migration and is MEDIUM — it needs a version marker on HOOK_ENTRY (src/lib/hook.ts:13 carries none) plus a migration paragraph. `hook on` gives one user a manual migration; it gives the fleet nothing.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — Two halves, and only one is the app's. READ (the app's, and required whatever ships): the adapter reads ~/.claude/settings.json, finds the SessionStart entry whose command contains `terum-skills`, and renders build.py:3645's description from THAT string, so the row names the command actually armed on this machine. WRITE (not the app's): the toggle spawns `terum-skills hook on` / `hook off` and re-reads.

#### MC-12 — The release observation is never refreshed under an app spawn, so the drawn 0.1.2/0.1.3 pair goes stale  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** SettingsUpdates, SettingsAbout
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** Four facts. (1) AN APP SPAWN IS NEVER INTERACTIVE: src/lib/prompt.ts:61 `const interactive = streams.interactive ?? Boolean(input.isTTY);` and the bin passes no override — src/index.ts:43 `io: terminalPrompter({ outputClosed })`. (2) SO NO APP-DRIVEN SYNC EVER REFRESHES THE ADVERTISEMENT: src/commands/sync.ts:43 `const interactive = !args.hook && !args.prune && io.interactive && 'confirm' in io;` gates the probe (`maintainReleaseState`, :45-48) against local evidence only (`recordRunningAndRegistry`, :49); the spec states the predicate verbatim at .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:295. (3) THE STDERR NOTICE IS SEPARATELY DEAD IN A GUI: src/index.ts:38-40 leaves `afterVerb` UNDEFINED unless `process.stderr.isTTY`, so `updateNotice` (src/lib/update.ts:172-179) never runs and the 24 h `ack` at :178 is never written — which also OVERTURNS .review/2026-09-07-desktop-spec/gap-3.md:322 (G1-5, "the 24 h ack is spent anyway"); the catalogue row's "never spent" is the correct reading. `noUpdateCheck` at src/index.ts:37 reads exactly CI // NO_UPDATE_NOTIFIER // TERUM_SKILLS_NO_UPDATE_NOTIFIER, which makes build.py:3668 exactly right. (4) `update` DOES PROBE — BUT ON THE DRAWN LAUNCH SHAPE IT PRINTS NO NUMBER: src/commands/update.ts:19 passes `force: true` and src/lib/update.ts:147 (`if (!args.force && fresh(state?.attempt?.at, now)) return null;`) means force bypasses the 24 h backoff, so `update` probes on every run `probePolicy` permits (src/lib/update.ts:88-91; RELEASE_PROBE_POLICY = 'github-teams', src/lib/constants.ts:4 — i.e. only when a configured team has a GitHub remote). But the printed line is conditional: src/commands/update.ts:28 `else if (description.matches // launch.kind === 'global' // launch.kind === 'local' // !observation) lines.push(`Latest advertised release: ${observed}`);` — with a newer advertisement and `launch.kind === 'npx'`, that arm is skipped and :33 pushes `advice(launch)` instead. Pinned byte-for-byte: src/commands/__tests__/update.test.ts:24 (npx) and :26 (unknown) and :25 (source) carry NO version line against a 0.1.1 advertisement with 0.1.0 running (:13-14); only global (:22) and local (:23) print it. CONSEQUENCE: `npx -y terum-skills@latest update` — the exact spelling build.py:3673 prints — refreshes the observation but returns no number for build.py:3667's "0.1.3 available". The state file is explicitly machine-local (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:117 "machine-local, never team truth"; :134 "safe for a user to delete this file; no reset flag ships") and §6's `update` paragraph at :291 calls the per-mode templates the byte-for-byte R.8 contract.
- **Field:** none
- **Verb or flag:** none — no new verb and no new flag. ONE TOKEN at src/commands/update.ts:28: replace `else if (description.matches // launch.kind === 'global' // launch.kind === 'local' // !observation)` with a bare `else`, so the advertised-release line prints in every launch mode, keeping the `policy === 'nobody'` arm (:26) and the probe-failure arm (:27) ahead of it. NOT the coster's `else if (observation)`: that deletes the `Latest advertised release: unknown` line that `observed` (:22) plus :28's `// !observation` disjunct prints TODAY in every mode, and which src/commands/__tests__/update.test.ts:61 asserts (the prerelease-only probe case, launch `global`). The advice block (src/commands/update.ts:39-46) is untouched — it still branches five ways on launch.kind, which is why the app must render it verbatim rather than composing an npm line (RM-08; corroborated at .review/2026-09-07-desktop-spec/gap-3.md:321).
- **Guard:** none — src/lib/guard.ts:6 scopes the guard to team-repo diffs; run/latest-version.json is machine-local (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:117).
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/update.test.ts:24 ('prints the exact npx template'): the expectation gains `Latest advertised release: 0.1.1 (observed ${at})` at index 2, after the `This copy:` line; src/commands/__tests__/update.test.ts:25 ('prints the exact source template'): same — the coster and refuter B both missed or under-counted this one; refuter A caught it; src/commands/__tests__/update.test.ts:26 ('prints the exact unknown template'): same; src/commands/__tests__/update.test.ts:38 ('preserves the npx request without calling it pinned'): `expect(io.lines[2]).toBe(`Cache request recorded as: …`)` BREAKS — the advice block shifts down one — and must become index 3, or better a `toContain`. NEITHER REFUTER FOUND THIS; it is the fourth assertion the widening changes, so the change is four assertions, not the coster's two or refuter B's three; src/commands/__tests__/update.test.ts: 'prints Latest advertised release: unknown in every launch mode when nothing has ever been observed' (new; generalises the existing global-only assertion at :61 so the regression the coster's edit would have introduced cannot come back); src/commands/__tests__/update.test.ts: 'a probe failure still reports the prior observation and exits 0' (existing at :46-51, re-asserted — the :27 arm must keep precedence over the widened :28); src/commands/__tests__/update.test.ts: 'force probes under a non-interactive Prompter and rewrites the advertisement' (new; pins fact (4) — that `update` is the only path that refreshes for a GUI, src/commands/update.ts:19)
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6, the `update` paragraph at :291 (rev 11) — one section, and it is where the phrase "when applicable" lives.
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** `update` prints the latest advertised release whenever the release-state file holds an observation — and `Latest advertised release: unknown` when it holds none — in every launch mode. The rev-11 phrase "when applicable" previously meant "when this copy is global or local, when the advertisement matches the running version, or when nothing has ever been observed", which left the npx, source and unknown modes printing update advice with no version in it: two of them are the modes a GUI front end actually spawns, and one is the spelling the product's own printed commands use. The advice block is unchanged and still branches five ways on how this copy was installed; only the advertised-release line becomes unconditional. The R.8 byte-for-byte templates in `src/commands/__tests__/update.test.ts` are updated for the npx, source and unknown modes, and the npx-request assertion that indexes into the printed lines moves down by one.
- **Why SIMPLE:** (1) no new field; (2) no new verb and no new flag — one widened condition inside an existing verb; (3) no guard change; (4) the test file already exists and already owns these templates (src/commands/__tests__/update.test.ts:18-29); (5) one paragraph to one section (:291); (6) one approver, Ryan. The honest caveat, stated rather than buried: this IS an amendment to a contract the spec calls "enforced byte-for-byte", so it is deliberate — but it is one printed line in three of five modes, with the four assertions that enforce it in the same commit. NO D-R4 INTERACTION: `update` touches only createReleaseState → run/latest-version.json (src/commands/update.ts:15, src/lib/update.ts:36-37); it never syncs and never writes run/<team>.stamp (src/lib/hook.ts:157, src/commands/sync.ts:347), so pressing the drawn Check button cannot suppress the SessionStart hook's hourly sync. THE CHANGE IS ALSO OPTIONAL IN ONE SHAPE, recorded rather than hidden: a `global`-launched copy already prints the line today (src/commands/update.ts:28, pinned update.test.ts:22), and rev 14 (1d5e3f9) made a PATH-resolved global install first-class — `resolveInvocationForm` returns 'bare' exactly when launch.kind === 'global' and the first PATH `terum-skills` realpaths to this install (src/lib/invocation.ts:22, :34; printed at :48-50) — while the product's own invite copy recommends `npm install -g terum-skills` (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:339). So an app that spawns a global install needs no CLI change at all; an app that spawns the drawn `npx` spelling does.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The drawn Check button (build.py:3776) and Show update command button (build.py:3667) are the SAME spawn: `update`, which passes force: true (src/commands/update.ts:19) and is the only path that refreshes the advertisement for a GUI. The app renders its stdout verbatim — never composing an npm or npx line, because advice(launch) branches five ways (src/commands/update.ts:39-46) — and draws "as of …" from the `(observed <ISO>)` suffix update already prints (src/commands/update.ts:22) or from the moment it ran. ONE APP RULE THE ROW NEEDS: Check must be a USER GESTURE ONLY — never on mount, focus, or a timer — because force: true bypasses the 24 h backoff (src/lib/update.ts:147), so every press is a live `git ls-remote` against github.com (the probe command at .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:295). That is the D-R4 spirit (gap-4 §12: the app never auto-syncs on a timer) applied to the probe.

#### RM-01 — Skill detail body (SKILL.md prose under the frontmatter)  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** SkillDetail, SkillDetailLight, SkillDetailFiles, SkillDetailNotInstalled, SkillDetailDisabled, SkillDetailStates
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `SkillRecord` is `{ id, name, team, directory, frontmatter, grants }` (src/lib/skills.ts:7-14 @ b5c0507, populated at src/lib/skills.ts:33 @ b5c0507) -- no body, no file list, no size. `parseSkillFrontmatter` matches with the exported `FRONTMATTER` regex (src/lib/schema.ts:147 @ b5c0507) and returns `{ ok, data, grants }` (src/lib/schema.ts:150-159 @ b5c0507, the return at :158), throwing the body away even though `match[0].length` is in hand. No verb Result carries it: `LsSkill` (src/commands/ls.ts:18 @ b5c0507), `SearchHit` (src/commands/search.ts:15-19 @ b5c0507), `InstalledResult` (src/commands/install.ts:33 @ b5c0507). BUT the bytes are already reachable for an in-process caller: `sourceFiles(root)` is exported and returns every file's Buffer plus the executable set (src/lib/skill-source.ts:66 @ b5c0507), `FRONTMATTER` is re-exported there (src/lib/skill-source.ts:7 @ b5c0507), and `SkillRecord.directory` is public (src/lib/skills.ts:11 @ b5c0507) -- so the row's premise 'body is not parsed anywhere' is half stale: nothing parses it, and nothing has to. There is no tokenizer anywhere in src/: the only size measurement is HYG6, which counts UTF-16 code units against a 20,000-CHARACTER guideline and only warns (src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:95 @ b5c0507, warnings gate nothing per src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:8-9 @ b5c0507; changed at 914ebb0). `skillRecords` always reads the CLONE (src/lib/skills.ts:20-28 @ b5c0507), never the placed copy.
- **Field:** Four edits, one value. (1) src/lib/schema.ts:150 signature -> `{ ok: true; data: SkillFrontmatter; grants: AllowedTools; body: string } / { ok: false; error: string }`. (2) src/lib/schema.ts:158 -> `return { ok: true, data: parsed.data, grants: allowedTools(parsed.data['allowed-tools']), body: source.slice(match[0].length) };`. (3) src/lib/skills.ts:12 add `body: string;` to `SkillRecord` and src/lib/skills.ts:33 add `body: parsed.body,`. (4) MANDATORY, and the coster's list omitted it: src/commands/install.ts:160 `skillAtSource` returns `{ ...expected, directory: source, frontmatter: parsed.data, grants: parsed.grants }` -- the spread satisfies the widened type, so without `body: parsed.body` a record built from a materialized pinned version silently carries the CLONE's body (callers src/commands/install.ts:94, src/commands/sync.ts:141 and :185). No owner-file field: nothing is written to people/<handle>.json, config.json or team.json.
- **Verb or flag:** none
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/lib/__tests__/schema.test.ts: parseSkillFrontmatter returns the body after the closing --- (CRLF and no-trailing-newline included); src/lib/__tests__/schema.test.ts: a file with no frontmatter still fails and reports no body; src/lib/__tests__/skills.test.ts: skillRecords carries each record's SKILL.md body; src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts: skillAtSource returns the pinned source's body, not the clone's
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 5.3 SKILL.md frontmatter (:195 @ b5c0507)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** Section 5.3 addition. `parseSkillFrontmatter` returns the SKILL.md body -- the bytes after the closing `---` -- alongside the parsed frontmatter and the normalized grants, and `SkillRecord` carries it as `body`. This adds no field to any file on disk and changes no parse rule: the boundary is the existing `FRONTMATTER` match the function already computes, so the split reaches a consumer as a value rather than as a regex a second reader has to reproduce. It does not unify the three sibling helpers that each re-implement the same boundary with their own literal (`declaredCategory`, `injectManagedFields`, `canonicalSkillMd`); that unification is a separate change and is not proposed here. Every caller that wants only frontmatter ignores the field, and the enumerator does no extra I/O because the file is already read in full. `skillAtSource` must set `body` from the source it parsed, so a record built from a materialized pinned version never carries the clone's prose.
- **Why SIMPLE:** One value returned from the one function that already computes its boundary, plus the three places that copy a SkillRecord. No owner-file field, no verb, no flag, no guard, no team.json write, no privacy call, no engine change, no sync/stamp interaction. The floor is genuinely zero for an in-process caller (src/lib/skill-source.ts:66 @ b5c0507 plus the exported FRONTMATTER at src/lib/schema.ts:147 @ b5c0507), so this is the cheapest non-zero rung, taken so the frontmatter/body boundary crosses the seam as data.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — Read `<clone>/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` (clone = the deterministic path `ConfigStore.teamClone` builds, src/lib/config.ts:91-93 @ b5c0507) or `~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` for an installed copy; split on the frontmatter; render the remainder as markdown; readdir + stat the folder for the file list, byte size and line count.
