#### RM-02 — allowed-tools / grants on the card and the approvals view  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** SkillDetailQuality, SkillDetailInstall, MarketplaceProjectInstall, Inbox, SettingsMachine
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `SkillRecord.grants` (src/lib/skills.ts:13 @ b5c0507, populated at src/lib/skills.ts:33 @ b5c0507) is `AllowedTools = { ok: true; normalized: string; hash: string } / { ok: false; raw: unknown }` (src/lib/schema.ts:116 @ b5c0507), normalized by `allowedTools()` (src/lib/schema.ts:124-132 @ b5c0507): absent or empty => the literal `none`; a list or comma-string => split/trim/dedupe/sort/join with newlines; anything else => malformed and never hashed (src/lib/schema.ts:143-144 @ b5c0507). Two verbs PRINT it and neither RETURNS it: `publish`'s printCard prints `allowed-tools: <normalized/MALFORMED>` (src/commands/publish.ts:214 @ b5c0507, beside `description:` at :212 -- the catalogue's :213/:211 were the 8bf12a8 numbers), and `install`'s `ensureConsent` prints the request (src/commands/install.ts:151 @ b5c0507; the malformed branch at :144). No Result carries grants: `LsSkill` (src/commands/ls.ts:18 @ b5c0507), `SearchHit` (src/commands/search.ts:15-19 @ b5c0507 -- its endorsement key is `endorsed`, :16, not `endorsement`), `InstalledResult` (src/commands/install.ts:33 @ b5c0507). `sync` re-derives them per skill at src/commands/sync.ts:185 @ b5c0507.
- **Field:** Two keys, three files. (1) src/commands/ls.ts:18 -> `export interface LsSkill { id: string; name: string; author: string; category: string; installs: number; latest: string; endorsement: string; grants: string / null; grantsHash: string / null; }`, filled at src/commands/ls.ts:60 from the `parsed` already in hand (src/commands/ls.ts:53): `grants: parsed.grants.ok ? parsed.grants.normalized : null, grantsHash: parsed.grants.ok ? parsed.grants.hash : null,`. (2) The same two keys on `SearchHit` (src/commands/search.ts:16), filled at src/commands/search.ts:58 from `skill.grants`. (3) REQUIRED OR THE BUILD BREAKS, and the coster's list omitted it: `format` is exported (src/commands/ls.ts:84) and `search` calls it with a seven-key object literal (src/commands/search.ts:60), so two new REQUIRED keys on `LsSkill` fail typecheck there -- extend that literal with `grants: hit.grants, grantsHash: hit.grantsHash`. No owner-file field, and nothing is written anywhere.
- **Verb or flag:** none
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: ls carries each skill's normalized grants and their hash; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: a malformed allowed-tools yields null grants and does not fail the listing; src/commands/__tests__/search.test.ts: search hits carry the same normalized grants as ls and still print the ls format
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 6, the `ls` paragraph (:343 @ b5c0507) and the `search` paragraph (:344 @ b5c0507)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** Section 6 addition to `ls` and `search`. Each carries, per skill, the normalized `allowed-tools` string and its sha256, alongside the fields it already carries. The value is `allowedTools()`'s output verbatim -- the same string `install`'s consent prompt and `publish`'s card print -- `none` when the skill declares no tools, and `null` when the declaration is malformed, which is the one case that is never hashed. Nothing new is computed and no printed line changes. The point is the hash: it is the key `config.approvals` is compared against, so a second consumer that re-normalizes a grant list on its own can reach a different digest and show 'approved' for a set the CLI considers unapproved. This carries the CLI's own answer instead.
- **Why SIMPLE:** Two keys on two in-memory Result types plus one call-site literal, all filled from values already in scope on the same lines. No owner-file field, no verb, no flag, no guard, no team.json write, no privacy call, no engine change, no sync/stamp interaction. Tests fit src/commands/__tests__; one paragraph to one section; one approver.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — Read `<clone>/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`, parse the YAML frontmatter, and re-implement `allowedTools()`'s normalization to build the chips.
- **Cites:** `src/lib/skills.ts:13 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/skills.ts:33 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:116 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:124-132 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:143-144 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:18 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:60 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:84 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/search.ts:16 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/search.ts:58 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/search.ts:60 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/publish.ts:214 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/install.ts:151 @ b5c0507`

#### RM-03 — Who-installed list per skill (the 'uses' popover names)  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** SkillDetailUsesHover, SkillDetail, SkillDetailHovers
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `readPeople(clone)` is exported and returns `Person[]` (src/lib/readme.ts:142 @ b5c0507); `Person.installed` is `installedSchema[]` = `{ id, version, scope, since }` (src/lib/schema.ts:36-41 @ b5c0507, on the person at :62) -- every field a popover row needs. `installCounts(people)` collapses that to a `Map<id, number>` and is documented as deliberately lossy under D38: 'the number is how many TEAMMATES hold the skill, not how many placements they hold' (src/lib/readme.ts:34-39 @ b5c0507, implemented at :40-44 with `new Set(person.installed.map(item => item.id))` at :42). `LsSkill.installs` is that count (src/commands/ls.ts:60 @ b5c0507) and `ls member <handle>` prints one person's installed list by name (src/commands/ls.ts:72 @ b5c0507), but nothing inverts the relation: no verb returns installer names per skill. `listSkills` already receives `people` (src/commands/ls.ts:49 @ b5c0507) and builds `counts` at :51. Two things the drawn avatars need are also absent from every Result this row touches: `LsResult.roster` is `{ handle, active }` only (src/commands/ls.ts:20 @ b5c0507, built at :34), and the only Result carrying a display name is `StatusResult.teams[].members: RosterEntry[]` (src/commands/status.ts:19 and :23 @ b5c0507, `RosterEntry = { handle, displayName }` at src/lib/skills.ts:66 @ b5c0507) -- a different verb -- while `readRoster` drops archived people entirely (src/lib/skills.ts:79 @ b5c0507), which is exactly the population D38 says the counts include.
- **Field:** Two edits. (i) src/lib/readme.ts, immediately after `installCounts` (:44): `export interface Installer { handle: string; displayName: string; scope: z.infer<typeof scopeSchema>; since: string; }` and `export function installersById(people: readonly Person[]): Map<string, Installer[]>` that pushes one entry per `person.installed` record -- one per RECORD, not per person, deliberately mirroring what `installCounts` collapses -- and sorts each list by `since` then handle. `displayName` is included because no Result the popover uses carries one (src/commands/ls.ts:20) and `readRoster` drops archived people (src/lib/skills.ts:79), so their avatars would otherwise be unrenderable. (ii) src/commands/ls.ts:18 -> add `installedBy?: readonly Installer[];` to `LsSkill`, filled at src/commands/ls.ts:60 from a `const installers = installersById(people)` built beside the existing `const counts = installCounts(people)` at :51. Declare it OPTIONAL: `format` is exported (src/commands/ls.ts:84) and `search` calls it with a seven-key object literal (src/commands/search.ts:60), so a required key fails typecheck there, and `search` has no installer map in scope (it reads people at src/commands/search.ts:37 and calls `installCounts` only at :53).
- **Verb or flag:** none
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/lib/__tests__/readme.test.ts: installersById lists every install record, including two scopes for one person, while installCounts still counts that person once; src/lib/__tests__/readme.test.ts: installersById carries display_name for an archived person that readRoster omits; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: ls carries installedBy per skill with handle, displayName, scope and since
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 6, the `ls` paragraph (:343 @ b5c0507)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** Section 6 addition to `ls`. `ls` carries, per skill, the install records that produced its install count: handle, display name, scope and `since`, oldest first. This is a re-projection of the same committed `people/*.json` entries `installCounts` already reads and introduces no new number. The two must not be confused, and D38 is unchanged: the COUNT is how many teammates hold the skill (deduped per person and id), while this LIST has one entry per install RECORD, so a teammate who installed the same skill globally and again into a project appears twice in the list and once in the count. Whether a departed teammate's historical install should still appear beside a live roster is not decided here -- that is TJ-13's question, and this field reports what the files say either way.
- **Why SIMPLE:** One exported helper placed next to the function it mirrors, plus one optional field on one Result, filled from data `listSkills` already has in scope. No owner-file field, no verb, no flag, no guard, no team.json write, no privacy call, no engine change, no sync/stamp interaction.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — Read every `<clone>/people/*.json` and group `installed[]` by `id`; take the header count from `LsSkill.installs` rather than from the grouped rows.

#### RM-07 — Approvals view joined with current grants (hash is not reversible)  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** SettingsMachine, SkillDetailQuality, SkillDetailNotInstalled, SettingsStates
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `config.approvals` is `z.record(z.string(), z.object({ grants: z.string(), approved_at: z.string() }).passthrough())` (src/lib/schema.ts:98 @ b5c0507) -- keyed by SKILL ID, the value a sha256 of the NORMALIZED grant string plus a date (hashed at src/lib/schema.ts:143-144 @ b5c0507). Written in exactly two places: `install`'s `ensureConsent` (src/commands/install.ts:153 @ b5c0507) and `sync`'s re-approval branch (src/commands/sync.ts:191 @ b5c0507). The hash is not reversible, so the chips must come from the skill's CURRENT declaration (`SkillRecord.grants`, src/lib/skills.ts:13 and :33 @ b5c0507), and since the join key is an id while the drawn label is a name it needs `skillRecords`/`findSkill` (src/lib/skills.ts:20 and :41 @ b5c0507). Spec section 5.4 says the same thing normatively: 'An entry authorizes exactly that grant set. Any placement whose computed hash differs from the stored one ... has no approval' (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:271 @ b5c0507; the field comment at :246). THE FRESHNESS PREDICATE EXISTS BUT IS COMMAND-PRIVATE: `function approved(config, id, grants)` at src/commands/sync.ts:299-301 @ b5c0507, called at :142 and :186; src/commands/install.ts:150 @ b5c0507 is a bare hash comparison reachable only after the malformed branch returns at :146 and the `none` branch returns at :148. The design's 'no way to revoke one yet' is TRUE at b5c0507: every `approvals` reference in src/commands and src/lib is src/commands/install.ts:150 and :153, src/commands/sync.ts:191 and :300, src/lib/schema.ts:98 and :104 -- no delete anywhere; only `ConfigStore.remove` drops the whole file (src/lib/config.ts:80-90 @ b5c0507).
- **Field:** none of its own -- the chips ride RM-02's `grants` / `grantsHash` on `LsSkill` (src/commands/ls.ts:18, filled :60) and the stored side is already readable through the exported `ConfigStore.read()` (src/lib/config.ts:13, :39-45).
- **Verb or flag:** One keyword, no call-site edits: add `export` to `function approved(config, id, grants)` at src/commands/sync.ts:299 @ b5c0507. There is precedent for a non-`run` export from a command module in the same code path (`export async function skillAtSource` at src/commands/install.ts:156 @ b5c0507). EXPLICITLY NO REVOKE VERB: the design discloses the absence in prose (build.py:3640) and draws no control, so none is implied. (If Ryan prefers the predicate in lib, moving it to src/lib/schema.ts beside `allowedTools` is the same verdict but costs three rewires -- src/commands/sync.ts:142, :186 and src/commands/install.ts:150 -- and the delta must then say that :150 omits the `none` branch only because :148 returns first, so the merge is behaviour-preserving.)
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: approved is true for a matching hash and for normalized 'none' with no stored entry, and false for a stale hash; src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: approved is false for malformed grants regardless of any stored approval; src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts: a malformed allowed-tools confirmed at the prompt still records no approval and leaves config.approvals untouched
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 5.4, the `approvals` paragraph (:246 and :271 @ b5c0507)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** Section 5.4 clarification. `config.approvals[<skill id>]` stores the sha256 of the grant string that was approved, not the grants themselves, so it can be compared but never displayed. Any surface that shows what was approved must therefore join the stored entry against the skill's CURRENT normalized `allowed-tools` and show the grants only when the hashes match; when they do not, the machine holds an approval for a set that no longer exists and the honest label is 'approval pending' -- the same condition that makes `sync` hold the skill and ask again. The comparison is exported from where it already lives so the display and the gate cannot diverge, and `none` -- a skill that normalizes to no tools -- is approved without any stored entry.
- **Why SIMPLE:** One `export` keyword over a three-line predicate that already exists, and no new field beyond RM-02's. No owner-file field, no verb, no flag, no guard, no team.json write, no privacy call, no engine change, no sync/stamp interaction. Tests extend the existing sync and install suites; one paragraph to one section; one approver.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — Read `config.approvals` (machine-local), read the skills' current grants, join on skill id, and compare `approvals[id].grants` against the current `grants.hash` BEFORE showing any chip as approved.

A caller that wants the comparison rather than the command already has it -- `createReleaseState` and `describeUpdate` are exported -- and MUST use those to render a version pair, because `update` itself forces a release probe and writes release state on every invocation.

#### RM-09 — Typed decline on Result (cancel is not an error)  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** SkillDetailRemove, SkillDetailRunEval, SkillDetailInstall, MarketplaceProjectInstall, SettingsLeave, SettingsPrune, ShareInvite
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `Result`'s failure branch is `{ ok: false; error: string; value?: T }` (src/lib/result.ts:3) and the only constructors are `success`/`failure` (src/lib/result.ts:5-6). A decline arrives as a plain failure carrying a human sentence and no discriminator. Confirm sites whose 'no' ends the verb: src/commands/connect.ts:200-202 ('Connect was declined.', thrown and wrapped as ConnectStepError phase 'consent', class at :39-44), src/commands/connect.ts:372 ('Forget was declined.'), src/commands/install.ts:145 and :152 ('Consent was declined ...'), src/commands/leave.ts:37-38, src/commands/publish.ts:84, src/commands/team.ts:121 and :493, src/commands/uninstallMachine.ts:58 (the one that already returns `failure(...)` instead of throwing). Each verb's top-level catch turns the throw into `failure(error.message)` (src/commands/connect.ts:155, src/commands/leave.ts:52), and src/lib/execute.ts:26-29 writes that string to stderr and sets exit 1 for declines and real failures alike. THREE decline models already exist and must not be re-costed or conflated: (1) `ConnectBatch.declined: string[]` returned inside `success(...)` (src/commands/connect.ts:35, filled at :82, returned at :151); (2) `publish`'s open-endorsement refusal returning `success({ ..., changed: false })` (src/commands/publish.ts:75-77); (3) `offerHook` returning the literal `'declined'` as an ordinary success value (src/lib/hook.ts:130, :134). Decisive for the app-side option: src/commands/connect.ts:148 returns `failure(message, batch.shared.length ? batch : undefined)` — a batch that recorded declines at :82 and then hit a later error returns on the FAILURE branch carrying those declines.
- **Field:** src/lib/result.ts:3 becomes `/ { ok: false; error: string; value?: T; cancelled?: true };` — one optional discriminator on the failure branch of a library type. NOT named `declined`: `personSchema` already declares `declined: z.array(skillIdSchema)` at src/lib/schema.ts:63, specified at .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:183 and :193 as the durable suppression list, written at src/commands/uninstall.ts:101-105 and cleared at src/commands/install.ts:135 — and the app renders both on the same screens.
- **Verb or flag:** none — no commander registration and no flag; src/cli.ts is untouched.
- **Guard:** none — src/lib/guard.ts untouched; no GuardAction, no PEOPLE_ACTIONS entry, no team.json row.
- **Tests:** src/lib/__tests__/result.test.ts: cancelled() carries cancelled: true and is otherwise a failure; failure() never sets it; src/commands/__tests__/connect.test.ts: 'leaves source, remote, and tracking untouched when the frontmatter confirmation is declined' (:427) also asserts result.cancelled === true; src/commands/__tests__/connect.test.ts: a batch that records a picker decline and then fails a later step returns ok:false WITHOUT cancelled (connect.ts:148), and its ConnectBatch.declined still lists the picker decline; src/commands/__tests__/leave.test.ts: 'does nothing when declined ...' (:58) also asserts result.cancelled === true; src/commands/__tests__/uninstallMachine.test.ts: 'cancels without writing when declined or the prompt channel is closed' (:67) asserts cancelled on the cancel case and NOT on the closed-prompt case; src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts: 'shows malformed allowed-tools verbatim, requires consent, and leaves no durable intent when declined' (:153) asserts cancelled; src/commands/__tests__/publish.test.ts: a push-policy publish declined at the confirm returns cancelled, not a bare failure; src/commands/__tests__/remove.test.ts: 'does not mutate the host when the confirmation is declined ...' (:114) asserts cancelled; src/lib/__tests__/execute.test.ts: a cancelled Result still writes its line to stderr and still exits 1 (the bin contract does not change)
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §3 — the 'Library-first (rev 7; ledger D23, now DECIDED)' paragraph at :59, which already names the phase-2 UI as the second caller of every run(args, io).
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** A verb's failing `Result` gains one optional discriminator, `cancelled?: true`, set only where the failure is a human answering no to a `Prompter.confirm` and nothing observable moved. It is spelled `cancelled`, not `declined`, because `declined` is already the committed suppression list on `people/<handle>.json` (§5.2) and the two appear on the same screens. It changes no CLI behaviour — a cancelled result is still one stderr line and exit code 1 — and exists for the second caller §3 already names: the phase-2 UI must tell 'you pressed Cancel' from 'the operation failed' without matching on error prose, which the `share`->`connect` rename has already shown to be unstable. `lib/result.ts` gains a `cancelled(error, value?)` constructor beside `failure`, and a `CancelledError` so a decline thrown inside a verb's body is re-tagged by that verb's existing top-level catch. `PromptClosedError` is never tagged — a closed channel is a real failure — so the flag is set at the confirm site, not in the catch. The three paths that already model a decline as data are unchanged: `ConnectBatch.declined`, `publish`'s open-endorsement refusal, and `offerHook`'s `'declined'` return; in particular `connect`'s batch failure return (which carries a partial batch) stays untagged, because a batch that also failed for another reason is not a cancellation.
- **Why SIMPLE:** No committed field, no verb, no flag, no guard, no team.json row, no privacy call, no engine change, no sync/stamp interaction. Tests slot into existing collocated files plus one new src/lib/__tests__/result.test.ts, which is the layout the spec mandates. One spec section, one approver. The change is broad but mechanical (one type line, one constructor, one error class, ~9 confirm sites across 7 command files) and the bar caps fields/verbs/guards/spec-sections/approvers, not files; the family brief names 'a new field on an existing Result' as SIMPLE explicitly. Two cautions recorded rather than hidden: (1) `install member/project` loops with a bare `await installOne(...)` and no error isolation (src/commands/install.ts:46 and :57), so one declined consent aborts a whole bulk install — the drawn 'Install 8 skills' dialog (build.py:2930) lands exactly there; batch isolation is a separate, uncosted gap. (2) RM-09 delivers nothing until RM-11 lands: src/lib/execute.ts:26-29 prints only the string and the flag is never rendered, so the sole consumer is an in-process importer.
- **App side:** MEDIUM (D20 ok) — Zero-CLI fallback: since the phase-2 UI supplies the Prompter itself (spec :59, src/lib/prompt.ts:14-20), the sidecar records whether its own confirm() answered false during this invocation and treats 'my confirm said no AND the verb returned ok:false' as a cancellation. The cruder variant is matching the failure string against the nine sentences.

#### RM-10 — Export the batched skillTrees reader (ls is O(skills) git spawns)  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** Library, LibraryLight, LibraryLoading, Marketplace, MarketplaceSkills, MarketplaceFull
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `listSkills` resolves each skill's version with one `latestTree` per skill at src/commands/ls.ts:59 — one `git rev-parse HEAD:skills/<name>` child process each (src/lib/readme.ts:119-123) — but it fans them out with `Promise.all(names.map(async ...))` at src/commands/ls.ts:52, so the children run concurrently: the catalogue's 'O(skills) git spawns' is true in COUNT and overstated in LATENCY. `search` batches eight at a time (src/commands/search.ts:51-52). The genuinely serial reader is `readReadmeData`'s `for` loop (src/lib/readme.ts:131-138): one `latestTree` at :136 PLUS one `latestReceiptVerdict` readdir+readFile at :137 (into :172-181), per skill. The batched reader already exists and resolves every skill tree in one `git ls-tree`: src/lib/teamRepo.ts:285-293, called internally at :152. It is an unexported `async function` whose first parameter is the module-private `type Git` (src/lib/teamRepo.ts:84), a closure built at :91 — so a literal `export` would leak that type. It is reached only inside the generic-git branch `!isGitHubRemote(remote) && options.action !== 'eval'` (src/lib/teamRepo.ts:148), i.e. on the GitHub remotes the product targets it runs today only on generic-git README paths. `regenerateReadmeInTree` already takes a pre-resolved map (src/lib/readme.ts:148 `latestBySkill?`, used at :164), so the batched shape is established; `readReadmeData` simply never got the parameter. NOT a blocker for the app: `latestTree` (src/lib/readme.ts:119) and `readReadmeData` (:126) are both exported today and `readReadmeData` already returns each skill's `latest` and eval verdict (:137), so the grid renders with zero CLI change. This row is a latency tune behind a skeleton the design already draws.
- **Field:** none
- **Verb or flag:** none — no commander registration and no flag; src/cli.ts untouched. One new exported function in src/lib/teamRepo.ts, beside the private reader: `export async function skillVersions(runner: Runner, clone: string, ref = 'HEAD'): Promise<Map<string, string>>`, which builds the same `(args) => runner.run('git', args, { cwd: clone })` closure the module builds at :91 and delegates to the private `skillTrees` at :285, so the module-private `type Git` (:84) never leaves the file and there is no second copy of the `^\d+\s+tree\s+([0-9a-f]{40})\t(.+)$` parser at :289.
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/lib/__tests__/teamRepo.test.ts: skillVersions resolves every skill tree from one git ls-tree and matches latestTree name by name; src/lib/__tests__/teamRepo.test.ts: skillVersions on a clone whose ref has no skills/ tree returns an empty map rather than throwing (the private reader throws through requireGitResult at :286 -> :297; the exported wrapper must not); src/lib/__tests__/teamRepo.test.ts: skillVersions surfaces a git failure with requireGitResult's text, `git ls-tree <ref>:skills failed: <stderr>` (teamRepo.ts:297) — which is NOT latestTree's text (readme.ts:121); src/lib/__tests__/teamRepo.test.ts: a name absent from the returned map is reported by the caller, not swallowed — the caller keeps ls's per-skill degradation (ls.ts:56-59) and search's all-failed escalation (search.ts:64-68)
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §3, the module map block at :34-53 — the `teamRepo.ts` line at :40.
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** `lib/teamRepo.ts` exports one additional read-only helper, `skillVersions(runner, clone, ref = 'HEAD'): Promise<Map<string, string>>`, which resolves every skill's tree hash from a single `git ls-tree <ref>:skills` — the batched reader `safeWrite` has used internally since §9's generic-git README path — and writes nothing. It differs from `latestTree` in two ways a caller must handle: its failure text is `git ls-tree ...:skills failed: <stderr>` rather than `Could not resolve the latest version of <name>: <stderr>`, and a skill git cannot resolve is simply ABSENT from the map rather than an error naming it. Callers therefore keep the §6 per-skill degradation ruling themselves — one unresolvable folder costs one row's version and one printed line, never the roster or the other rows (rulings walk R11/R12, 2026-09-06) — and `skillVersions` returns an empty map, never throws, when the ref carries no `skills/` tree. It exists because §3's second caller renders the whole catalogue at once, where one child process per skill is the dominant cost. No verb's behaviour or output changes.
- **Why SIMPLE:** An additive export beside an existing pure read: no field, no verb, no flag, no guard, no owner file, no §6.0 table row, no privacy call, no engine change, no sync or stamp interaction — `skillVersions` never fetches, matching the discipline `ls` states at src/commands/ls.ts:22 ('it deliberately neither pulls nor prompts'). One spec section, one approver, tests in the existing src/lib/__tests__/teamRepo.test.ts. Three obligations recorded rather than hidden, because they make this a NEW FUNCTION rather than an `export` keyword: the private symbol's first parameter is the module-private `Git` (:84); the private reader throws when `skills/` is absent from the ref (:286 -> :297) where a UI wants an empty map; and a map loses WHICH skill failed and WHY, which is exactly what ls.ts:56-59 and search.ts:64-68 encode. The optional second edit — giving `readReadmeData` the same `latestBySkill?` parameter `regenerateReadmeInTree` already takes (src/lib/readme.ts:148) — is a pure performance parameter with byte-identical output and can land separately; it is not part of the minimum. Alternative Ryan may prefer, recorded not recommended: host the batched reader in `lib/version.ts` instead, which §3's module map already designates 'tree-hash version resolution + cache checkout' (:43) and which src/lib/readme.ts:118's own comment says will absorb `latestTree` at M2 integration. That is better spec alignment and avoids the `Git` closure, at the cost of a second copy of the ls-tree parser.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The sidecar spawns `git ls-tree HEAD:skills` in the team clone itself and parses the same `^\d+\s+tree\s+([0-9a-f]{40})\t(.+)$` lines the CLI parses at src/lib/teamRepo.ts:289. Or, with zero work at all, it calls the already-exported `readReadmeData` (src/lib/readme.ts:126), which returns every skill's `latest` and eval verdict today.

#### RM-11 — Packaging for in-process import: src/lib-entry.ts barrel, exports map, declaration: true, tarball CI assertion  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** 
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `package.json` declares `"type": "module"` (:6), `"bin"` (:14-16) and `"files": ["dist", "LICENSE", "NOTICE", "README.md"]` (:17-22) — and NO `main`, NO `exports`, NO `types`. `tsconfig.build.json:4` emits `{ noEmit: false, outDir: "dist", rootDir: "src", sourceMap: true }` with NO `declaration`, so no `.d.ts` is produced or shipped. `src/index.ts` cannot serve as the library entry: it installs stream handlers at :23-28 (the `outputClosed` promise is :22) and runs `await buildProgram(execute, undefined, {...}).parseAsync()` at :49 — it parses argv on import. The verbs are already library-shaped: every `src/commands/<verb>.ts` exports `run(args, io)`, `src/cli.ts:33` takes an injectable `CliVerbs` map, and `src/commands/` holds exactly 20 verb modules at b5c0507 (receiptCheck landed at 4149287; the hidden `receipt-check` is registered at src/cli.ts:115). §3 states the obligation outright at .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:59, ledger D21/D23 at .planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:134 and :136. CI already packs and inspects the tarball, asserting `dist/index.js` is present and `src/`/`__tests__` are absent (.github/workflows/ci.yml:56-63) with a foreign-project install-and-run smoke that invokes the bin BY PATH at :67. `src/lib/teamRepo.ts:365-367` resolves `../index.js` from the built `dist/lib/` at runtime, so `dist/index.js` must keep shipping beside `dist/lib/` under any entry story. CORRECTION to the coster's citations: `isolatedModules: true` is tsconfig.json:11, not :10 (:10 is `esModuleInterop`); the stream handler loop is src/index.ts:23-28, not :22-27.
- **Field:** No zod schema changes — the manifest keys are packaging. Concretely: `package.json` gains `"types": "./dist/lib-entry.d.ts"` and `"exports": { ".": { "types": "./dist/lib-entry.d.ts", "default": "./dist/lib-entry.js" }, "./cli": "./dist/index.js", "./package.json": "./package.json" }`; tsconfig.build.json:4 gains `"declaration": true` (NOT `declarationMap`: the maps would point at `../src/**`, which .github/workflows/ci.yml:61 refuses to ship, so they would be dead weight in the tarball).
- **Verb or flag:** none — no commander change. One new source file, `src/lib-entry.ts`, a barrel re-exporting `run` from all 20 `commands/<verb>.ts` modules under their verb names plus the lib modules; it must alias rather than star-export because `readme.ts` exists in BOTH src/lib/ and src/commands/, and every interface re-export must be `export type { ... }` because tsconfig.json:11 sets `isolatedModules: true`.
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/__tests__/bin.test.ts: the built library entry (dist/lib-entry.js) imports without parsing argv and exposes run for all 20 verbs — extends the existing 'the built bin (dist/index.js)' describe (:24), which already compiles into a scratch dist at :33 and never touches the repo's own dist/; src/__tests__/bin.test.ts: importing the barrel installs no process stream handlers and sets no process.exitCode (the src/index.ts:23-28 loop and :49 must stay unreachable from the barrel); src/__tests__/release-plan.test.ts: the package.json exports map resolves '.', './cli' and './package.json' to files the build emits, and no other subpath resolves; src/__tests__/release-plan.test.ts: dist/index.js still ships beside dist/lib/, so src/lib/teamRepo.ts:365-367's runtime '../index.js' resolution survives the exports map
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §3 — the 'Library-first (rev 7; ledger D23, now DECIDED)' paragraph at :59, and the module map block at :31-57.
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** §3's library-first obligation gains its packaging half. `src/lib-entry.ts` is the package's library entry: a barrel that re-exports `run` from all twenty `commands/<verb>.ts` modules under their verb names, plus the read-model and seam modules — `lib/{auth,community,config,constants,execute,fs,guard,hook,invocation,launch,local-skills,package,placer,prompt,readme,remote,result,runner,schema,skill-source,skills,teamRepo,update,version}.js` and `lib/evals/{hygiene,receipt,results}.js`. It is deliberately not `index.ts`, which installs stream handlers and parses argv on import. `execute.ts` is exported on purpose: it is the documented Result -> stderr/exit-code mapping, i.e. the piece a second caller must be able to SEE in order not to reuse it. `package.json` gains `types` and an `exports` map with `.` -> the barrel, `./cli` -> the bin, and `./package.json`; `tsconfig.build.json` gains `declaration` so the types ship inside the existing `files: ["dist"]`. The CI package job's must-have list gains `dist/lib-entry.js` and `dist/lib-entry.d.ts` so the library entry cannot silently stop shipping, and keeps asserting `dist/index.js`, which `lib/teamRepo.ts`'s push-guard launcher resolves at runtime. Adding `exports` closes deep imports into `dist/` that a consumer can reach today (a package with no `exports` permits every subpath); nothing known consumes them, and this is the deliberate cost of a curated surface. No verb's behaviour changes and nothing in `src/` imports `src/index.ts`, so the barrel introduces no cycle.
- **Why SIMPLE:** Additive throughout: no owner-file field, no verb, no flag, no guard, no team.json row, no privacy call, no engine change, no sync/stamp interaction; tests extend the two existing top-level suites; one spec section; one approver. Verified non-breakages at the pin: (1) `bin` resolution is by path and CI's smoke runs the bin by path (.github/workflows/ci.yml:67), so the bin is unaffected; (2) src/lib/package.ts:8 reads the manifest with a RELATIVE `createRequire(...)('../../package.json')`, which an exports map does not gate, and `"./package.json"` is exported anyway; (3) `files: ["dist", ...]` already ships whatever dist/ contains, so `.d.ts` files ship with no further manifest change; (4) src/__tests__/bin.test.ts:33 already builds the scratch dist with `--declarationMap false`, so declaration emit does not disturb the existing bin suite. TWO honest cautions rather than four claimed non-breakages: the coster's justification 'nothing is taken away' is FALSE — a package with no `exports` field permits EVERY subpath, so `import 'terum-skills/dist/lib/skills.js'` resolves today against exactly the files package.json:17-22 ships, and the map closes all of them; the honest statement is 'it closes deep imports nothing known consumes'. And `declaration: true` under `strict` + `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` (tsconfig.json:8-9) can fail the build with TS4023/TS2742-class errors wherever an exported function's inferred type names a non-exported type — and this codebase has exactly that shape (`type Git`, src/lib/teamRepo.ts:84; `ConnectStepError`, src/commands/connect.ts:39). This session may not run npm build, so that is a NAMED BUILD RISK, not a verified non-breakage; `npx tsc -p tsconfig.build.json --declaration --emitDeclarationOnly --outDir <scratch>` in a throwaway clone settles it. Alternative Ryan may prefer, recorded not recommended: a subpath-wildcard exports map (`"./lib/*"`, `"./commands/*"`, `"./cli"`, `"./package.json"`) deletes the barrel — two edits, no new source file, no isolatedModules `export type` discipline and no readme.ts collision — at the cost of making every module under dist/lib/ semver-visible from day one and leaving no single entry for CI to assert.
- **App side:** MEDIUM (D20 ok) — Skip packaging entirely — the sidecar either vendors the CLI source into the app build, or drives the published binary over a JSON-lines stdio protocol.
- **Cites:** `package.json:6 @ b5c0507`, `package.json:14-16 @ b5c0507`, `package.json:17-22 @ b5c0507`, `tsconfig.json:8-9 @ b5c0507`, `tsconfig.json:11 @ b5c0507`, `tsconfig.build.json:4 @ b5c0507`, `src/index.ts:22 @ b5c0507`, `src/index.ts:23-28 @ b5c0507`, `src/index.ts:49 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:33 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:115 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/package.ts:8 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/teamRepo.ts:84 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/teamRepo.ts:365-367 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/connect.ts:39 @ b5c0507`, `src/__tests__/bin.test.ts:24 @ b5c0507`, `src/__tests__/bin.test.ts:33 @ b5c0507`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:56-63 @ b5c0507`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:61 @ b5c0507`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:67 @ b5c0507`, `eslint.config.js:16 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/prompt.ts:14-20 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/connect.ts:107-113 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:59 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:134 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:136 @ b5c0507`

#### RM-18 — print-only information not on the Result (search partial failures, install exclude warning, interactive sync failure payload)  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** OnboardingError, MarketplaceError, LibraryError, ShareError, InboxError, SettingsSync
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** Three distinct defects at b5c0507, and the catalogue's sync half was backwards at 8bf12a8. (1) sync's failure paths drop the whole payload interactively. notice() pushes to notices[] in EVERY mode and additionally prints when !args.hook (src/commands/sync.ts:71 @ b5c0507), so notices are always collected - but both failure returns attach the SyncResult only when args.hook: `failure(\`Sync finished with N team(s) skipped…\`, args.hook ? {…} : undefined)` (src/commands/sync.ts:293 @ b5c0507) and the outer catch (src/commands/sync.ts:295 @ b5c0507). An interactive sync that could not fetch one team returns ok:false with no placed, no deferred, no notices - exactly what build.py:4044-4050 needs. Only the lock-skip line is genuinely hook-suppressed (src/commands/sync.ts:109 @ b5c0507). (2) install drops the placer's notices. place() returns { path, snapshot, notices } (src/lib/placer.ts:51 @ b5c0507) carrying the stranded-previous-copy line (:79) and the exclude-file warning (:81); installOne prints them (src/commands/install.ts:125 @ b5c0507) and the quarantine-drift line (:119), then returns InstalledResult = { id, team, path, version } (src/commands/install.ts:33 @ b5c0507 - NOT :32, both refuters caught this), constructed at :139. SCOPE NARROWING (refuter A, verified): the exclude warning is project-scope only - placer.ts:80 pushes it only when options.projectRoot is set, and install.ts:121 passes projectRoot only for scope.kind === 'project' (src/commands/install.ts:102 @ b5c0507) - so it can never fire on the drawn global-install boards. (3) search partial failures are print-only. failures[] (src/commands/search.ts:30 @ b5c0507) collects per-team errors (:68, :71) but becomes a failure() only when EVERY configured team failed (:76); a mixed run returns ok:true with the failures printed at :72-73 and gone. Per-skill record problems (:35) and per-person read failures (:37) are printed and discarded. CLOSED sub-flip, confirmed by both refuters: the per-skill VERSION half is already on the Result - SearchHit.unresolved (src/commands/search.ts:16-19 @ b5c0507, doc comment at :17: 'a UI greys out Install from this') - and predates both pins.
- **Field:** No owner-file field and no zod line - nothing is persisted. Three Result-shape edits: (a) delete the `args.hook ?` ternary at src/commands/sync.ts:293 and :295 so both read `failure(msg, { placed, deferred, notices, changed, hook: Boolean(args.hook) })`; (b) `export interface InstalledResult { id: string; team: string; path: string; version: string / null; notices: string[]; }` at src/commands/install.ts:33, fed from placed.notices at src/commands/install.ts:125 and the drift line at :119; (c) `export interface SearchResult { hits: SearchHit[]; warnings: string[]; }` with `run(args, io): Promise<Result<SearchResult>>` at src/commands/search.ts:22, warnings fed from the existing failures[] array.
- **Verb or flag:** none - no commander registration changes (src/cli.ts:122, :125, :126 @ b5c0507 unchanged).
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: an interactive sync that cannot fetch one team returns ok:false carrying placed, deferred and notices, and the --hook path is byte-identical; src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts: a project install whose .git/info/exclude write fails returns the placer notice on InstalledResult as well as printing it; src/commands/__tests__/search.test.ts: a mixed run returns ok:true with the unsearchable team on warnings, and an all-teams failure is still a failure Result
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 6 Command behavior (the heading at :279), one paragraph covering all three verbs
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** Every verb's Result carries everything the run learned, on BOTH outcomes. A verb that degrades - one team unreachable, one person file unreadable, one exclude line unwritten - records that on the value it returns and does not rely on print to have carried it, because the phase-2 UI (section 3) supplies a Prompter whose print is a channel to a human, not a wire format. Concretely: sync attaches its SyncResult on the failure path in EVERY mode, not only --hook (the hook discriminant on the value stays accurate, so the bin's hook-notice routing in lib/execute.ts is unaffected); install's InstalledResult gains notices: string[], the placer lines it prints today; and search returns { hits, warnings }, where warnings holds the per-team degradations it currently only prints. The two verbs deliberately keep DIFFERENT outcome shapes and the difference is the point: a mixed search stays ok:true, because a partial catalogue is a usable answer and section 6's exit gate should not fail a run that found skills, whereas a partial install is ok:false with its successes on the value, because placements already written are durable state a script must not read as clean. Printed output is unchanged in all three cases - this adds to the value, it moves nothing off the terminal.
- **Why SIMPLE:** No owner file, no schema, no guard, no new verb, no new flag; three additive Result edits, one spec section, one approver, and each is independently landable. Two hazards checked and clear. (i) Double-printing: isHookSync gates on value.hook === true AND two array fields (src/lib/execute.ts:41-42 @ b5c0507) before writeHookNotices (:45-47), and the interactive path sets hook:false, so attaching the value interactively cannot double-print. (ii) Ripple: installOne's return value is discarded at every non-test call site (src/commands/sync.ts:143, :268, src/commands/team.ts, and inside install.ts), and SearchHit has no consumer outside search.ts - search's only caller is src/cli.ts:126 @ b5c0507, which ignores the value. Refuter B's objection that RM-18 and RM-19 pick opposite outcome shapes for 'some items degraded' is UPHELD as a completeness defect and is now answered inside the delta rather than left implicit; search's choice is the correct one and the deliberate exit-0-on-mixed-run comment at src/commands/search.ts:64-68 @ b5c0507 is the reason.
- **App side:** FORBIDDEN (D20 no) — The only app-side alternative is to scrape Prompter.print lines and regex them back into structure - reconstructing 'Skipping <team>: could not fetch …' (src/commands/sync.ts:120 @ b5c0507), 'Placed … but could not add .claude/skills/<name> to .git/info/exclude: …' (src/lib/placer.ts:81 @ b5c0507) and '<team> could not be searched: …' (src/commands/search.ts:73 @ b5c0507) into fields.

#### RM-19 — Batch verbs without error isolation (install member / project)  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** MarketplaceProjectInstall, MarketplaceProjectNotInstalled, MarketplacePersonNotInstalled, MarketplaceStates
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** install --kind member awaits installOne in a bare for loop with no try (src/commands/install.ts:45-48 @ b5c0507, the call at :46, iterating person.installed at :45); install --kind project does the same on one line (src/commands/install.ts:57 @ b5c0507). The first throw - a foreign folder at a target without --force (src/commands/install.ts:112 @ b5c0507), a busy target lock, a declined consent (src/commands/install.ts:145 and :152 @ b5c0507) - escapes to the verb's outer catch (src/commands/install.ts:74 @ b5c0507), which returns failure(message) WITH NO VALUE, so the caller loses even the items that already placed. Those items are not rolled back: each successful installOne has already written config.placements (src/commands/install.ts:122-124 @ b5c0507) and committed a people-file entry through safeWrite (src/commands/install.ts:128-137 @ b5c0507). sync solves exactly this problem, in the same file family, six lines long, with the rethrow that matters: `for (const skill of candidates) { try { await installOne(…); placed++; } catch (error) { if (error instanceof PromptClosedError) throw error; defer(team, skill.name); notice(…); } }` (src/commands/sync.ts:267-273 @ b5c0507, the rethrow at :270). The partial-payload slot already exists: failure<T>(error, value?) returns { ok:false, error, value } (src/lib/result.ts:1-3, :6 @ b5c0507) and src/commands/sync.ts:293 already uses that shape.
- **Field:** none - no zod line, no persisted field. Control flow plus reuse of the existing Result third slot (src/lib/result.ts:6 @ b5c0507). Wrap the loop bodies at src/commands/install.ts:45-48 and :57 in try/catch, collect per-item errors, and return `results.length ? failure(msg, results) : failure(msg)`.
- **Verb or flag:** none - `program.command('install <ref> [value]')` stays exactly as registered at src/cli.ts:122 @ b5c0507.
- **Guard:** none - verified, no guard change is hidden here: 'install' is already in PEOPLE_ACTIONS (src/lib/guard.ts:36 @ b5c0507) and row (b) at src/lib/guard.ts:49 permits people/<handle>.json; nothing touches team.json (rows c/d/e at :50 are untouched).
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts: install member isolates one failing candidate - the others land, the failure names it, and the successes ride the failure Result; src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts: install project reports every failing skill by name and still returns the placed ones; src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts: a closed prompt channel during a batch aborts the batch rather than being isolated into N identical failures; src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts: a safeWrite deadline exhaustion aborts the batch instead of retrying to deadline once per remaining item; src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts: an isolated batch leaves one config.pending entry per failed item, and a following sync clears them
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 6 Command behavior (the heading at :279), the install entry
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** install member <handle> and install project <name> isolate per item, exactly as sync's endorsed batch already does. One skill that cannot be placed - a foreign folder at its target, a busy target lock, a declined tool grant - is reported by name and the batch continues; the Result carries every skill that did place. When at least one item failed the Result is ok:false, so the exit code still tells a script the batch was not clean, and its value carries the successful InstalledResults, because those placements are already durable in config.placements and in the people file and must never be reported as not having happened. TWO ERROR CLASSES ARE BATCH-FATAL AND ARE RETHROWN RATHER THAN ISOLATED. PromptClosedError: a channel that is gone is not a per-item failure. And a remote-access or safeWrite-exhaustion failure: safeWrite retries with backoff to a 30-second deadline before throwing, so isolating it would turn one unreachable remote into one retry-to-deadline loop per remaining item - eight of them behind the drawn 'Install 8 skills' button. A CONSEQUENCE THE IMPLEMENTER MUST EXPECT: an isolated batch can leave one config.pending entry per failed item, where today a single throw leaves one. That is correct and not a leak - pending is written before placement and cleared only after the people-file write, sync replays pending, and a team with any pending entry is deliberately left unstamped so the next session retries - but it means an isolated batch changes what the next sync --hook does, and that is intended.
- **Why SIMPLE:** No owner file, no schema, no guard, no verb, no flag. A within-verb control-flow change copying a pattern already in the codebase (src/commands/sync.ts:267-273 @ b5c0507), tests in the existing src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts, one spec paragraph, one approver. Both completeness gaps the refuters found are folded into the one paragraph, so it stays one paragraph and one section: refuter A's second batch-fatal class (SafeWriteExhausted at src/lib/teamRepo.ts:178 @ b5c0507, with DEFAULT_DEADLINE_MS = 30_000 at src/lib/teamRepo.ts:73 @ b5c0507 - I verified the number, so the worst case behind an 8-skill button really is about four minutes) and refuter B's pending-residue consequence (written at src/commands/install.ts:92, cleared at :138, replayed by sync, and gating the stamp at src/commands/sync.ts:290 @ b5c0507).
- **App side:** MEDIUM (D20 ok) — The app could refuse the batch verbs and loop `install <ref>` itself once per skill, isolating in its own code - the ids come from team.json.projects[name].skills (src/lib/schema.ts:48 @ b5c0507) or from readPerson(clone, handle).installed (src/lib/skills.ts:64 @ b5c0507), both readable.
- **Cites:** `src/commands/install.ts:45-48 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/install.ts:57 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/install.ts:74 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/install.ts:92 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/install.ts:112 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/install.ts:122-124 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/install.ts:128-137 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/install.ts:138 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/install.ts:145 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/install.ts:152 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:267-273 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:290 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:293 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/result.ts:1-3 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/result.ts:6 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/teamRepo.ts:73 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/teamRepo.ts:178 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/guard.ts:36 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/guard.ts:49 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:122 @ b5c0507`

#### RM-21 — Windows home derivation (placementHome checks a POSIX suffix)  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** OnboardingBoot, OnboardingStates, SettingsMachine
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** placementHome(store) decides where a GLOBAL placement goes and does it with a hard-coded POSIX suffix: `function placementHome(store: ConfigStore): string { return store.root.endsWith('/.terum/skills') ? dirname(dirname(store.root)) : store.root; }` (src/commands/install.ts:238 @ b5c0507). It feeds `resolveTarget('claude-code', scope, repoRoot, input.home ?? placementHome(input.store))` (src/commands/install.ts:103 @ b5c0507), whose global arm is platform-correct because it uses join: `global: (home) => join(home, '.claude', 'skills')` (src/lib/placer/agent-paths.ts:11 @ b5c0507, via src/lib/placer.ts:20-26 @ b5c0507). The default store root is `join(homedir(), '.terum', 'skills')` (src/lib/config.ts:37 @ b5c0507), which on Windows is C:\Users\x\.terum\skills and does NOT end with '/.terum/skills'. So the ternary falls through, home becomes the store root, and every global placement lands at C:\Users\x\.terum\skills\.claude\skills - a directory Claude Code never reads. It is the only such derivation in the product. THE APP CANNOT ROUTE AROUND IT: InstallArgs.home exists (src/commands/install.ts:27 @ b5c0507) and installOne takes home (src/commands/install.ts:78 @ b5c0507), so an explicit install can pass one - but SyncArgs has NO home field (src/commands/sync.ts:24-31 @ b5c0507) and the endorsed batch calls installOne({ team, id, store, runner, cwd }) with none (src/commands/sync.ts:268 @ b5c0507). sync is the main placement path on a fresh machine and is exactly what OnboardingBoot draws.
- **Field:** none - no zod line, no interface member, no new option, nothing persisted. One function body replaced in place at src/commands/install.ts:238, using the path primitives already imported at src/commands/install.ts:4 (`import { basename, dirname, join } from 'node:path'`): `function placementHome(store: ConfigStore): string { const parent = dirname(store.root); return basename(store.root) === 'skills' && basename(parent) === '.terum' ? dirname(parent) : store.root; }`
- **Verb or flag:** none
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts: placementHome resolves a <home>/.terum/skills root to <home> and leaves any other root alone, asserted on both basename predicates rather than on a separator literal; src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts: a global placement resolves under the derived home for a Windows-shaped root (a fixture root built with the win32 separator), so the branch is exercised on this CI; src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: the endorsed batch places under the same home an explicit install uses (the existing assertion at :334 stays green unmodified)
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 4.3 Placement targets (the heading at :136)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** The global placement root is derived from the store root with path primitives, never by matching a POSIX separator. placementHome walks up from <root> only when its last two segments are literally '.terum' and 'skills', using basename and dirname, which are separator-correct on every platform - the same reason join at the agent-paths table is correct. This replaces a suffix test, store.root.endsWith('/.terum/skills'), that could never match on Windows, where the default root is C:\Users\<name>\.terum\skills: the test fell through, the store root itself was used as the home, and every global placement landed at <store root>\.claude\skills, a directory Claude Code never reads. Behaviour on POSIX is unchanged for every root shape the suite already pins. Nothing is stored and nothing is derived from this process's HOME - re-resolving a global path from the environment is the failure section 7's orphan pass already refuses.
- **Why SIMPLE:** No owner-file field, no verb, no flag, no guard, no schema change, no new import; one function body and one new unit test, in the existing layout. I REJECTED THE COSTER'S SHAPE ON VERIFIED TEST EVIDENCE, which both refuters found independently and I re-opened myself. Its `ConfigStore.home = options.home ?? homedir()` would change the meaning of every createConfigStore call that reaches a global placement without an explicit home - 25 non-test and 201 test call sites at b5c0507 - and two existing tests pin the current derivation and pass no home: src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts:124-125 roots a store at <fixture>/home/.terum/skills and asserts the placement at join(home,'.claude','skills','sample') (:134-135) with no home argument at :132, exercising the dirname(dirname()) branch; src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts:329-334, whose own comment reads "sync's endorsed batch places under the store's own home", asserts join(store.root,'.claude','skills','second') and exercises the fallthrough branch. Under the coster's shape both would write into the developer's real $HOME/.claude/skills. That is not 'tests that fit the existing vitest layout' (condition 4) and it is a real-filesystem hazard. THE BASENAME FIX PRESERVES BOTH, and I checked the fixture roots rather than assuming: sync.test.ts:29 builds its store at join(fixture.root, 'state') (basename 'state', so fallthrough, unchanged) and src/lib/__tests__/config.test.ts:10 at join(tmp, 'skills') (basename 'skills' but parent is not '.terum', so fallthrough, unchanged). The coster rejected the cheap path for the wrong reason - its objection ('cannot be proven on this CI') applies to the endsWith(join(sep,…)) variant, which contains a separator literal; a basename/dirname predicate contains none and IS testable here.
- **App side:** MEDIUM (D20 ok) — Pass home: os.homedir() on every install call the app makes (InstallArgs.home exists at src/commands/install.ts:27 @ b5c0507).

#### RM-22 — Zero-team state and missing-tool detection (git, gh, claude) as first-class data  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** OnboardingBoot, OnboardingError, SettingsAccount, SettingsEvals, LibraryEmpty, MarketplaceNoResults, ShareEmpty, InboxEmpty
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** Two of the three named halves are better than the reports claim; the third is a genuine hole. (1) ZERO TEAM IS ALREADY FIRST-CLASS DATA - CLOSED. status with no configured team takes selected = Object.entries(config.teams) = [] (src/commands/status.ts:33 @ b5c0507), prints getStartedLines (:34), never enters the loop, leaves `lines` empty and returns success({ version, teams: [] }) (src/commands/status.ts:100 @ b5c0507). A missing config.json is not an error either: read() returns emptyConfig() on ENOENT (src/lib/config.ts:42 @ b5c0507, shape at src/lib/schema.ts:104 @ b5c0507). So the app's first-run predicate is a structured ok:true Result with teams: []. selectTeam's three throw strings remain for verbs that need a team (src/lib/config.ts:25 unknown, :28 zero teams, :29 ambiguous). (2) gh IS PROBED AND EXPORTED - the STATES half is CLOSED. ghState(runner) runs `gh --version`, then `gh auth status` through the private ghAuthenticated, and returns { installed, authenticated }; ghState is exported, ghAuthenticated is not (src/lib/auth.ts:21-29, :31-33 @ b5c0507; GhState at src/lib/auth.ts:16 @ b5c0507). The three drawn states map onto it exactly and the friendly copy for the two bad ones already exists (src/lib/auth.ts:135 and :136 @ b5c0507 - the coster's ':134-135' cites the signature and omits the second state). A which-style probe would be strictly worse: it cannot see SIGNED OUT. (3) The agent CLI is probed, exported, but only inside eval's preflight: `--version` at src/lib/evals/agent.ts:211 then one real agent task at :219, with distinct messages at :213 and :221, exported at src/lib/evals/agent.ts:208 @ b5c0507. (4) GIT IS NEVER PROBED - THE REAL HOLE. `grep -rn "'--version'" src --include=*.ts` outside tests returns exactly two sites, src/lib/auth.ts:23 and src/lib/evals/agent.ts:211. Runner.run types the command as 'git' / 'gh' (src/lib/runner.ts:9 @ b5c0507) and systemRunner rejects on the child's error event, so a machine without git gets a raw `spawn git ENOENT` string through each verb's outer catch. One partial mitigation exists and is a hint, not a signal: status prints 'check that git is installed before repairing anything' (src/commands/status.ts:55 @ b5c0507) alongside describeClone's { state:'incomplete', reason:'unverifiable' } (src/lib/teamRepo.ts:427 type, :434 constructed, :445 exported @ b5c0507). (5) hints.ts NO LONGER EXISTS (deleted at 1d5e3f9); its copy is the exported getStartedLines (src/lib/invocation.ts:53-58 @ b5c0507), used at src/commands/status.ts:34 and src/cli.ts:44 (NOT :46 - both refuters caught this).
- **Field:** none - no zod line, nothing persisted. One new export beside ghState, in the same file and the same shape, immediately after src/lib/auth.ts:29: `export async function gitState(runner: Runner = systemRunner): Promise<{ installed: boolean }> { try { return { installed: (await runner.run('git', ['--version'])).code === 0 }; } catch { return { installed: false }; } }`
- **Verb or flag:** none - no commander registration and no flag. The app calls the exported function through the in-process seam (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:59 @ b5c0507). A `doctor` verb is deliberately NOT proposed: that would be a second row and a second spec section.
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/lib/__tests__/auth.test.ts: gitState reports installed when git --version exits 0; src/lib/__tests__/auth.test.ts: gitState reports not installed on a non-zero exit and on a spawn error, and never throws
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 6.1 setup (the heading at :373), where the read-only GitHub preflight already lives
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** Setup and the phase-2 UI can ask, before any question, whether the two tools the product shells out to are present. gh is already answerable through the exported ghState (installed / authenticated). git gains the same treatment: an exported gitState that runs `git --version` through the injected Runner and reports installed, returning false rather than throwing on a spawn error. It is a read-only probe - it starts nothing, writes nothing, is not a verb, and is not a readiness test for anything else - and it exists so that a missing binary is a first-class state with its own copy, instead of a raw `spawn git ENOENT` surfacing through an unrelated verb's failure string. The zero-team state needs no addition: status already returns ok:true with an empty teams list on a machine that has joined nothing, and a missing config.json reads as an empty config rather than an error.
- **Why SIMPLE:** One export in one file: no owner file, no schema, no verb, no flag, no guard, tests in the existing src/lib/__tests__/auth.test.ts, one spec paragraph, one approver. SCOPE DISCIPLINE, STATED TWICE. (i) The agent-CLI probe split lives in src/lib/evals/agent.ts:208-223 @ b5c0507, which makes Ajay a second approver and breaks condition (6); RM-27 already owns 'lift the agent-CLI --version probe out of preflight', so it is not costed twice here. (ii) The two VALUES drawn beside the gh states - 'Signed in as {login}' and 'gh {version}' (build.py:3536-3537) - are RM-27's, whose corrections entry names them in terms ('The tools block must return a version and a login, not two booleans'). Refuter A is right that they are not on GhState and that the coster's 'the three drawn states map onto ghState exactly' understates the row; refuter A is WRONG that neither row owns them. I also checked the login and it is cheaper than refuter A assumed: it needs no scrape of `gh auth status`, because collectIdentity already fetches it with `gh api user -q .login` when config carries none and persists it as config.github (src/lib/auth.ts:68-71 @ b5c0507, schema.ts:95 @ b5c0507, written through login at src/commands/login.ts:22-23 @ b5c0507) - so the drawn login is a config read. The VERSION genuinely has no producer: ghState reads `gh --version` and keeps only version.code, discarding stdout (src/lib/auth.ts:23-24 @ b5c0507). One line on GhState would fix it; it belongs to RM-27.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — For gh states: import the exported ghState (src/lib/auth.ts:21 @ b5c0507) and render the three drawn states directly (build.py:3533-3544). For the drawn login: read config.github (src/lib/schema.ts:95 @ b5c0507) through the exported ConfigStore.read (src/lib/config.ts:11-20, :37 @ b5c0507) - it is also what the sidebar footer draws (build.py:267). For zero team: read status()'s teams: [] (src/commands/status.ts:100 @ b5c0507). For git: nothing exists to call; that third is the CLI export above.
