#### RM-27 — gh sign in / sign out / install and the agent-CLI state: no verb wraps the lifecycle, and the only login path needs a real TTY  *(recommended: both; approver: none (no CLI change). The optional halves would need TWO: Ryan for §6 `login`/§13 default 38 (:553) and Ajay for eval-engine §7.4 (:372) — which is exactly why they are not this row's price.)*

- **Boards:** Settings, SettingsStates, SettingsEvals
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** The three STATES are a free probe; the two VALUES beside them and the three BUTTONS are not — and nothing surfaces any of it to a caller. `ghState(runner)` returns `{ installed, authenticated }` (type at src/lib/auth.ts:16, body :21-29) and maps one-to-one onto the drawn three; `ghAuthenticated` is `(await runner.run('gh', ['auth','status'])).code === 0` (src/lib/auth.ts:31-33). Per correction RM-27, `ghState` runs `gh --version` and reads ONLY the exit code, discarding stdout (src/lib/auth.ts:23-24), so the drawn `gh 2.76.1` has no producer; and the login is not on `GhState` either — it is fetched inside `collectIdentity` only when config carries none (`gh api user -q .login`, src/lib/auth.ts:70-71) and persisted as `config.github` (src/lib/schema.ts:95). `LoginResult` is `{ gh: GhState; handle: string }` (src/commands/login.ts:15), and `login` prompts and WRITES config (src/commands/login.ts:22-23), so it is not usable as a read. No other verb carries gh state: `StatusResult` is `{ version, teams }` (src/commands/status.ts:23), and §6 forbids adding it — status is “offline ... only one `git remote get-url origin` probe per readable team, no host access” (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:345), asserted in §12 (:496). The ACTION half does not exist: `detectOrOfferGh` returns early unless `io.interactive` (src/lib/auth.ts:42) then hands the process's own stdio to a child (src/lib/auth.ts:44), and §3 says the phase-2 channel sets `interactive` true (:59), so from a window the offer fires mid-verb into a stdio no human sees; no `gh auth logout` path exists anywhere in src/; and “Install gh” would run a package manager, which the product refuses on principle (src/commands/uninstallMachine.ts:136-137 prints the step and never runs it). The agent CLI is probed only inside eval's PAID preflight: `spawnCollect(['--version'])` at src/lib/evals/agent.ts:211 with `ccVersion` kept at :216, then a real one-turn agent task at :219 whose failure says “check that `claude` is logged in” (:221) — so “signed in” is inferred from a paid task succeeding.
- **Field:** none is required. OPTIONAL and NOT recommended by this row: widen the library type at src/lib/auth.ts:16 to `export interface GhState { installed: boolean; authenticated: boolean; version?: string; }` and fill it from the value already fetched — replace src/lib/auth.ts:23-24 with `const probe = await runner.run('gh', ['--version']); if (probe.code !== 0) return { installed: false, authenticated: false };` and return `{ installed: true, version: probe.stdout.trim().split('\n')[0], authenticated: await ghAuthenticated(runner) }` at :28. No zod field: `config.github` (src/lib/schema.ts:95) already holds the drawn login.
- **Verb or flag:** none. OPTIONAL and NOT recommended: split src/lib/evals/agent.ts:208-216 into `export async function agentVersion(): Promise<Result<{ ccVersion: string }>>` with `preflight` calling it before the existing temp-dir task (:217-221), so a displayed version costs no agent turn. Neither optional half reaches a caller: nothing under src/lib has a typed public entry point (package.json declares `bin` only and tsconfig.build.json never sets `declaration`), and no verb payload carries `GhState` except `login`, which prompts and writes.
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** (only if the optional widening is taken) src/lib/__tests__/auth.test.ts: ghState reports the first line of `gh --version` stdout and still reports installed:false on a non-zero code; (only if taken) src/lib/__tests__/auth.test.ts: ghState makes no `gh api` call — the denying runner in src/lib/__tests__/fixtures.ts refuses it; (only if taken) src/lib/evals/__tests__/agent.test.ts: agentVersion returns ccVersion without spawning the preflight task
- **Spec section:** none required. If the optional halves are taken they are TWO sections in TWO documents: .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6 `login` / §13 default 38 (:553) for `GhState`, and .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md §7.4 (:372) for the preflight split.
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** none required. OPTIONAL (gh half, Ryan), paste-ready if he wants the CLI to own the probe: “`GhState` carries the fact its probe already produces: `version`, the first line of `gh --version` stdout, alongside the unchanged `installed`/`authenticated`. `ghState` remains offline and makes no GitHub API call — the GitHub login any surface shows comes from `config.github` (§5.4), which `login` populates once via `gh api user -q .login`. No caller is required to render either value and `login`'s printed lines are unchanged. Signing in and signing out remain gh's own commands: nothing in terum-skills runs `gh auth login` except the existing §6 `login` offer on an interactive TTY channel, and no verb runs `gh auth logout` or a package manager.”
- **Why SIMPLE:** The CLI buys nothing here that the app cannot read for itself, and paying CLI-side is what would break the bar. Every value drawn is a PER-MACHINE fact about this laptop's tooling — the three states are `gh --version` and `gh auth status` EXIT CODES (src/lib/auth.ts:23,32), the version is that command's stdout, the login is `config.github` (src/lib/schema.ts:95) and the agent line is `claude --version` (src/lib/evals/agent.ts:211) — so D20 does not bite and an app-local read is legitimate. There is no read verb to hang them on: `login` prompts and writes (src/commands/login.ts:22-23) and `status` is forbidden host access (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:345, :496). The ACTING buttons are a different price and cannot be bought by any payload field: an app-opened EXTERNAL terminal is MEDIUM (a new platform-conditional spawn capability across three OSes — the same capability AC-08's “Show in Finder” and build.py:3710's needs), and an in-app PTY hosting `gh auth login` is HARD (the only path hands stdio to a child at src/lib/auth.ts:44, §3 :59 does not describe such a channel, and it needs macOS/Windows/Linux re-verification). Neither is needed: build.py:3540's own copy promises only that Sign in “opens gh's own login IN A TERMINAL”, so instruction-only is what the design already says.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — Three read-only local probes the app runs itself — `gh --version` (installed + the drawn version string), `gh auth status` (authenticated), `claude --version` (the agent line) — plus `config.github` read from `~/.terum/skills/config.json` for “Signed in as <login>”. The three buttons become instruction affordances: the command in a `cli_box` with Copy and a “Check again” that re-probes, and a plain link to https://cli.github.com for Install gh. The app must NOT shell `gh auth login` into a hidden child. No new capability is invented: the app already spawns the CLI over the app spec's Prompter/Result seam, and these are three more spawns of the same shape.
- **Cites:** `src/lib/auth.ts:16,21-29,31-33,40-46,69-71 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/login.ts:15,17,21-23 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/status.ts:23 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:95 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/evals/agent.ts:208-221 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/uninstallMachine.ts:136-137 @ b5c0507`, `package.json (bin only; no exports/main/types) @ b5c0507`, `tsconfig.build.json (no `declaration`) @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:59,345,496,553 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:372,480 @ b5c0507`

#### RM-29 — Machine-teardown consent is print-only, its dialog is undrawn, and evals/ is in neither the removed nor the kept list  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan (phase-1 spec §6 `uninstall`, :352))*

- **Boards:** SettingsAdvanced, SettingsEvals, SettingsLeave, SettingsPrune
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `uninstallMachine` gathers the facts at src/commands/uninstallMachine.ts:21-40 and prints twelve consent lines before asking (src/commands/uninstallMachine.ts:42-57): headline :42; teams with credential-stripped remotes and handles :43; placed skills :44; local clones :46 plus “(a clone holding uncommitted or unpushed work is moved to <quarantine> instead)” :47; “Version cache and run files for these teams” :49; the hook file :50; the config path :51; a `Kept:` line naming quarantine with its item count and backups :52; “Connected-skill sources stay where they are” :55 (this line read “Shared-skill sources” at 8bf12a8 — renamed by 71e7a0a); the membership/rejoin note :56; and the package note :57; then `io.confirm('Remove terum-skills from this machine?')` at :58. `MachineUninstallResult` (:16) is the AFTER report, produced at :138. The sweep is `['run','cache','teams','quarantine'].map(...).concat(store.root)` with a non-recursive `rmdir` (:120-121); ENOTEMPTY/EEXIST on the ROOT is skipped SILENTLY (:126) while every other non-empty directory is printed as `Kept ...` and pushed to `kept` (:127-128). So `<root>/evals/<team>/<id>/<runId>` with its transcripts and sandboxes (src/commands/eval.ts:103-107) survives, is named in NEITHER list, and is invisible in both directions — as would be any app-owned file under the store root (MC-08). This is spec-conformant, not a bug: §6 `uninstall` says “removes empty directories non-recursively, preserves quarantine/, backups/ and every shared source” (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:352) and never mentions `evals/`, which post-dates it.
- **Field:** No zod field and no new type. One inventory line: in the gather block (beside src/commands/uninstallMachine.ts:35-38, where quarantine and backups are already stat-ed) add `const evals = join(store.root, 'evals'); const evalTeams = (await entries(evals)).length;` and extend the existing `Kept:` print at :52 to name it when present, then `if (evalTeams) kept.push(evals);` beside :53-54 — so the tree is disclosed in the same sentence, from the same code, as quarantine and backups.
- **Verb or flag:** none. The coster's `uninstall --preview` (one flag on one existing verb, plus a `plan` payload) is within the bar but is NOT recommended and is not this row's price — see reason.
- **Guard:** none — `uninstallMachine` never calls `safeWrite`, and neither does the `teardownTeam` it delegates to, so src/lib/guard.ts:10's `GuardAction` union and src/lib/guard.ts:36's `PEOPLE_ACTIONS` are untouched and no §6.0 table row is added.
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/uninstallMachine.test.ts: the Kept line names the evals tree when one exists, with quarantine and backups, and the run leaves it on disk; src/commands/__tests__/uninstallMachine.test.ts: with no evals tree the Kept line is byte-identical to today's; src/commands/__tests__/uninstallMachine.test.ts: the surviving evals tree also appears in MachineUninstallResult.kept
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6, the `uninstall` row at :352
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** “`uninstall`'s consent inventory names the local evaluation run trees under `<store root>/evals/` among what is Kept. The sweep is unchanged and stays non-recursive, so those trees survive exactly as they do today, alongside `quarantine/`, `backups/` and every connected source; this is a disclosure of existing behaviour, not a new retention rule, and it takes no position on how or when run trees are pruned.”
- **Why SIMPLE:** SIMPLE and clean: one print line and one `kept` entry in one file, no owner-file field, no zod change, no verb, no flag, no guard (verified: no `safeWrite` on this path), tests in the existing src/commands/__tests__/uninstallMachine.test.ts, one spec section, one approver. It fixes the one thing that is actually WRONG in the CLI — a retained tree disclosed in neither direction. The coster's `uninstall --preview` + `plan` shape is deliberately NOT taken: at the seam the spec reserves for the app (a caller supplying the `Prompter`, .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:59 and §13 default 40 at :555) all twelve consent lines ALREADY arrive as `io.print` calls in order and `io.confirm` at :58 is a callback the GUI answers, so a preview is `confirm → false`, which the code already handles; and at the spawn-and-parse seam `createExecute` discards `outcome.value` entirely (src/lib/execute.ts:24-29, no `--json` anywhere in src/cli.ts) so a new `plan` field is unreadable, while a spawned `uninstall` cannot be confirmed at all (`terminalPrompter` fails closed on a non-TTY, src/lib/prompt.ts:61,70). It also has an unpriced defect: a previewing success returns `{teams: [], removedPlacements: 0, hookRemoved: false, configRemoved: false, record: ''}` which a caller cannot distinguish from “nothing was there to remove”. The `evals/` sentence must be a DISCLOSURE only — the pruning policy is Ajay's and is explicitly provisional (.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:121), so the delta must not harden it.
- **App side:** FORBIDDEN (D20 ok) — The app could walk `~/.terum/skills` and `config.json` itself and render its own list of what removal would take. Mechanically easy. It must not.
- **Cites:** `src/commands/uninstallMachine.ts:16,21-40,42-58,120-128,136-138 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/eval.ts:103-107 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/execute.ts:21-29 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/prompt.ts:61,70 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/guard.ts:10,36 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:59,352,555 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:121 @ b5c0507`

#### RM-30 — No verb payload carries a skill's description, which every card draws  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan (phase-1 spec, verb payloads))*

- **Boards:** Library, Marketplace, MarketplaceProject, MarketplacePerson, SkillDetail, Inbox
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `export interface LsSkill { id: string; name: string; author: string; category: string; installs: number; latest: string; endorsement: string; }` — NO description (src/commands/ls.ts:18). `SearchHit` adds `team`/`endorsed`/`unresolved` but no description (src/commands/search.ts:15-19, hit built at :58). Both verbs hold the value one line earlier and drop it: `search` filters on `skill.frontmatter.description` (src/commands/search.ts:42) and `ls` parses the frontmatter at src/commands/ls.ts:53 and reads four other fields off it at :60. `publish` prints it and returns none (src/commands/publish.ts:212). The only shipped reader that RETURNS it is the README's: `ReadmeSkill.description: string` (src/lib/readme.ts:18, interface :15-24), filled at :137 from `parsed.data.description` and rendered into the table's Description column at :87 — so the closest thing to a Library read model in the tree is the README generator's, not `ls`'s. The value is schema-guaranteed: `skillFrontmatterSchema` declares `description: z.string()` (src/lib/schema.ts:109), and `parseSkillFrontmatter` is what both verbs already call.
- **Field:** No zod field — src/lib/schema.ts:109 already parses it. One field on each of two payload interfaces plus the two literals that fill them: src/commands/ls.ts:18 becomes `export interface LsSkill { id: string; name: string; description: string; author: string; category: string; installs: number; latest: string; endorsement: string; }` and src/commands/ls.ts:60 gains `description: parsed.data.description,` (the object is already `parsed.data`, read at :53); src/commands/search.ts:16 gains `description: string;` and src/commands/search.ts:58 gains `description: skill.frontmatter.description,` (the same expression already evaluated at :42).
- **Verb or flag:** none — no verb, no flag. `format()` (src/commands/ls.ts:84) interpolates named properties, so every printed line is byte-identical, including `search`'s reuse of it via the explicit seven-key literal at src/commands/search.ts:60.
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: LsSkill carries the SKILL.md description verbatim and the printed line is unchanged; src/commands/__tests__/search.test.ts: SearchHit carries the description of every hit, including a hit whose latest tree is unresolved; src/commands/__tests__/search.test.ts: a description over the HYG6 size threshold is returned unmodified (it is a warning, not a refusal)
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6, the `ls` row at :343 (`search` at :344 inherits by “one hit per line in `ls` format”)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** “`ls` and `search` return each skill's SKILL.md `description` on the Result. It is the value both verbs already read — `search` matches on it and `ls` parses the frontmatter it comes from — and it is returned verbatim, unclipped and untruncated, exactly as the README generator returns it. The printed `ls` format line is unchanged: description is a payload field, not a column, because a one-line-per-skill listing cannot carry it without wrapping.”
- **Why SIMPLE:** The cheapest true row in the batch and the one no refuter could damage. No zod change (already parsed at src/lib/schema.ts:109), no owner file, no verb, no flag, no guard, no write, no engine change, no sync interaction. Two interface lines and two object-literal lines, from values already in hand at both call sites (src/commands/ls.ts:53, src/commands/search.ts:42). Output safety verified directly: every `toEqual` in src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts and search.test.ts asserts over `io.lines`, `io.asked`, a Result or file bytes — none over an `LsSkill`/`SearchHit` object — so a new key breaks no assertion. Tests fit two existing files; one spec section; one approver.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The app reads `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` from the team clone and parses its own frontmatter.
- **Cites:** `src/commands/ls.ts:18,53,60,84 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/search.ts:15-19,42,58,60 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/publish.ts:212 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/readme.ts:15-24,87,137 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:109 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:114 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:343,344 @ b5c0507`

#### RM-31 — SyncResult carries no per-team outcome, and an interactive partial sync returns no payload at all  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan (phase-1 spec §6 `sync`))*

- **Boards:** SettingsSync, SettingsTeams, InboxEmpty, LibraryError, MarketplaceError, ShareError, SkillDetailError, OnboardingError
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `export interface SyncResult { placed: number; deferred: string[]; notices: string[]; changed: boolean; hook: boolean; }` (src/commands/sync.ts:32). The per-team information exists only as locals — `const skipped = new Set<string>();` and `const unreachable: string[] = [];` (src/commands/sync.ts:99-100) — and the outcomes are decided and discarded: FRESH, the hook-mode hour stamp, is `if (args.hook && await stampIsFresh(...)) { skipped.add(team); continue; }` (:106) and is SILENT; LOCKED is an English notice and only when `!args.hook` (:109-110); a gate throw is a notice (:114); UNREACHABLE pushes to both sets (:120-121); a busy clone is `CloneBusy → notice(error.message); skipped.add(team);` (:126) with the comment explaining a team must never land on `deferred` because `deferred` renders as a count of SKILLS (:124-125). A team skipped because another run holds `run/<team>.lock` returns ok:true with changed:false. And on BOTH failure paths the value is attached ONLY in hook mode — `args.hook ? { placed, deferred, notices, changed, hook: true } : undefined` at :293 and the same guard on the catch at :295 — so an interactive partial sync loses `placed`, `deferred` and `notices` entirely, while exit 1 means “some team was skipped”, not “the sync failed” (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:353). `status` already sets the opposite precedent: it attaches its value on both failure paths unconditionally (src/commands/status.ts:100-101).
- **Field:** No zod field. Two payload types at src/commands/sync.ts:32 — `export interface TeamSyncOutcome { team: string; outcome: 'synced' / 'fresh' / 'locked' / 'unreachable' / 'busy' / 'errored' / 'dropped'; placed: number; deferred: string[]; stamped: boolean; }` and `export interface SyncResult { placed: number; deferred: string[]; notices: string[]; changed: boolean; hook: boolean; teams: TeamSyncOutcome[]; }`. Attribution is NOT free and must be threaded at three sites, none of which tracks a team today: (a) `placed` is one function-scoped counter incremented at src/commands/sync.ts:144 and :268 — make it a `Map<string, number>` keyed by team and sum it for the top-level total; (b) `defer` (src/commands/sync.ts:63) pushes labels into a FLAT array and records only the team in `incomplete` — add a `Map<string, string[]>` beside it, one line; (c) `reconcileShared` (src/commands/sync.ts:154, defined src/commands/connect.ts:231) and `reconcileOrphans` (src/commands/sync.ts:277, defined :302) both call `defer(team, label)` from OUTSIDE the per-team loop, so (b) is what makes their deferrals attributable. The six decision points that set `outcome` already exist: :106 fresh, :109-110 locked, :114 errored, :120-121 unreachable, :126 busy, and `synced` in the stamp loop at :288-291 (where `stamped` is exactly the branch that reaches `writeStamp`); `dropped` covers the fourth `continue` in that loop — a team no longer in `final.teams` (:289).
- **Verb or flag:** none. One behavioural line changes: at src/commands/sync.ts:293 and :295, drop the `args.hook ?` guard so the value rides EVERY failure path — `failure(msg, { placed, deferred, notices, changed, hook: Boolean(args.hook), teams })` — matching what `status` already does at src/commands/status.ts:100-101.
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: a locked team is reported as outcome 'locked' with stamped false while the other team is 'synced' and stamped; src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: a hook-mode fresh-stamp skip is reported as 'fresh' on the Result and still prints nothing; src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: an interactive unreachable-team run returns ok:false AND carries placed/deferred/notices/teams; src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: per-team placed and deferred are attributed correctly when the orphan pass defers a placement belonging to a different team from the one that placed skills; src/lib/__tests__/execute.test.ts: an interactive failing sync Result with a value writes no hook notices to stderr (isHookSync stays false)
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6, the `sync [--hook]` row at :353
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** “`sync` returns a per-team outcome alongside its totals: for each configured team, whether it synced, was rate-limited fresh, was locked by another run, had a busy clone, was unreachable, errored, or was dropped from config mid-run — with that team's placed count, its deferrals, and whether its stamp advanced. The classification is the one the run already makes; nothing new is probed and no team's treatment changes. The Result is also attached on every failure path, not only in hook mode, so a partial sync that exits 1 still reports what it completed. Exit codes, printed lines, notice routing and the §8 stamp rule are unchanged: only teams that completed with no undone work are stamped.”
- **Why SIMPLE:** SIMPLE: no owner file, no zod, no verb, no flag, no guard, no team.json row, no privacy call, no engine change. Two safety checks had to pass and both do. (1) Attaching the value on the interactive failure path changes NO OUTPUT: `createExecute` gates its hook-notice printing on `isHookSync(outcome.value)`, which requires `hook === true` (src/lib/execute.ts:25, :41-42), and the interactive Result sets `hook: false`, so only the one stderr line and exit 1 remain (:26-29). (2) No sync/stamp interaction is smuggled in: `writeStamp` stays at src/commands/sync.ts:291 under exactly the same condition (:288-290), and `stamped` merely REPORTS that branch. D-R4 is unaffected — the app still must not sync on a timer, an app sync still writes `run/<team>.stamp` through the verb, and `sync --hook` still no-ops within the hour via `stampIsFresh` (:106). §8's stdout reservation is safe: the hook's directive is written from `placed` alone at :279, and `writeHookNotices` iterates `notices` and `deferred` only (src/lib/execute.ts:45-47), so a new `teams` array is invisible to both streams. The one correction to the coster's basis: this is NOT “a decomposition of counters the run already keeps” — per-team `placed` and `deferred` do not exist today and must be threaded at the three sites named in `field`. The work is larger than advertised; no bar condition breaks.
- **App side:** FORBIDDEN (D20 no) — The app could infer per-team outcomes by string-matching `notices` (they start `Skipping <team>: ...`) or by stat-ing each `run/<team>.stamp` before and after.
- **Cites:** `src/commands/sync.ts:32,63,99-100,106,109-110,114,120-121,124-126,144,154,268,277,279,288-291,293,295,302 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/connect.ts:231,234 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/execute.ts:25,41-42,45-47 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/status.ts:21,100-101 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:353 @ b5c0507`

#### RM-32 — sync takes no --team, so the per-team 'Sync now' button on the team row has no verb  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan (phase-1 spec §6 `sync`))*

- **Boards:** SettingsTeams, SettingsSync
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `program.command('sync').description(...).option('--hook').option('--prune')` — no `--team` (src/cli.ts:125) — and `SyncArgs` has no `team` field (src/commands/sync.ts:24-31). It is the odd one out: `team remove` --team (src/cli.ts:75), `invite` --team (:91), `ls` --team (:93) with `member` (:95) and `project` (:96), `status` --team (:97), `publish` --team (:111), `validate` --team (:114), `eval` --team (:116), `connect` --team (:119), `install` --team (:122), `uninstall-skill` --team (:123). (The seed row's “…and share” is now “…and connect” — 71e7a0a; the hidden `share` stub at src/cli.ts:121 swallows `--team` through `allowUnknownOption()` only to refuse.) `selectTeam` — the helper every neighbour uses — already implements the semantics: unknown name → `Team <name> is not configured.`, one team → that one, several with no request → `More than one team is configured; pass --team <name> (...)` (src/lib/config.ts:23-30). And `sync` walks EVERY configured team in FIVE places, not two: the work loop (src/commands/sync.ts:101), `reconcileShared` (:154, defined src/commands/connect.ts:231, gated only by `skipTeams.has(tracked.team)` at connect.ts:234), the placement loop over `currentConfig.placements` (:158, gated only by `skipped.has(entry.team)` at :162), `reconcileOrphans` (:277, defined :302, gated only by `skipTeams.has(placement.team)` at :308), and the stamp loop (:288).
- **Field:** No zod field. One arg on src/commands/sync.ts:26: `hook?: boolean; prune?: boolean; team?: string; config?: ConfigStore; runner?: Runner; cwd?: string;`
- **Verb or flag:** Exactly one new flag on one existing verb, src/cli.ts:125: add `.option('--team <team>', 'sync only this configured team')` and pass `team: options.team` into both `active.sync(...)` shapes. IMPLEMENTATION (this is the load-bearing half, and it is the OPPOSITE of filtering the loops): refuse the two incoherent combinations FIRST, before `--prune`'s early return at src/commands/sync.ts:84-88 — `if (args.team && args.prune) throw new Error('sync --prune covers the whole machine; drop --team.');` and `if (args.team && args.hook) throw new Error('sync --hook syncs every configured team; drop --team.');` (the precedent is src/commands/ls.ts:25-26) — then, immediately after `const config = await store.read();` (:89), resolve the selection with `selectTeam` and PRE-POPULATE the existing skip set: `if (args.team !== undefined) { const [only] = selectTeam(config.teams, args.team, args.form); for (const name of Object.keys(config.teams)) if (name !== only) skipped.add(name); }`, plus `if (skipped.has(team)) continue;` at the top of the work loop (:102). Nothing else changes. All FIVE all-team walks then inherit the exclusion from the one mechanism already designed for it — the work loop, `reconcileShared` (connect.ts:234), the placement loop (:162), `reconcileOrphans` (:308) and the stamp loop (:289) — and the exit-1 accounting stays right because only src/commands/sync.ts:121 pushes to `unreachable`, which a deliberately unselected team never reaches. Do NOT introduce a `teamNames` binding over the work and stamp loops only: that leaves three walks unfiltered.
- **Guard:** none — with the skip-set implementation the guard is never reached for an unselected team. With the naive two-loop filter it IS reached: `reconcileOrphans` would prompt “Adopt orphaned placement at <path>?” (src/commands/sync.ts:317) for another team's placements and, on y or n, call `repo.safeWrite(..., { action: 'install' / 'uninstall', handle: binding.handle, ... })` against that team's clone (:320, :332) — committing `people/<handle>.json` in a team this run never locked.
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: --team syncs only the named team and leaves the other team's clone, placements and stamp untouched; src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: --team stamps only the named team (the stamp loop honours the same selection as the work loop); src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: --team runs no orphan-adoption prompt and performs no safeWrite for an unselected team's orphaned placement (the test that fails on the naive two-loop implementation); src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: --team leaves an unselected team's connected-source reconciliation untouched (reconcileShared honours the same skip set); src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: --team with an unknown name fails with selectTeam's message and writes nothing; src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: --team with --prune and --team with --hook are each refused before the prune early-return and before any lock is taken
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6, the `sync [--hook]` row at :353 (the row header becomes `sync [--hook] [--team <name>]`)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** “`sync [--team <name>]` restricts the run to one configured team, selected by the same `selectTeam` rule every other team-scoped verb uses; unknown names fail before any work. Every other configured team is treated exactly as a skipped team is: not locked, not fetched, not reconciled, its placements and connected sources untouched, its orphaned placements neither adopted nor declined, and its stamp unchanged — so a per-team sync neither suppresses nor accelerates the session hook for any other team, and no people file outside the selected team is written. `--team` is refused with `--prune`, which is machine-wide, and with `--hook`, which exists to cover every configured team at session start. Exit codes and the skipped-team failure line are unchanged and are computed over the selected team alone; a team excluded by `--team` is not a skipped team for that count.”
- **Why SIMPLE:** SIMPLE: exactly one new flag on one existing verb (condition 2), the flag every neighbouring verb already carries, implemented with the helper they already share (src/lib/config.ts:23) and the skip set the run already keeps. No owner file, no zod, no guard change, no committed write, no new mechanism. D-R4 is respected and worth stating: a per-team sync writes only that team's stamp (src/commands/sync.ts:291), so it suppresses the SessionStart hook for that team alone (`stampIsFresh`, :106) and leaves every other team's next hook run exactly as it was — precisely the behaviour the drawn per-team button should have. The one hazard is the implementation, not the price: `sync` has FIVE all-team walks and filtering only the work and stamp loops reaches `safeWrite` in an unselected team's clone (:320, :332) WITHOUT that team's mutex — the exact second-writer the mutex exists to stop, as the code's own comment says (“EVERY sync takes the mutex ... `team leave` holds it while it removes the team's placements”, :96-98, acquired at :107). Pre-populating `skipped` is one binding and closes all five.
- **App side:** FORBIDDEN (D20 no) — Wire the per-team button to a whole-machine `sync` and label it as if it were scoped; or reach into `~/.terum/skills/teams/<team>` and run git itself.
- **Cites:** `src/cli.ts:75,91,93,95,96,97,111,114,116,119,121,122,123,125 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:24-31,84-89,96-98,101,102,107,121,154,158,162,277,288-291,302,308,317,320,332 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/connect.ts:231,234 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/config.ts:23-30 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:25-26 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:350,353 @ b5c0507`

#### RM-38 — 'Recently updated' sort has no field: nothing in the read model carries a last-changed date  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** Library, LibraryNoResults, LibraryLight, LibraryCollapsed, MarketplaceFilters, MarketplaceFull, MarketplaceProject
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** No skill carries a timestamp. LsSkill is {id, name, author, category, installs, latest, endorsement} (src/commands/ls.ts:18) and latest is shortHash(latestTree(...)) (src/commands/ls.ts:59-60) where latestTree is git rev-parse HEAD:skills/<name> (src/lib/readme.ts:119-123) — a tree hash, not a date; shortHash slices 8 characters (src/lib/readme.ts:224). SearchHit (src/commands/search.ts:15-19) carries latest and unresolved and no date either. The only dates in any schema are installedSchema.since (src/lib/schema.ts:40, day-granular, per person), config.placements[].placed_at (:100, per machine) and config.approvals[].approved_at (:98) — none is 'when this skill last changed'. A true last-updated needs a git-log walk of the clone, and no verb reads git history: a grep for a 'log' argument across src/**/*.ts at b5c0507 returns nothing, and resolveVersion's doc comment frames versions as 'exactly a skill tree, never a commit or a history walk' (src/lib/version.ts:8). The clone is not shallow (git clone -q --branch main --, src/lib/teamRepo.ts:348), so the history is there to walk.
- **Field:** `updated: string;` on LsSkill (src/commands/ls.ts:18) and on SearchHit (src/commands/search.ts:15-19) — an ISO-8601 committer date or '—', fed by a new sibling of latestTree in src/lib/readme.ts: `export async function latestChange(runner: Runner, clone: string, name: string): Promise<string>` running `git log -1 --format=%cI -- skills/<name>`
- **Verb or flag:** none (the field rides the existing ls and search payloads; format() at src/commands/ls.ts:84 gains one more '; ' segment)
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/lib/__tests__/readme.test.ts: latestChange returns the committer date of the last commit touching a skill folder; src/lib/__tests__/readme.test.ts: latestChange degrades to an em dash for a folder with no history; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: ls carries updated for every skill and one unresolvable folder costs only its own row; src/commands/__tests__/search.test.ts: search hits carry updated and the shared format() line gains exactly one segment
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6, the `ls` bullet (and its `search` sentence — one paragraph covering both readers)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** `ls` and `search` each carry one more field per skill, `updated`: the committer date of the most recent commit that touched skills/<name>, read with `git log -1 --format=%cI -- skills/<name>` in the team clone and rendered as an ISO-8601 instant. Both readers gain it together because they print through one function (src/commands/ls.ts:84, whose comment already records that search prints hits through it), so the §6 `ls` line format gains exactly one segment for both. It is read-only, taken from committed history, and identical on every clone at the same commit. Like `latest`, one folder git cannot resolve costs exactly that row: the failure is caught, one line is printed, the field becomes '—', and every other row survives (rulings walk R11). This is the tool's first `git log`; §7's 'never a commit or a history walk' governs resolveVersion — which resolves a version to a tree — and is not weakened by a read-only date on the inventory. `updated` is the field a UI sorts 'recently updated' by; installed[].since and placements[].placed_at are deliberately NOT substitutes — the first is per-person, the second per-machine, and neither answers 'when did this skill last change'.
- **Why SIMPLE:** SIMPLE on every condition — no owner file, no zod field, no verb or flag, no guard, tests in the existing layout, one spec paragraph in one section covering both readers, Ryan. Two costs to record honestly: it adds a second git invocation per skill to a verb that is already O(skills) spawns (src/commands/ls.ts:59 inside a Promise.all; gap-4.md:725 R3 measures the same shape), and the batched reader R3 recommends piggybacking on, skillTrees, is module-private at src/lib/teamRepo.ts:285 (called only from :152), so there is nothing to batch onto today.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The app runs `git -C <clone> log -1 --format=%cI -- skills/<name>` itself, once per skill, against the clone the CLI maintains.
- **Cites:** `src/commands/ls.ts:18 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:59-60 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:83-84 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/search.ts:12 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/search.ts:15-19 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/readme.ts:119-123 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:40 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:100 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/version.ts:8 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/teamRepo.ts:285 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/teamRepo.ts:348 @ b5c0507`

#### RM-39 — No read-only health classification: update-available / local-changed exist only inside the mutating sync loop  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** SkillCard, Library, LibraryLight, SkillDetail, SkillDetailHovers, InboxAlert
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** The classification is computed inside sync's placing loop and interleaved with its writes: the comparison is at src/commands/sync.ts:207-209 (snapshot the source tree, snapshot the placed copy if the target is ours, continue when both match the ledger fingerprint) and is re-taken under the target lock at :227, immediately after quarantineDrift has already MOVED the drifted copy (:224, src/lib/placer.ts:157); the foreign-collision decision is at :220-221 and `blocked` is a print-and-defer helper (:73). The vocabulary is defined at .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:371. Read-only alternatives return nothing equivalent: `ls --local` yields tracking PROSE (src/commands/ls.ts:94-110, 'placement recorded from <team> @<8-hex>' at :108, 'untracked locally' at :109) and TeamStatus (src/commands/status.ts:17-22) has no per-skill health. No exported pure classifier exists — but every ingredient does and each is exported: snapshotSkillDirectory (src/lib/placer/vendor/skillhub/skill-fingerprint.ts:15), snapshotIfPresent (src/lib/placer.ts:165), inspect (src/lib/placer.ts:29), and the ledger fingerprint on config.placements[path] (src/lib/schema.ts:100), which showLocal already reads (src/commands/ls.ts:90) alongside the clone path (:121) and the placed path (:135). The THIRD flag has no source at all: hygiene's codes are HYG1..HYG6 (src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:6; the assessment is {errors, warnings} at :9 and HYG6's size cap is a non-blocking warning at :95 since 914ebb0), and nothing anywhere parses a SKILL.md for referenced paths.
- **Field:** `health: 'ok' / 'update-available' / 'local-changed' / 'both' / 'placement-missing' / 'untracked'` on LocalSection.rows[] at src/commands/ls.ts:19, computed inside showLocal from three fingerprints it can already reach: config.placements[path].fingerprint (src/lib/schema.ts:100, read at src/commands/ls.ts:90), snapshotIfPresent(entry.path) (src/lib/placer.ts:165), and snapshotSkillDirectory(join(store.teamClone(ref.team),'skills',name)) (src/lib/placer/vendor/skillhub/skill-fingerprint.ts:15)
- **Verb or flag:** none — the field rides the existing `ls --local` payload
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: --local reports ok when the ledger, the placed copy and the clone tree agree; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: --local reports update-available when only the clone tree differs; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: --local reports local-changed when only the placed copy differs; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: --local reports both when the placed copy and the clone tree each differ; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: --local reports untracked for a folder with no shared ref and no placement, and computes no health for it; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: an unreadable clone costs that row's health alone, not the section
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6, the `ls` bullet's --local paragraph (the vocabulary it uses is defined at :371)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** `ls --local` carries one more field per row, `health`, so a caller can ask what state a placement is in without running the sync that would change it. It is a pure comparison of three fingerprints — the one on the ledger entry, the one taken from the placed folder, and the one taken from the tree the clone holds now for that skill — and it is one of ok, update-available, local-changed, both, placement-missing, or untracked (a folder with neither a connected source nor a placement, which has no fingerprints to compare). It performs no write, takes no lock and moves nothing: `quarantineDrift` remains the only thing that acts on a local-changed answer, and §6's local-changed / blocked vocabulary is unchanged — this only gives it a read-only reading. Note that this is a genuinely NEW comparison rather than a restatement of sync's: for a placement pinned to a version, sync compares against the materialized pinned tree, so it never asks whether the team's current tree has moved. A clone the row cannot read costs that row's health alone ('unknown'), the way an unresolvable latestTree costs one row of `ls`.
- **Why SIMPLE:** SIMPLE and it breaks zero conditions: no owner file and no zod field (LocalSection is a TypeScript interface), no new verb and no new flag (the field rides `ls --local`), no guard change, tests in the existing src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts, one spec paragraph in one section, one approver. Crucially it needs NO packaging change: the value crosses the seam on a verb Result, which is the seam §3 already assigns to the UI (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:59, 'calls the same functions'), so no exports map, no types, no declaration emit and no addition to src/lib/__tests__/package.test.ts. Exporting a pure classifyPlacement from src/lib/placer.ts is a good refactor (one definition instead of the two inline comparisons at src/commands/sync.ts:209 and :227) but it is NOT what buys the design its badges, and it cannot return 'foreign' — that verdict comes from inspect()'s lstat (src/lib/placer.ts:15, :29), not from three fingerprints.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The app compares the three fingerprints itself: config.placements[<path>].fingerprint (src/lib/schema.ts:100), a fresh snapshot of the placed folder, and a fresh snapshot of the clone's skills/<name>. All three are reads; none runs sync.
- **Cites:** `src/commands/ls.ts:19 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:90 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:121 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:135 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:100 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/placer.ts:29 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/placer.ts:165 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/placer/vendor/skillhub/skill-fingerprint.ts:15 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:184 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:207-209 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:227 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:371 @ b5c0507`

#### RM-40 — A local-origin (unshared) skill's detail page: the rail renders team-only fields unconditionally  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** SkillDetail (missing state), SkillDetailLight, SkillDetailRailClosed, SkillDetailNoReceipt, Library, SettingsSharing
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** A local skill is in no team clone. localSkills returns LocalEntry = {name, path, shared: SharedRef[], placement?: PlacementRef, inspection} with Inspection = candidate / rejected / failed (src/lib/local-skills.ts:10-13, LocalEntry at :14), and `ls --local` prints 'untracked locally' for an entry with neither a connected source nor a placement (src/commands/ls.ts:109). Such a folder has no id (minted by connect, src/commands/connect.ts:191), no tree version (resolveVersion resolves HEAD:skills/<name> in a clone it is not in, src/lib/version.ts:22-27), no installs (installCounts counts ids in people[].installed, src/lib/readme.ts:40; src/lib/schema.ts:62), no guaranteed metadata.author (a managed field connect adds, src/cli.ts:114), no receipt (eval is documented as 'Evaluate a shared skill locally', src/cli.ts:116) and no install command (the three-part ref needs a repository, spec :287). Critically for the app: the predicate is computed and then DISCARDED — src/commands/ls.ts:131 is `const tracked = entry.shared.length > 0 // entry.placement !== undefined;`, used only as a filter at :133, and :135 pushes {name, path, state, problem?} into LocalSection.rows (src/commands/ls.ts:19). The only trace that crosses the seam is stateOf's PROSE (:94-110). showLocal also returns {roster: [], skills: [], local: sections} (:151), so `ls --local` yields no team skills at all.
- **Field:** `tracked: boolean` on LocalSection.rows[] at src/commands/ls.ts:19, populated from the variable that already exists at src/commands/ls.ts:131 by adding one property to the object pushed at :135
- **Verb or flag:** none — the field rides the existing `ls --local` payload
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: --local marks a folder with no shared ref and no placement as tracked false; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: --local marks a folder with a connected source as tracked true; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: --local marks a folder with only a recorded placement as tracked true; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: tracked agrees with the printed state line for every row
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6, the `ls` bullet's --local paragraph
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** Each `ls --local` row carries `tracked`, the boolean the command already computes to decide which folders it lists: true when the folder has at least one connected source or a recorded placement, false when it has neither — the state the printed line calls 'untracked locally'. It is the same fact the prose carries, exposed as a value so a caller does not have to match an English sentence that exists to be read by a person; the printed output is unchanged. It writes nothing, takes no lock, and adds no work: the value is already in hand when the row is built.
- **Why SIMPLE:** SIMPLE on every condition: no owner file and no zod field (LocalSection is a TypeScript interface), no new verb or flag, no guard change, tests in the existing src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts, one spec paragraph, one approver. The coster's NA is true about the fields that would have to be INVENTED (a local skill genuinely has no repo, author-of-record, receipt, install count or version) and false about the predicate the app must read: LocalEntry never leaves the library, so without this field the app's only option is to string-match the prose sentinel 'untracked locally' (src/commands/ls.ts:109) — the same fragility this family objects to elsewhere, and untested.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — One app rule. When a Library entry's row is `tracked: false` (the new field; today, the sentinel state 'untracked locally' at src/commands/ls.ts:109), the detail rail hides Repo, Author, Evaluated, Installs and Version, replaces the Share-with-CLI block with a Connect primary action pointing at connect <path> (src/cli.ts:119), and shows no health indicator (there is no ledger fingerprint to compare against — RM-39). Two mechanics the rule must state: the Library merges TWO ls calls, because showLocal returns roster: [] and skills: [] (src/commands/ls.ts:151); and the fields the rule says to KEEP have no producer today either — LocalSection.rows carries no file count, no size, no token estimate and no description (inspection.description is read only to compute `problem` at :134 and then discarded), so a local skill's detail page renders name, path, scope and the Connect action and little else until sourceFiles / scanSkillFolder (src/lib/skill-source.ts:66, :83) reach a Result.
- **Cites:** `src/commands/ls.ts:19 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:109 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:131 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:133 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:135 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:151 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/local-skills.ts:10-13 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/local-skills.ts:14 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:116 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:119 @ b5c0507`

#### RM-41 — config.pending is on no read verb, so the mid-operation state cannot be rendered or explained  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan (phase-1 spec section 6) - ONE approval covering both RM-41 and RM-48, per OD-4)*

- **Boards:** SettingsMachine, SettingsSync, LibraryError, InboxError, MarketplaceError, ShareError
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** config.pending is the durable intent log and is written BEFORE consent and BEFORE placement. The zod line is `pending: z.array(z.object({ op: z.enum(['install','uninstall']), id: skillIdSchema, team: z.string(), scope: scopeSchema, started: z.string() }).passthrough())` (src/lib/schema.ts:99 @ b5c0507). install builds the entry at src/commands/install.ts:90 - and writes a FIFTH key the schema does not declare, `version: latest`, which survives on .passthrough() - pushes at :92, asks consent at :96, removes the entry at :99 only when it did not already exist (pendingAlreadyExists, :91) and consent threw, and clears it at :138 after the people-file safeWrite lands. uninstall builds `pendings` at src/commands/uninstall.ts:80 and pushes at :81; clears at :108. sync replays per team at src/commands/sync.ts:131 and reads the undeclared version key back at :139 (`'version' in pending && typeof pending.version === 'string'`). Spec: .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:267 (why it exists), :350 and :351 (the ordering), :534 (13.25). NO read verb exposes it: StatusResult is {version, teams} (src/commands/status.ts:23) and TeamStatus (:17-22) has no pending member; LsResult is {local?, roster, skills} (src/commands/ls.ts:20). The only user-facing mention of the word is sync's own description, "finish pending work" (src/cli.ts:125). So Result.ok === false still has three indistinguishable meanings - declined, failed before placement, failed after placement with the folder on disk and in config.placements but the people file unwritten - which is why no optimistic UI may be rolled back on ok:false (gap-4 D-R3).
- **Field:** No zod line - the schema field already exists at src/lib/schema.ts:99. One TypeScript member on the existing TeamStatus interface, inserted at src/commands/status.ts:21 beside `stale`: `pending: { op: 'install' / 'uninstall'; id: string; scope: Config['pending'][number]['scope']; version: string / null; started: string }[];`. The `version` key is REQUIRED and the coster dropped it: install.ts:90 writes it and sync.ts:139 replays from it, so without it the app cannot distinguish "sync will finish this" from "sync will place a different commit". `team` is dropped because it is the block's own key. Producer: inside the per-team loop, BEFORE the `clone.state` branch at status.ts:52, `detail.pending = config.pending.filter((entry) => entry.team === team).map(...)` reusing the config already read at status.ts:32. It must sit outside the ok-branch (opened at :60) or a broken clone would report "nothing in flight" while an install is genuinely half-done.
- **Verb or flag:** none
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/status.test.ts: 'reports a team's pending entries on the Result even when its clone is absent'; src/commands/__tests__/status.test.ts: 'reports no pending entries for a team that has none, and never another team's'; src/commands/__tests__/status.test.ts: 'carries the version key a pinned install wrote, and null for an unpinned one'; src/commands/__tests__/install.test.ts: 'leaves the pending entry visible to status between the placement and the people-file write'
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 6, the `status` bullet at :345 - ONE paragraph shared with RM-48, because gap-4.md:707 (OD-4) decided `syncedAt` AND `pending` together as option (a).
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** `status` additionally returns, per team, two machine-local values it already has in hand. (1) `pending`: the `config.pending` entries for that team as `{op, id, scope, version, started}`, read from the same config load as the rest of the block - `version` is the key `install` writes onto the entry and `sync` replays from, so a pinned interrupted install is distinguishable from an unpinned one. (2) `syncedAt: string / null`: the ISO timestamp of `run/<team>.stamp`'s last modification, or null when no stamp exists, from the same stat the stale notice already performs. Both are populated BEFORE the clone is described, so a team with an absent, incomplete or foreign clone still reports work in flight and when it last synced. `stale` is unchanged and keeps its current placement and meaning. This is a Result-only addition: the printed block is unchanged, because the printed contract is what status's tests and the section 12 acceptance walk pin. A non-empty `pending` means this machine is mid-operation - an install or uninstall that wrote its intent and has not yet cleared it - and its only remedy is `sync`, which finishes or unwinds the entry (5.4, 13.25). It is not an error and not a failure of the last command: an entry survives a decline only when it was already present from an earlier interrupted run. The two fields are read together: a team with non-empty `pending` is not stamped (section 8), so an old `syncedAt` beside a non-empty `pending` is the honest signal that the last run left work undone, not that sync failed.
- **Why SIMPLE:** No new owner-file field (the schema field exists at schema.ts:99), no verb, no flag, no guard row, no team.json write, no privacy call, no engine change. The CLI edit itself touches no sync/stamp path: status neither writes nor reads run/<team>.stamp beyond the existing staleLine call at status.ts:85. Tests land in the existing src/commands/__tests__ layout; one paragraph to one section; one approver. Every SIMPLE condition holds. THE D-R4 CONDITION THE COSTER DENIED: the row's single recommended remedy - a `Sync now` button on the pending card - is an app-initiated sync, which writes run/<team>.stamp (src/commands/sync.ts:291, writeStamp :347) and thereby suppresses the SessionStart hook's own sync machine-wide for an hour (STAMP_FRESH_MS src/lib/hook.ts:149; consumed src/commands/sync.ts:106). The read is free; the button is D-R4 (gap-4.md:59) and is user-intent only.
- **App side:** FORBIDDEN (D20 ok) — The forbidden half: the app reads ~/.terum/skills/config.json itself and lists `pending`. The legitimate half, which needs no datum at all: the app RULE that `pending` is a first-class state with exactly one action (Sync now), and that no optimistic UI is ever rolled back on ok:false.
- **Cites:** `src/lib/schema.ts:99 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/install.ts:90,91,92,96,99,138 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/uninstall.ts:80,81,108 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:106,131,139,289,290,291,347 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/status.ts:17-23,32,52,60,85,100 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:20 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:125 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/hook.ts:149 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:267,345,350,351,534 @ b5c0507`, `gap-4.md:59 (D-R4), gap-4.md:707 (OD-4 option (a))`

#### RM-44 — ls --local rows drop the team and flatten status into prose; the Sharing board draws a team column and a state chip  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan (phase-1 spec section 6, the `ls` bullet))*

- **Boards:** SettingsSharing, SettingsStates, Library
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `LocalSection.rows` is `{ name: string; path: string; state: string; problem?: string }` at src/commands/ls.ts:19 (the catalogue's :17 drifted; :17 is LsArgs). `stateOf` (src/commands/ls.ts:94-110) returns PROSE with the team embedded in the sentence: `connected source for ${ref.team}; ${status}` (:106 - that string itself changed at 71e7a0a from "shared source for"), `not endorsed in local clone` / `endorsed (<badge>)` / `repository copy missing from local clone` / `repository status unknown` (:99-103), `placement recorded from ${team}[ @<8hex>]` (:108), `conflicting tracking: ...` / `untracked locally` (:109). That vocabulary is a printed contract pinned verbatim by .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:343 (the catalogue's :340 drifted; :340 is `team leave`). The enumerator already carries the structure the row throws away: SharedRef {id, team} and PlacementRef {id, team, version} at src/lib/local-skills.ts:8-9, both held on LocalEntry (:14) and both in hand at src/commands/ls.ts:135 where the row is built. THE CORRECTION IS RIGHT AND ITS CITES ARE ALL STALE AT b5c0507: the four-way chip is produced only inside the MUTATING reconciler - reconcileShared at src/commands/connect.ts:231 (correction says share.ts:122), which digests both sides at :273-274 (share.ts:163-164), decides against `config.shared[id].baseline` at :275-276 (share.ts:165-166), PRINTS the divergence line at :277 (share.ts:167), otherwise pushes and rebaselines at :291-318, and returns Promise<void>; sync calls it at src/commands/sync.ts:154. So it cannot be run to render a page. Both read-only halves exist: canonicalDigest is exported at src/lib/skills.ts:87 (imported connect.ts:16) and baseline is src/lib/schema.ts:97. Both resolution buttons already have their flags: `--keep-source <id>` and `--keep-repo <id>` on src/cli.ts:119 (correction says share.ts:23,39 and cli.ts:112). Spec :343 ends "No git, no network, no prompt, no write."
- **Field:** No zod line - config.shared[].baseline already exists at src/lib/schema.ts:97. Two TypeScript members on LocalSection['rows'] at src/commands/ls.ts:19: `shared: { id: string; team: string }[]; placement?: { id: string; team: string; version: string / null };` populated at src/commands/ls.ts:135 from `entry.shared` / `entry.placement`, which are already in scope. Plus ONE read-only producer in src/lib/skills.ts, beside canonicalDigest (:87): `export async function sharedState(source: string, repoDirectory: string / undefined, baseline: string / undefined): Promise<'in-sync' / 'local-edit' / 'repo-ahead' / 'diverged' / 'not-connected'>`, factored out of the three lines at src/commands/connect.ts:275-276, :291 and :295 so the reconciler and the read path share ONE definition instead of two.
- **Verb or flag:** none - the drawn `Share` button is `connect <path>` (src/cli.ts:119) and the two resolution buttons are `--keep-source` / `--keep-repo` on that same registration; all three exist.
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: 'carries the SharedRef team and the PlacementRef version on the row while the printed sentence is byte-identical'; src/lib/__tests__/skills.test.ts: 'sharedState agrees with reconcileShared on all five baseline combinations, including a missing baseline'; src/commands/__tests__/connect.test.ts: 'reconcileShared decides through sharedState' (the regression that keeps the two from drifting); src/commands/__tests__/sync.test.ts: 'the set of teams writeStamp is called for is unchanged by the sharedState refactor' (the D-R4 guard on the refactor)
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 6, the `ls` bullet at :343 - ONE paragraph. Section 5.3 is CITED, not amended: the comparison is relocated byte-identical, so 5.3's decision procedure text does not change.
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** `ls --local` additionally returns, per row, the ledger references it already resolved - each `shared` entry's {id, team} and the `placement`'s {id, team, version} - and, for connected rows, a machine-local `sharedState`: `in-sync` (both digests equal the baseline), `local-edit` (only the source moved), `repo-ahead` (only the repository copy moved), `diverged` (neither equals the baseline, or no baseline is recorded), `not-connected` (no shared entry). The PRINTED OUTPUT IS UNCHANGED: the state sentences above remain the contract, and the structured fields exist so a host need not parse them. `sharedState` is the same comparison section 5.3's reconciler makes, factored out and read-only - it digests with the existing canonicalDigest, and it never writes, never pushes, never quarantines and never touches `config.shared[].baseline`; the reconciler calls it so the two cannot drift, and section 5.3's decision procedure is unchanged by the relocation. It reads two folder trees per connected row, which is filesystem work only: `ls --local` remains no git, no network, no prompt, no write. `diverged` is advisory until the user runs `connect --keep-source <id>` or `connect --keep-repo <id>`, which remain the only resolutions; `repo-ahead` is NOT advisory - the next sync overwrites the local source and moves the previous one to quarantine - and a host must render it as a pending destructive action, not as a quieter `in-sync`.
- **Why SIMPLE:** No owner-file field, no verb, no flag, no guard, no team.json row, no privacy call, no eval-engine change. Tests fit src/commands/__tests__ and src/lib/__tests__; ONE paragraph to ONE section (5.3 is cited, not amended, because a behaviour-preserving relocation does not change 5.3's text); one approver. THE COSTER'S SAFETY CLAIM WAS FALSE AND IS RESTATED: it said the change "touches no sync/stamp path". It does. reconcileShared's `defer` populates `incomplete` (src/commands/sync.ts:63), and src/commands/sync.ts:289 skips writeStamp (:291) for any team in `incomplete` - so a sharedState that disagreed with the inline logic in even one case would change WHICH TEAMS GET STAMPED, i.e. D-R4's one-hour hook suppression (src/lib/hook.ts:149; gap-4.md:59). The refactor is behaviour-preserving by construction and the fourth test above is the guard; the safety must be asserted that way, not by denying the path exists.
- **App side:** HARD (D20 ok) — Either (a) hash the source folder and the clone copy itself and compare against config.shared[].baseline, or (b) parse stateOf's prose sentence to recover the team and the state.
- **Cites:** `src/commands/ls.ts:19,94-110,106,135 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/local-skills.ts:8-9,14 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/connect.ts:16,231,255,273-277,291-318,311-318,312,313,318 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/skills.ts:87 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:63,154,289,291 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:97 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/hook.ts:149 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:119 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:343 @ b5c0507`, `commit 71e7a0a`, `gap-4.md:59 (D-R4)`, `build.py:3390,3391,3716-3724,3726-3727,3729-3741,3731,3851-3853,4438,4447`

#### RM-45 — Git-transport access failure is a separate class from the gh sign-in: no probe is permitted and no fix-it button may ship  *(recommended: both; approver: none for the CLI (nothing changes). Ryan only if the OPTIONAL tightening below is taken.)*

- **Boards:** LibraryError, InboxError, ShareError, MarketplaceError, Settings
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `explainGitAccessFailure(remote, stderr)` at src/lib/remote.ts:204 (doc :203, "Pure diagnostics for known git access failures; callers supply the actual transport URL") is the only diagnosis and it is after-the-fact. It classifies not-found / credentials / push-denied / ssh-denied (:207-210), branches non-GitHub -> "Access is managed on that host; ask a team admin to grant you access" (:212-213), GitHub-SSH -> key access (:219-220), and GitHub-HTTPS -> the three-cause explanation plus the host-scoped remedy `gh auth setup-git --hostname github.com` at :232, :234 and :239, each closing with "This changes global Git credential configuration for github.com and its gist host." The copy is carried as a STRUCTURED, TYPED member, not only as prose: `RemoteAccessError` (src/lib/teamRepo.ts:66-71) has `readonly explanation: string` at :67 and its constructor folds it into .message; the four producers are src/lib/teamRepo.ts:96-97, :171, :350-351 and :478-479. src/lib/runner.ts:20 sets GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT: '0' on every non-inherited run, so a missing credential always fails and never prompts. The prohibition is SPEC TEXT: "The tool never changes credential configuration itself, offers to do so, probes access, or retries with different credentials." - .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:336 (the catalogue's :333 is `team create` and drifted). status disclaims readiness: "exit 0 means the query succeeded, not a setup-readiness or membership test" (src/cli.ts:97; spec :345). Nothing at b5c0507 probes git access; ghState (src/lib/auth.ts:21-29) probes only `gh`, the other half. THE ONE PLACE THE COPY DOES NOT RIDE THE RESULT (refuter A's F3, which I reopened and confirmed): on a NON-HOOK `sync`, src/commands/sync.ts:119-121 routes the RemoteAccessError into `notice(...)`, which pushes to `notices` and prints (:71, `if (!args.hook) io.print(line)`), and the run then returns `failure('Sync finished with N team(s) skipped: ... See the notices above.', args.hook ? { placed, deferred, notices, changed, hook: true } : undefined)` at src/commands/sync.ts:293 - the payload is UNDEFINED for a non-hook sync. notices ride the Result only on the success path (:294) and in hook mode.
- **Field:** none
- **Verb or flag:** none
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** none named
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 6, the `team join` remote-url bullet at :336 - CITE, do not amend.
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** None for the CLI. The app-side rule to record (a frontend rule, no spec change): "The git-transport access failure is a distinct class from the gh sign-in and is the case two of the four drawn `Open settings` buttons actually land in. The app renders explainGitAccessFailure's text as returned - as selectable text with a copy button on the `gh auth setup-git --hostname github.com` line - and does nothing else: it never probes access, never offers to run that command, never writes credential configuration, and ships no Fix-it button; spec :336 forbids all four, and the command rewrites global Git credential config for github.com and its gist host. Settings > Account's `Access to team repos` row stays read-only for the same reason: there is no predicate the CLI is permitted to evaluate, so a live status there would be a claim the product cannot make. On the `sync` path the app must capture the Prompter's print stream, not the failure string: for a non-hook sync the failure payload is undefined and the message says 'See the notices above', so an app that renders the failure string alone renders a sentence pointing at notices it does not have."
- **Why SIMPLE:** The diagnosis exists, is complete across all four transport classes, is carried as a typed member on RemoteAccessError (src/lib/teamRepo.ts:67) as well as folded into .message, and is regression-pinned character-for-character by src/lib/__tests__/remote.test.ts:213-237. The prohibition is likewise already written and binding (spec :336). Zero CLI work is REQUIRED. One OPTIONAL tightening is worth naming because refuter A found the hole and I confirmed it: attach the existing payload on the non-hook failure too - change `args.hook ? {...} : undefined` at src/commands/sync.ts:293 to pass the payload unconditionally, since `notices` is already a SyncResult member (src/commands/sync.ts:32). That edit is itself SIMPLE (no owner file, no verb, no flag, no guard, one paragraph to section 6's `sync` bullet, Ryan) and it removes the app's dependency on stream capture. It is not required: an in-process caller supplies the Prompter and therefore already receives every notice through `io.print` (src/commands/sync.ts:71) - implementing the existing interface, not a new Prompter member (src/lib/prompt.ts) - and a spawned bin captures stdout.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — On a failed `sync` / `team join` / `publish`, render the diagnosis the CLI already composed - the git detail line and the explanation paragraph - in the existing centered_state + error_line shape, with a copy button on the `gh auth setup-git --hostname github.com` line and NO button that runs it. Settings > Account's `Access to team repos` row stays a read-only sentence. Two obligations the coster's version missed: (a) SOURCE - on `team join` / `publish` / safeWrite take it from the failure string (the explanation is folded into RemoteAccessError.message, src/lib/teamRepo.ts:66-71), but on a non-hook `sync` take it from the captured print stream, because the failure payload is undefined there (src/commands/sync.ts:293); (b) D-R4 - share_error :3219 and market_error :3358 make `Sync now` the PRIMARY action, and every press writes run/<team>.stamp (src/commands/sync.ts:291, :347) and silences the session-start hook machine-wide for an hour (src/lib/hook.ts:149; src/commands/sync.ts:106; gap-4.md:59), so it is user-intent only and must never be auto-retried.
