#### RM-47 — CloneState's four states have no drawn surface, and it is the likeliest cross-window failure  *(recommended: both; approver: none for the CLI; Teddy for the six copy variants)*

- **Boards:** LibraryError, InboxError, MarketplaceError, ShareError, SettingsTeams
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** The payload is already on an existing Result and already discriminated. `TeamStatus.clone: CloneState` at src/commands/status.ts:18. `CloneState` is the five-arm union at src/lib/teamRepo.ts:427: `{state:'absent'} / {state:'incomplete'; reason:'not-a-repository'/'no-team-json'/'unverifiable'; error?} / {state:'foreign'; origin} / {state:'ok'; origin}`. `describeClone(root, normalized, runner)` is the single definition at src/lib/teamRepo.ts:445 (doc :437-444: "an interrupted `team leave`, a restore that skipped dotfiles"), with probeOrigin at :430-435. status prints a repair line per state at src/commands/status.ts:52-59 - the unverifiable case advises checking git and explicitly does NOT advise moving the directory (:55), the others print `Restore it: [move <clone> aside, then run ]<invocation> team join <remote>` (:57-58). Two facts the seed did not have and I re-verified: (1) src/commands/status.ts:39 initialises detail.clone to {state:'incomplete', reason:'unverifiable'} BEFORE any probe, so the field is never absent; (2) the whole payload rides the FAILURE path too - `return lines.length ? failure(lines.join('\n'), { version, teams }) : success({ version, teams })` at src/commands/status.ts:100, and the outer catch at :101 also attaches it - so the app can render clone state even when status exits 1, which is exactly the cross-window case. setup re-clones an absent clone silently (src/commands/setup.ts:157-158) and refuses on incomplete (:164) / foreign (:166) with the move-aside instruction, from the same function. Spec :345 pins the recovery advice.
- **Field:** none
- **Verb or flag:** none
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** none named
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 6, the `status` bullet at :345 - CITE, do not amend.
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** None for the CLI. The app-side rule to record: "`TeamStatus.clone` is a five-arm discriminated union and the app renders one full-surface state per arm, never a generic error: `absent`, `incomplete/not-a-repository`, `incomplete/no-team-json`, `incomplete/unverifiable` (the origin probe threw; advise checking git and do NOT advise moving anything), and `foreign` (a clone of a different remote, naming that origin). Each carries status's own repair line as the secondary action. `absent` does NOT mean 'another window left this team': a completed `team leave` deletes the config entry (src/commands/leave.ts:114) and status iterates config.teams (:33), so a left team VANISHES from the payload. The cross-window-leave signal is 'a team that was in the last payload is not in this one', which the app computes by diffing two StatusResults; `absent` means an interrupted leave, a hand-deleted folder, or a restore that skipped it. A SIXTH surface is required beside the five arms: a per-team read failure with `clone.state === 'ok'` and `readable:false`, which is the state the drawn ShareError already depicts. The app must read `clone` from the payload on the FAILURE Result as well as the success one (status.ts:100, :101), because a broken clone is precisely the case that makes status exit 1, and it must never infer clone state from a failing read of its own."
- **Why SIMPLE:** The value already exists, is already typed as a union, is already on StatusResult, survives the failure path, and its repair copy is already composed per arm. Zero CLI work; both refuters independently confirmed CLOSED and I re-verified every line. The remaining cost is copy over the existing centered_state plus one unhealthy variant of the Settings > Teams `Clone` row.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — Switch on TeamStatus.clone.state (and .reason) and render one of FIVE full-surface states, each with the CLI's repair line as the secondary action and, for `foreign`, the reported origin - PLUS a sixth, generic per-team-read-failure surface for the clone.state === 'ok' + readable:false case. Separately and legitimately: detect a cross-window `team leave` by diffing consecutive StatusResults for a team key that disappeared, since that case produces no CloneState at all.
- **Cites:** `src/commands/status.ts:18,33,39,52-59,55,57-58,60,90-97,92-95,94,100,101 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/teamRepo.ts:427,430-435,437-444,445 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/leave.ts:110,114,115,116,117 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/setup.ts:157-158,164,166 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:345 @ b5c0507`, `build.py:728,759-761,787-792,2214-2219,3215-3221,3355-3359,3567`

#### RM-48 — No read verb returns the last-sync timestamp, and three drawn surfaces print one  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan (phase-1 spec section 6 / section 8) - the SAME single approval as RM-41)*

- **Boards:** InboxEmpty, SettingsTeams, SettingsSync, Library, MarketplaceHome, Share, ProjectDetail, PersonDetail
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** The datum is on disk TWICE and reaches no surface. `writeStamp` writes `new Date().toISOString()` as the file BODY and stamps its mtime at the same instant (src/commands/sync.ts:347), called per team at :291 after that team's sync fully succeeded - and WITHHELD when the team has undone work (:289, `skipped // incomplete // !Object.hasOwn(final.teams, team)`) or any remaining pending entry (:290, `if (final.pending.some((entry) => entry.team === team)) continue;`). On the read side the mtime is read and DISCARDED: `stampIsFresh` stats stampPath(storeRoot, team), takes details.mtimeMs, and returns a BOOLEAN (src/lib/hook.ts:165-168), with STAMP_FRESH_MS = one hour at :149 and the future-skew rule STAMP_SKEW_MS at :151 applied in the same expression at :166; `staleLine` returns a sentence or null (:171-174). `TeamStatus.stale` is a boolean (src/commands/status.ts:21, set at :86 from :85) and StatusResult is {version, teams} (:23). stampPath itself is exported (src/lib/hook.ts:157) but only within the process. Two facts that matter for the delta and that I re-verified: (1) staleLine and `stale` are computed INSIDE the clone-ok branch (src/commands/status.ts:60 opens the else, :85-86 sit inside it), so a team with an absent, incomplete or foreign clone reports stale: false - a latent wrong claim the app would inherit verbatim; (2) `search` computes the same staleness independently at src/commands/search.ts:62 via the same staleLine, pinned by spec :344. D-R4: an app-initiated sync writes the stamp and suppresses the machine's next session-start sync for an hour (src/commands/sync.ts:106; src/lib/hook.ts:149; gap-4.md:59), so the freshness READ is free but the Sync-now button beside it on all three boards is the D-R4 surface.
- **Field:** No zod line - the value is an mtime on run/<team>.stamp, machine-local recovery state under section 4.2, never a schema field and never committed. One TypeScript member on TeamStatus at src/commands/status.ts:21: `syncedAt: string / null;` - populated from a new sibling of stampIsFresh in src/lib/hook.ts, beside stampPath (:157): `export async function stampedAt(storeRoot: string, team: string): Promise<string / null> { try { const details = await stat(stampPath(storeRoot, team)); return details.isFile() ? new Date(details.mtimeMs).toISOString() : null; } catch (error) { if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === 'ENOENT') return null; throw error; } }`. Set it in src/commands/status.ts BEFORE the clone.state branch at :52 - NOT beside `stale` at :86 - so a broken clone still reports when it last synced. `stale` is NOT touched: it keeps its current placement inside the :60 else and its current meaning.
- **Verb or flag:** none
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/lib/__tests__/hook.test.ts: 'stampedAt returns the stamp mtime as ISO and null when the stamp is absent'; src/lib/__tests__/hook.test.ts: 'stampedAt reports a future-dated stamp verbatim while stampIsFresh still calls it not fresh'; src/commands/__tests__/status.test.ts: 'reports syncedAt for a team whose clone is absent, incomplete and foreign'; src/commands/__tests__/status.test.ts: 'syncedAt is null and stale is true when no stamp exists'; src/commands/__tests__/status.test.ts: 'stale keeps its current placement and value: a broken clone still reports stale false'
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 6, the `status` bullet at :345 - the SAME one paragraph as RM-41, per gap-4.md:707 (OD-4 option (a)).
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** See RM-41's spec_delta: OD-4 decided `syncedAt` and `pending` together as option (a), one spec change to section 6's `status`, so the two rows share ONE paragraph and ONE Ryan approval. The `syncedAt` half reads: "`status` additionally returns, per team, `syncedAt: string / null` - the ISO timestamp of run/<team>.stamp's last modification, or null when no stamp exists - read from the same stat the stale notice already performs and populated BEFORE the clone is described, so a team with an absent, incomplete or foreign clone still reports when it last synced. `stale` is unchanged: it keeps its current placement inside the readable-clone branch and its current meaning, and this addition makes no claim about it. `syncedAt` is reported VERBATIM, including a stamp dated in the future - the skew rule that makes such a stamp not fresh belongs to `stale` and must not silently rewrite the timestamp. The value means 'this team's last FULLY SUCCESSFUL sync': the stamp is written per team only after that team's run completed with no deferred work and no remaining pending entry (section 8), so it is not a claim that anything changed, and null does not mean 'never synced' - `team leave` deletes the stamp with the rest of the team's run artifacts, so a rejoined team reads null while being perfectly current. A host renders it as a freshness disclosure, never as a success receipt. `syncedAt` lands on `status` only; `search` continues to report staleness as its existing one-line notice and gains no timestamp."
- **Why SIMPLE:** No owner-file field (schema.ts is untouched), no verb, no flag, no guard, no team.json row, no privacy call, no eval-engine change. Nothing is WRITTEN: stampedAt stats a file the code already stats, so the read cannot interact with D-R4 - an app SYNC writes the stamp, an app READ does not. Tests fit src/lib/__tests__ and src/commands/__tests__; one paragraph to one section, shared with RM-41; one approver, shared with RM-41. This is gap-4.md:707 OD-4's own recommended option (a). D-R4 STILL APPLIES TO THE ROW, on the button rather than the field: all three drawn surfaces put a `Sync now` beside the timestamp (build.py:3568, :3653, and :2207 prints the command), and every press writes run/<team>.stamp (src/commands/sync.ts:291, :347) and silences the session-start hook machine-wide for an hour (src/lib/hook.ts:149; src/commands/sync.ts:106).
- **App side:** FORBIDDEN (D20 ok) — The forbidden half: the app stats ~/.terum/skills/run/<team>.stamp itself (or reads its ISO body, which src/commands/sync.ts:347 writes) and formats the relative string. The legitimate half: formatting the CLI's ISO into '12 minutes ago' is pure presentation and belongs in the app - and the raw `stamp` spelling the design also prints (build.py:3568) is the same field rendered absolutely, so one CLI field feeds both drawn spellings.
- **Cites:** `src/lib/hook.ts:149,151,157,165-168,166,171-174 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/status.ts:21,23,52,60,85,86,100 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/sync.ts:106,289,290,291,347 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/search.ts:62 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/leave.ts:110 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:344,345 @ b5c0507`, `gap-4.md:59 (D-R4), gap-4.md:707 (OD-4 option (a))`, `build.py:331-332,2207,3366-3367,3568,3653`

#### RM-49 — readReadmeData fails whole on one bad skill folder; ls and search degrade per row  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** Library, LibraryError, SkillCard, MarketplaceSkills
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** The catalogue's premise is INVERTED at b5c0507, and I re-derived the inversion independently. (1) readReadmeData (src/lib/readme.ts:126-140) is NOT the Library read model: grep -rn readReadmeData src/ returns only the definition and the hidden README verb (src/commands/readme.ts:5 import, :19 call). Its own doc comment is stale and is the likely source of the whole error — src/lib/readme.ts:125 reads 'used by the hidden workflow command and ls', and ls does not call it. It fails whole deliberately and correctly for a committed derived artifact (readme.ts:132, :134, :135, :136; generateReadme fails closed on marker counts at :97). (2) The fail-whole the app actually hits is ls. listSkills maps folder names inside Promise.all (src/commands/ls.ts:52) with NO try/catch: readFile of skills/<name>/SKILL.md rejects at :53 and :54 throws 'Invalid skills/<name>/SKILL.md'; the rejection escapes await listSkills at :35 into the function-level catch at :46 and returns failure(...) — roster, every other skill and the exit code lost to one leftover folder. (3) ls fails whole on the ROSTER too, which no earlier analysis but refuter B carried: ls.ts:33 calls readPeople(clone) (src/lib/readme.ts:142-145), a Promise.all over people/*.json in which one malformed file rejects everything. (4) Only the git-tree lookup degrades: ls.ts:59 catches latestTree, prints one line and returns '—' under the R11 ruling at :56-58; its test is src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts:42. (5) search already does all of it — skillRecords with onProblem at src/commands/search.ts:35, a per-people-file catch at :37 (its comment at :36 calls the roster 'cosmetic'), a per-hit rejection print at :57, SearchHit.unresolved at :18 set at :58, and the all-rows-failed escalation at :64-68. (6) The per-folder enumerator exists and is exported: skillRecords(clone, team, options) (src/lib/skills.ts:20-39), doc line :19 'Enumerate usable skills without allowing one bad folder to poison the whole team', per-folder try/catch :27 and :34-36, frontmatter check :31, folder-vs-frontmatter name agreement :32, root-ENOENT -> [] at :24, SkillProblem {name,message} at :16. Used by findSkill (:41), endorsedCandidates (:55), search (search.ts:35), status (src/commands/status.ts:79) and ls's OWN --local path (ls.ts:124). Only ls's team path does not use it. (7) status already ships the structured per-row counts (TeamStatus.unreadableMembers status.ts:19 from readRoster :62-65; TeamStatus.unreadableSkills :20 from skillRecords :78-81, printed at :69 and :83) but returns no skills array (sharedSkills is a number, :80), so it cannot serve as the Library read model. (8) Packaging: package.json declares bin only (:14-16) and ships dist (:17-22); there is no exports map, no main, no types, and tsconfig.build.json:4 emits no declaration, so nothing in src/lib is a package export and the app consumes the CLI through the hand-written Backend interface (frontend-stack.md:193-210).
- **Field:** none (no zod field, no committed file). Two TypeScript Result members in one command file: src/commands/ls.ts:18 -> export interface LsSkill { id: string; name: string; author: string; category: string; installs: number; latest: string; endorsement: string; unresolved: boolean; } (mirrors SearchHit.unresolved, src/commands/search.ts:18); src/commands/ls.ts:20 -> export interface LsResult { local?: LocalSection[]; roster: readonly { handle: string; active: boolean }[]; skills: readonly LsSkill[]; problems: readonly { source: string; message: string }[]; } (source is 'skills/<name>' or 'people/<file>.json'; the two problem shapes in the tree, SkillProblem {name,message} at src/lib/skills.ts:16 and readRoster's {file,message} at src/lib/skills.ts:69, do not unify, so one narrow local shape is cheaper than widening either)
- **Verb or flag:** none. src/cli.ts:93-96 (ls, ls member, ls project) is untouched; no new flag and no --json.
- **Guard:** none. src/lib/guard.ts is untouched — no GuardAction (guard.ts:10), no PEOPLE_ACTIONS entry, no team.json row (guard.ts:150-155, rows c/d/e).
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: 'one malformed skill folder costs one row and one reported line, never the roster or the other skills' (the ls twin of src/commands/__tests__/search.test.ts:155); src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: 'one malformed people file costs one roster row and one reported line, and the install counts come from the files that parse' (the ls twin of search.test.ts:163); src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: 'a folder whose frontmatter name disagrees with its directory is reported and skipped in ls, ls member and ls project, as skillRecords and findSkill already require'; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: 'an unreadable skills/ root still fails the whole verb, so an empty team and an unreadable clone are never the same answer'; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: 'LsSkill.unresolved marks only the row git could not resolve and LsResult.problems names every skipped source' (extends the existing case at ls.test.ts:42); src/commands/__tests__/search.test.ts: 'search still prints hits in ls format after LsSkill gained unresolved' (guards the shared formatter at search.ts:60)
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 6 'Command behavior' — the `ls` bullet at :343 (NOT :279-297: :279 is the section-6 heading, :281-289 the ref-form table and :291-297 the update / Discovery / Notice paragraphs, none of them a read verb; `search` is :344, `status` :345). The delta must sit beside the sentence already at :343 — 'Read each referenced team's team.json and skill records once across all sections, caching the result; a failed read or any skipped unparsable folder makes repository status unknown, never missing' — which rules the --local path (ls.ts:124) and must keep doing so.
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** `ls` degrades per row (rev N). The team path of `ls` enumerates skills through `skillRecords()` — the same enumerator `search`, `status` and `ls --local` already use — and reads `people/*.json` one file at a time the way `search` does, so one unreadable, unparseable or misnamed `skills/<name>/` folder and one malformed people file each cost exactly that row and one reported line, never the roster, the other skills, or the exit code. The boundary is the `skills/` root itself: if the root cannot be read the verb still fails whole, because a caller must never be shown an empty team when it is looking at an unreadable clone. `LsResult` gains `problems`, naming every skipped source, and `LsSkill` gains `unresolved`, set exactly as `SearchHit.unresolved` is, so a consumer can tell a version that is legitimately `—` from a row that degraded. Two consequences are deliberate and are behaviour changes on repositories that are healthy today: a folder whose frontmatter `name` disagrees with its directory is now reported and skipped by `ls`, `ls member` and `ls project` — `skillRecords`, the README reader and `findSkill` all already refuse it, so `ls` was advertising skills `install` would not resolve — and a skipped people file no longer hides the roster, though its member's install counts are then absent rather than zero and must be reported as such. The README generator keeps its fail-closed read (section 9): a derived artifact that silently drops a skill is worse than one that refuses to regenerate.
- **Why SIMPLE:** No owner file, no zod field, no verb, no flag, no guard action, no team.json row, no privacy call, no engine change, no hook path (the hook command is sync --hook, src/lib/hook.ts) and no run/<team>.stamp interaction (ls reads no stamp; only search does, search.ts:62). It is two function bodies plus two Result members in one command file, replacing hand-rolled loops with the exported enumerator written for exactly this purpose (src/lib/skills.ts:19), with tests that mirror search's existing ones one-for-one, one spec paragraph in one section, one approver. Every SIMPLE condition holds. The change list is larger than the coster's by four items that are all still inside the bar: the roster read at ls.ts:33 (readPeople is NOT replaceable by readRoster — readRoster drops email, src/lib/skills.ts:66, which ls.ts:68's author join needs, and filters archived members, :79, which ls.ts:34's inactive labelling needs; the fix is a per-file catch shaped like search.ts:37); the FOURTH success site at ls.ts:151 (showLocal), which a required LsResult.problems must also satisfy; the one-line addition of unresolved: hit.unresolved at src/commands/search.ts:60, without which the required member makes that object literal non-assignable to LsSkill and breaks npm run typecheck; and the stale doc comment at src/lib/readme.ts:125.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The Backend adapter (frontend-stack.md:193-210) enumerates per folder and per people file and renders three outcomes, with the rule written down once — degrade the row for anything scoped to one skills/<name>/ or one people/<handle>.json; fail the view only for the skills/ root. (1) root unreadable -> the drawn library_error whole-view (build.py:787-793); (2) one folder skipped -> there is NO card (skillRecords returns no record, so there is no name, description, size or install count to draw) and a per-view notice names it; (3) one folder readable but its version unresolvable -> the card renders, latest is '—', and per R12 (src/commands/search.ts:17) the Install affordance is greyed rather than the dash carrying the meaning.

#### RM-S1 — The Team policy card's Publishing and Skill license values are on no read Result  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** SettingsTeams, SettingsLeave
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** teamSchema parses the whole document and policy is REQUIRED, not optional: "policy: z.object({ publish: z.enum(['pr', 'push']), skill_license: z.string().min(1) }).passthrough()" (src/lib/schema.ts:50, inside the object at :43-51; type Team at :52). readTeam(clone) returns it whole (src/lib/skills.ts:63). NO READ VERB RETURNS IT. LsResult is { local?, roster, skills } (src/commands/ls.ts:20); ls parses team.json on every run (ls.ts:32) but uses only archived (:34) and skillEndorsement (:60). ValidateResult is { name, findings, warnings } (src/commands/validate.ts:12) although validate holds the policy in hand at :24. The ONLY Result carrying policy.publish anywhere is PublishResult (src/commands/publish.ts:32-36, the field at :34, populated at :59) — produced by actually endorsing a skill, i.e. by opening a pull request. skill_license reaches NO Result: it is consumed inside publish (:63, :102), connect (connect.ts:191, :199, and the private readTeamPolicy at connect.ts:422 which throws away everything but license), eval (eval.ts:90), validate (validate.ts:40) and hygiene (hygiene.ts:15, :84) — never returned. Grep confirms no other module in src references TeamStatus or StatusResult at all.
- **Field:** NO new zod line — teamSchema already carries the block at src/lib/schema.ts:50 and it is required. The added line is one TypeScript member on the existing non-committed interface at src/commands/status.ts:17-22:   policy: { publish: 'pr' / 'push'; skill_license: string } / null; initialised `policy: null` in the detail literal at status.ts:38-41 (alongside the existing memberCount: null, sharedSkills: null, membership: null), and assigned `detail.policy = teamJson.policy;` immediately after the readTeam call already made at status.ts:70. Zero extra I/O and zero new failure mode: the document is already read, already validated, and the assignment sits inside the clone.state === 'ok' branch (status.ts:60-89) whose catch (status.ts:90-97) leaves the null in place.
- **Verb or flag:** none — status is registered as-is at src/cli.ts:97 with its single --team option; no commander change, no new printed line.
- **Guard:** none — this is a read. GuardAction (src/lib/guard.ts:10) and PEOPLE_ACTIONS (guard.ts:36) are untouched, no safeWrite is reached, and the §6.0 permitted-writes table is not approached.
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/status.test.ts: 'returns the team policy block on TeamStatus and adds no printed line'; src/commands/__tests__/status.test.ts: 'leaves policy null for an absent, foreign, incomplete or unreadable clone'
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6 command behavior, the `status` bullet at line 345 (one section, one paragraph).
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** `status` additionally returns, per team, the team document's policy block on `TeamStatus` as `policy: { publish: "pr" / "push"; skill_license: string } / null`, read from the same `readTeam(clone)` the archived membership check already performs, and `null` whenever the clone is not readable — exactly like `memberCount`, `sharedSkills` and `membership`. Nothing new is printed: the §6 `status` stdout contract is unchanged line for line, and the constraint "only one `git remote get-url origin` probe per readable team" still holds because no read is added. The field exists so a UI can show what the team decided for everyone (the publishing gate and the skill license) without opening `team.json` itself, which ledger D23 / default 40 forbid the frontend from doing. It is a read: `team.json` is not written, the §6.0 permitted-writes table is untouched, and no guard action changes.
- **Why SIMPLE:** All six SIMPLE conditions hold, each verified at the pin. (1) No new field in any owner file — the change is a member on a non-committed Result interface and the zod line already exists (schema.ts:50). (2) No new verb and no new flag (cli.ts:97 unchanged). (3) No guard change and no team.json row (guard.ts:10, :36 untouched; this is a read). (4) Two tests in the existing src/commands/__tests__/status.test.ts, which already builds a fixture clone and asserts on result.value.teams[0] plus io.lines. Every existing TeamStatus assertion in that file uses toMatchObject (:55, :68, :77, :112, :128, :146, :157) and the single strict toEqual on a Result (:102) has teams: [], so a new member churns no existing test. (5) One paragraph into one section (spec:345). (6) One approver, Ryan — grepping the phase-1 spec for StatusResult/TeamStatus/LsResult/PublishResult returns zero hits, §3 (:59) fixes only run(args, io) and the Prompter's four methods plus `interactive` (not Result shapes), and the eval-engine §12 UI contract (2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:478-498) governs receipt JSON only, so Ajay is not a second approver. status is the right target over ls on cost and on precedent: status already holds the parsed document at :70 so the marginal cost is one literal key plus one assignment, whereas ls would need the value at three success sites (ls.ts:45, :73, :81); and gap-4.md:707 (OD-4) already ruled the identical read-model question for the sibling fields on the SAME drawn card and picked "(a) add both to StatusResult — a spec change to §6's status". Sequencing, not cost: a Result member is invisible until the seam exists — on the recorded O2 sidecar the down-frame is Result<T> verbatim (tauri-feasibility.md:854) and package.json declares only bin (:14-16) and files (:17-22) with no exports/main/types, so this ships as dead code until RM-11 (packaging) lands. That punt is booked as its own row in catalogue.final.json.
- **App side:** FORBIDDEN (D20 no) — The app would open <config root>/teams/<team>/team.json itself (the path ConfigStore.teamClone(team) resolves, src/lib/config.ts:10-19), parse it in TypeScript, and render policy.publish and policy.skill_license into the two rows at build.py:3576-3577.

#### TJ-04 — Admin status column needs a live GitHub API call (offline contradiction)  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** Share, ShareLight, ShareStates, ShareLoading, ShareEmpty
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** Nothing reads admin status outside the removal mutation. `grep -rn "permissions.admin" src/` at b5c0507 returns exactly src/commands/team.ts:104 plus fixtures in src/commands/__tests__/remove.test.ts; the admin LIST call is src/commands/team.ts:108, reached only after the self-removal check (:79), the `hostOperationAllowed` gate (:80, src/lib/remote.ts:266-272), the origin/main fetch (:85), the target's people file (:87-88), the admin refusal (:106) and `!args.archiveOnly` (:107) — unreachable except by removing someone. `ls` prints `handle` and ` (inactive)` only (src/commands/ls.ts:40-41 @ b5c0507) and `LsResult.roster` is `{ handle, active }` (:20). `status` says so out loud: `io.print('  From the local clone; GitHub access is not checked.')` (src/commands/status.ts:61 @ b5c0507), then `Members: <n>` and up to five `@handle — Display Name` lines (:66-68). So the design's Status column has NO read path at all — not merely no offline one. Spec §6.0 anchors why (:328), and on a generic-git remote there is 'no portable admin predicate' (:330). Ledger D21 (:134) requires the UI to work offline.
- **Field:** none. `LsResult` (src/commands/ls.ts:20 @ b5c0507) gains `hostRole?: 'admin' / 'member' / 'unknown'` and `hostCheckedAt?: string` on its roster entries — a widening of an in-process RETURN TYPE that is never serialized to disk, not a field in an owner file.
- **Verb or flag:** ONE new flag on ONE existing verb: append `.option('--host', 'also check GitHub for admin permission and pending invitations (GitHub remotes only; needs gh)')` to the `ls` command chain at src/cli.ts:93 @ b5c0507, and add `host?: boolean` to `LsArgs` (src/commands/ls.ts:17 @ b5c0507). Refuse it beside the two guards already there: `if (args.local && args.host) throw new Error('--local lists your machine, not a team roster; drop --host.')` at src/commands/ls.ts:25-26 (showLocal returns `roster: []`, :151, so --host would have nothing to mark). Body is a lift of code that already exists: `gh api repos/{ownerRepo}/collaborators?permission=admin --paginate --slurp` (the shape at src/commands/team.ts:108), parsed with the existing `paginatedItems` helper (:147-151), matched case-insensitively against each roster person's `github` field the way :110 already lowercases logins, degraded with the existing `explainGhFailure` (src/lib/auth.ts:157).
- **Guard:** none. `ls` performs no `safeWrite` — 'read-only team inventory; it deliberately neither pulls nor prompts' (src/commands/ls.ts:22 @ b5c0507) — and `GuardAction` (src/lib/guard.ts:10 @ b5c0507) is untouched.
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: "--host marks admins from collaborators?permission=admin and everyone else Member"; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: "a member whose github is '' is marked unknown, never Member"; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: "--host on a generic-git remote prints the §6.0 host message and still prints the roster"; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: "--host without gh prints the explainGhFailure line, omits the column, and ls still exits 0"; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: "--host on a 403 from collaborators?permission=admin omits the column rather than failing"; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: "--host cannot be combined with --local"; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: "without --host the roster is byte-identical to today and makes no gh call"
- **Spec section:** ONE section: .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6, the `ls` bullet at :343 (beside the host-scoped paragraph at :328).
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** "`ls [--host]` — the inventory is offline by default and unchanged. `--host` adds one live GitHub read, `repos/{owner}/{repo}/collaborators?permission=admin`, and marks each roster handle whose `people/<handle>.json` `github` login appears in it as Admin and every other active member with a non-empty `github` as Member, printing the moment the check was made. A member whose `github` is empty (§5.2 permits it for a generic-git member) is marked unknown, never Member — the CLI does not infer a role from a missing identity. Admin is never stored: it is a host capability (§6.0) and the host is the only thing that enforces it, so the CLI reads it and forgets it. On a generic-git remote, without a logged-in `gh`, or with the call refused or failing for any reason — including a 403 to a non-admin caller, which GitHub and not the CLI decides — `--host` prints the §6.0 host message or the `gh` failure and the roster without the column; `ls` still exits 0 and never prints a remembered or inferred role. `--host` is not combinable with `--local`. Nothing about `ls` without `--host` changes, and no `ls` invocation writes anything."
- **Why SIMPLE:** All six SIMPLE conditions hold and both refuters re-verified them at b5c0507: (1) no new field in any owner file — the widening is on `LsResult`, an in-process return type (src/commands/ls.ts:20); (2) exactly one new flag on one existing verb (src/cli.ts:93); (3) no guard change at all (src/commands/ls.ts:22, src/lib/guard.ts:10); (4) `src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts` exists; (5) one paragraph to one section (:343); (6) one approver. The gh call, its error message, its pagination parser and its degradation helper all already exist (src/commands/team.ts:108, :109, :147-151; src/lib/auth.ts:157). It does not break D21: `ls` stays offline by default and `--host` degrades to the offline roster rather than failing. IMPORTANT: this is the SAME single flag TJ-05 costs and the SAME flag TJ-02's chip renders — three rows, one change.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The app renders `ls --host`'s output: an Admin/Member/unknown chip with the moment of the check, and an explicit 'not checked' state when the flag degraded. It caches nothing that it would show as a role later, and it never shells out to `gh` itself — a second implementation of an authorization read would diverge from `hostOperationAllowed` (src/lib/remote.ts:266-272 @ b5c0507) and from the generic-git message the spec fixes at :330.
- **Cites:** `src/commands/team.ts:79 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:80 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:104 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:106 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:107 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:108-110 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:147-151 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:17 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:20 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:22 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:25-26 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:40-41 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:151 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/status.ts:61 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:93 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/auth.ts:157 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:60 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/remote.ts:266-272 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/guard.ts:10 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:328 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:330 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:343 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:555 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:133 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:134 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:136 @ b5c0507`, `design build.py:3009`, `design build.py:3046-3047`, `design build.py:3058-3063`, `design build.py:3194`

#### TJ-05 — Pending invitations list ('invited 2026-09-05 by ryan')  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** Share, ShareLight, ShareInvite, ShareStates, ShareEmpty, ShareLoading
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `invite` writes nothing to the team repo — 'It deliberately has no team-repo write path' (src/commands/invite.ts:13 @ b5c0507); its only effect is `gh api -X PUT --include repos/{endpoint}/collaborators/{login}` (:29) plus printed output (:33-42). So a pending invitation exists ONLY on GitHub. The read already exists in the codebase: `gh api repos/{ownerRepo}/invitations --paginate --slurp` (src/commands/team.ts:115 @ b5c0507), error-checked at :116, parsed through `paginatedItems<{ id?: number; invitee?: { login?: string } }>` (:117, helper :147-151) — but only inside `team remove`, behind the self-removal check (:79), the host gate (:80), the fetch (:85), the people-file resolution (:87-88), the admin refusal (:106) and `!args.archiveOnly` (:107), and consumed only to DELETE the invitation (:130-134). Neither `ls` nor `status` reads it: `LsResult.roster` is `{handle, active}` over people/*.json only (src/commands/ls.ts:20, :33-34 @ b5c0507) and `status` prints 'From the local clone; GitHub access is not checked.' (src/commands/status.ts:61 @ b5c0507). Offline there is nothing to show, by construction. (A second, unrelated invitations read exists for the invitee's own side — `gh api user/repository_invitations` in `team join`, src/commands/team.ts:496 @ b5c0507 — which is not this list.)
- **Field:** none. `LsResult` (src/commands/ls.ts:20 @ b5c0507) gains `invitations?: { login: string; invitedBy: string / null; invitedAt: string / null }[]` — an in-process return type, never serialized.
- **Verb or flag:** THE SAME single flag as TJ-04 — `.option('--host', …)` on the `ls` chain at src/cli.ts:93 @ b5c0507, `host?: boolean` on `LsArgs` (src/commands/ls.ts:17). Body: lift src/commands/team.ts:115-117 @ b5c0507 into a read (dropping the `.filter(...)` at :117 that narrows to one login) and widen the parse type from `{ id?: number; invitee?: { login?: string } }` to also carry `inviter?: { login?: string }` and `created_at?: string`, both OPTIONAL. Printed after the Members block at src/commands/ls.ts:41. The read must NOT inherit the admin precondition at src/commands/team.ts:106 — GitHub decides what a non-admin may see; a 403 omits the section rather than failing the verb.
- **Guard:** none. `ls` performs no `safeWrite` (src/commands/ls.ts:22 @ b5c0507) and `GuardAction` (src/lib/guard.ts:10 @ b5c0507) is untouched.
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: "--host lists pending invitations with inviter and date"; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: "--host renders 'invited by —' when the payload has no inviter or created_at"; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: "--host on a 403 to repos/{o}/{r}/invitations omits the section and ls still exits 0"; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: "--host on a generic-git remote prints the host message and no invitations"; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: "a paginated invitations response is flattened by paginatedItems"; src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts: "invitations are listed as invitations and never counted in the roster"
- **Spec section:** ONE section: .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md §6, the `ls` bullet at :343 — the same paragraph TJ-04 amends, next to `invite` (:339) and the host-scoped paragraph (:328).
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** "Pending invitations are host state, not repo state: `invite` adds a collaborator on GitHub and writes nothing to the team repository (D9), so an invitee has no `people/<handle>.json` until they join and choose their handle. `ls --host` therefore lists them from `repos/{owner}/{repo}/invitations` — the same call `team remove` already makes to cancel them — printing each invitee's GitHub login, with the inviter's login and the invitation date only when GitHub supplies them and an em dash when it does not. They are listed as invitations, never as members, and never counted in the roster. The read carries no admin precondition of its own: GitHub decides what a non-admin may see, and a refusal omits the section rather than failing the verb. On a generic-git remote, without `gh`, or offline, the list is absent with the §6.0 host message; the CLI never remembers an invitation between runs."
- **Why SIMPLE:** All six conditions hold: (1) no owner-file field; (2) one flag, SHARED with TJ-04 — this row costs nothing extra once that flag exists; (3) no guard change; (4) `src/commands/__tests__/ls.test.ts` exists and `src/commands/__tests__/remove.test.ts:29 @ b5c0507` already carries a paginated invitations fixture to copy the shape from; (5) one paragraph; (6) Ryan. The call, its error message and its pagination parser all exist at src/commands/team.ts:115-117 and :147-151. HONESTY CAVEAT I will not paper over, raised by the coster and independently confirmed by both refuters and by me: the CLI's parse type at src/commands/team.ts:117 narrows to `{id, invitee.login}` and the only in-repo fixture is `[[], [{ id: 9, invitee: { login: 'member-gh' } }]]` (src/commands/__tests__/remove.test.ts:29), so THIS REPOSITORY DOES NOT PROVE that `inviter` and `created_at` are present. They are GitHub's documented repository-invitations schema, not a fact verifiable at b5c0507; an implementer must confirm against one live response and must render an em dash rather than a guess. That is why both fields are optional in the parse type, why the spec paragraph says 'only when GitHub supplies them', and why the missing-fields case is a named test.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — The app renders `ls --host`'s invitation list and nothing else. The tempting app-local move — recording 'I invited sortiz on 2026-09-05' when the user presses Invite — is FORBIDDEN: it is a second source of truth for a team-wide fact, it goes stale the instant the invitee accepts, another admin's `team remove` cancels it (src/commands/team.ts:130-134 @ b5c0507), or GitHub expires it, and it is per-machine, so a second admin's app would show a different roster.
- **Cites:** `src/commands/invite.ts:13 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/invite.ts:29 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:79 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:80 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:106 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:107 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:115 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:116 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:117 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:130-134 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/team.ts:147-151 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/__tests__/remove.test.ts:29 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:17 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:20 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:22 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:33-34 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/ls.ts:41 @ b5c0507`, `src/commands/status.ts:61 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:88-92 @ b5c0507`, `src/cli.ts:93 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/schema.ts:60 @ b5c0507`, `src/lib/remote.ts:266-272 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:328 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:339 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:343 @ b5c0507`, `.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:52 @ b5c0507`, `design build.py:2364`, `design build.py:3051`, `design build.py:3067`, `design build.py:3078`, `design build.py:3168`, `design build.py:3179`, `design build.py:3199`

#### TJ-10 — Project description and icon are drawn everywhere; team.json has neither field  *(recommended: both; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** MarketplaceProjects, MarketplaceProject, Marketplace, MarketplaceStates, MarketplaceProjectNotInstalled
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** `teamSchema.projects` is `z.record(z.string(), z.object({ remotes: z.array(z.string()), skills: z.array(skillIdSchema) }).passthrough())` (src/lib/schema.ts:48 @ b5c0507) - no declared description, no icon; the section 5.1 worked example is the same two keys (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:160 @ b5c0507). No verb creates or edits a project entry: `TeamArgs` is `create / join / remove / workflow-update` (src/commands/team.ts:29 @ b5c0507, the fourth arm added by 4149287 and print-only with no write path, src/commands/team.ts:60-62 @ b5c0507), `team create` scaffolds `projects: {}` (src/commands/team.ts:523 @ b5c0507), and `publish --project` throws `Unknown project <name>` for a key not already present (src/commands/publish.ts:56,171 @ b5c0507). So the `projects` object is hand-maintained on the host today. THE DECISIVE FACT the coster walked past: section 5 opens with "Zod-validated on read; unknown fields preserved on write." (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:149 @ b5c0507), and the code keeps that promise - the project entry is `.passthrough()` (src/lib/schema.ts:48), and every one of the four `team.json` writers re-serializes the PARSED document: `endorse` returns `JSON.stringify(fresh, ...)` (src/commands/publish.ts:176 @ b5c0507, written at :108), `archiveMutation` writes `{ ...team, archived: [...] }` (src/commands/team.ts:165 @ b5c0507), the rejoin writes `{ ...teamDocument, archived: ... }` (src/commands/team.ts:420-421 @ b5c0507), and the fourth is the empty-repo scaffold (src/commands/team.ts:525 @ b5c0507). Row (c) does not object either: `onlySkillListsChanged` scrubs `global`/`projects` via `sameExcept` and then compares each project entry MINUS `skills` (src/lib/guard.ts:175-183 @ b5c0507), so an unchanged extra key is byte-identical on both sides; rows (d) and (e) compare `projects` wholesale under `sameExcept(..., ['archived'])` (src/lib/guard.ts:187,192 @ b5c0507) and are unaffected. Nested passthrough retention is the tested convention (src/lib/evals/receipt.ts:2-3, src/lib/evals/__tests__/receipt.test.ts:76-79, src/lib/__tests__/auth.test.ts:118-122, src/commands/__tests__/uninstall.test.ts:215-217 @ b5c0507).
- **Field:** none - `description` needs NO schema line: spec:149 already promises unknown fields are preserved on write and src/lib/schema.ts:48 @ b5c0507 is `.passthrough()`. (If Ryan prefers it DECLARED - `description: z.string().optional()` inside the project object at src/lib/schema.ts:48 - that is MEDIUM, breaking only the family rule that a team.json schema field is never SIMPLE; it still needs no verb, no guard change and no section 6.0 row.)
- **Verb or flag:** none
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/lib/__tests__/schema.test.ts: a project entry keeps a hand-maintained `description` through teamSchema.parse and through a re-serialization (pins spec:149 for team.json, which today is pinned only for receipts and config); src/lib/__tests__/guard.test.ts: row c admits a publish into a project that carries a `description`, and still refuses a publish that CHANGES it (extends the 'rows c, d, e - team.json' describe at src/lib/__tests__/guard.test.ts:69 @ b5c0507)
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 5.1 (spec:151-167, the worked example at :154-164)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** **`team.json projects` entries are `{remotes, skills}` plus whatever the team hand-maintains (rev 13).** The two keys the tool reads and writes are `remotes` and `skills`: row (c) of the section 6.0 table permits `publish` to change `projects[].skills` and nothing else inside an entry, and no verb creates or removes a project key at all - `publish --project` refuses a key that is not already present and `team create` scaffolds `projects: {}` - so the `projects` object is hand-maintained on the host through GitHub's own editor (a clone push carrying that change is refused by the pre-push guard, section 6.0). Because the schemas preserve unknown fields on write (section 5), an entry may carry additional descriptive keys written by hand alongside the rest of the entry - `description` is the one phase 2 renders. They parse, they survive every write (each writer re-serializes the parsed document), and row (c)'s comparison ignores them as long as they do not change, so `publish` is unaffected. They are documentation for humans and for the UI, never a constraint: nothing validates them and no verb writes them. The phase-2 UI renders `projects[<key>].description` read-only when it is present and renders nothing when it is absent; it never stores a description of its own, because a project description is team-wide truth and the UI holds no second source of truth (ledger D20). A project's icon is not a repository field at all: the UI derives it deterministically from the project key, the way it derives a person's initials from `display_name`.
- **Why SIMPLE:** The coster costed this HARD as a declared team.json field (schema line + verb + two guard branches + a new section 6.0 row) and offered only a lossy design removal or a MEDIUM `gh api ... -q .description` network read. Both are more expensive than the path the coster's own evidence establishes and then walks past. Since NO verb creates a project key, an admin is already hand-writing every entry on GitHub; one more line in that same edit costs nothing on our side and is a first-class, spec-blessed shape (spec:149). Against the bar: no owner-file field (nothing is added to teamSchema), no verb, no flag, no guard change, no section 6.0 row, tests in the existing vitest layout, one paragraph to ONE section (5.1), one approver. Two honesty notes rather than hidden costs: (a) the hand edit must be made in GitHub's web editor, because a clone push carrying it is refused by the pre-push guard (src/lib/guard.ts:88-90 @ b5c0507 via src/commands/guardPush.ts:56-60 @ b5c0507) - the same out-of-band route TJ-12 already names, and TJ-10 and TJ-12 must not be given opposite treatments for one mechanism; (b) the key is undeclared, so it reaches the app as `unknown` and the app must type-check and escape it before rendering.
- **App side:** SIMPLE (D20 ok) — Two separable things. (1) DESCRIPTION: render `projects[<key>].description` from the team clone's team.json when the key is present, render nothing when it is absent. The sentence lives in the repo, every teammate reads the same one, the app stores nothing - a pure render of committed truth, which is the role D20 assigns the UI (ledger:133). (2) ICON: derive it deterministically from the project key (a fixed map or a hash into the existing icon set), exactly as the app derives a person's initials from `display_name` (src/lib/schema.ts:56 @ b5c0507) where the mock hardcodes `initials` (build.py:981-986).

#### TJ-11 — Cancelling a pending invitation is unreachable: team remove needs a people file the invitee does not have  *(recommended: cli; approver: Ryan)*

- **Boards:** Share, ShareStates, ShareInvite, OnboardingInvite, OnboardingStates
- **CLI today @ b5c0507:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:340 @ b5c0507 says `team remove` "cancels any pending invitation", and it does: it lists `repos/{ownerRepo}/invitations --paginate --slurp` (src/commands/team.ts:115-117 @ b5c0507) and DELETEs each match (src/commands/team.ts:130-134 @ b5c0507, the DELETE at :132). But it can never reach that code for a pending invitee: it resolves the target first with `git show origin/main:people/<handle>.json` and throws `No member <handle> in <team>: people/<handle>.json is not on origin/main.` (src/commands/team.ts:87-88 @ b5c0507) - precisely a pending invitee's state - and the invitation filter is keyed on the `github` login read out of that same file (src/commands/team.ts:94-99 @ b5c0507, matched at :117), so there would be nothing to match on. The verb is also handle-keyed by signature (src/commands/team.ts:78 @ b5c0507). There is NO cancel verb: `invite <github-login...>` registers with only `--team` (src/cli.ts:88-92 @ b5c0507) and `invite` has one code path, `gh api -X PUT --include repos/<owner>/<repo>/collaborators/<login>` (src/commands/invite.ts:29 @ b5c0507), under a header saying "It deliberately has no team-repo write path" (src/commands/invite.ts:13 @ b5c0507). PARTIAL CLOSED: two of the three affordances the design needs already exist. Re-running `invite <login>` re-issues the PUT (GitHub's own re-invite) and unconditionally re-prints the Slack-ready join block (src/commands/invite.ts:42 @ b5c0507, built at :53-55) - so RESEND and RE-COPY THE JOIN BLOCK are already there; only CANCEL is missing.
- **Field:** none
- **Verb or flag:** exactly one new flag on an existing verb. In src/cli.ts, on the `invite` block at :88-92 @ b5c0507, add `.option('--cancel', 'withdraw the pending invitations for these logins instead of sending them')`; the action at :92 already spreads `...options`. In src/commands/invite.ts:10 @ b5c0507, `export interface InviteArgs extends WithForm { logins: readonly string[]; team?: string; cancel?: boolean; config?: ConfigStore; runner?: Runner; }`, and extend `InviteResult` (src/commands/invite.ts:11 @ b5c0507) with `cancelled: readonly string[]; notPending: readonly string[];`.
- **Guard:** none
- **Tests:** src/commands/__tests__/invite.test.ts: --cancel deletes the pending invitation for a GitHub login, including one on page two of the paginated list (reuse the page-two invitations fixture at src/commands/__tests__/remove.test.ts:29 @ b5c0507); src/commands/__tests__/invite.test.ts: --cancel reports a login with no pending invitation on notPending and still cancels the remaining logins; src/commands/__tests__/invite.test.ts: --cancel refuses a generic remote before it invokes gh (mirrors invite.test.ts:45 @ b5c0507); src/commands/__tests__/invite.test.ts: --cancel refuses without repository admin permission, with team remove's message (mirrors the probe at src/commands/team.ts:104-106 @ b5c0507); src/commands/__tests__/invite.test.ts: --cancel says gh is missing or logged out instead of blaming the invitation cap (mirrors invite.test.ts:52 @ b5c0507); src/commands/__tests__/invite.test.ts: --cancel does not print the Slack join block
- **Spec section:** .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md section 6, the `invite` bullet (spec:339)
- **Spec delta (paste-ready):** **`invite --cancel <github-login>...` (rev 13).** The same GitHub-only verb also withdraws an invitation it sent: with `--cancel` it lists `repos/{owner}/{repo}/invitations` and DELETEs every invitation whose invitee login matches, printing one line per login - cancelled, or no pending invitation for that login - and it writes nothing to the team repository, in either direction. Withdrawal is the same authority as revocation, so it runs the same host gate `team remove` runs (section 6.0): `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo} -q .permissions.admin`, refusing on anything but `true`. On a generic-git remote it fails before any host call exactly as the send path does, because there is no invitation to cancel (section 6.0). It is keyed on the GitHub login because that is all a pending invitation has - the invitee has no `people/<handle>.json`, and therefore no handle, no display name and no role, until they join - and that is also why `team remove <handle>` cannot do this job: `team remove` is handle-keyed and resolves its target through `people/<handle>.json` on `origin/main`, failing before any host call when that file is absent. `team remove` is otherwise unchanged and remains the departure path for a member who has joined. `InviteResult` gains `cancelled` and `notPending` alongside `invited` and `already`, so the phase-2 UI (D21) renders the outcome from the `Result` rather than from the printed lines; under `--cancel` the Slack-ready join block is not printed, and neither is the 50-per-day invitation cap, which is a send-path failure.
- **Why SIMPLE:** Every ingredient already exists in the repository: the invitation listing (src/commands/team.ts:115-117 @ b5c0507), the DELETE (src/commands/team.ts:132 @ b5c0507), the admin probe (src/commands/team.ts:104-106 @ b5c0507), the paginated-response reader (`paginatedItems`, src/commands/team.ts:147-151 @ b5c0507), the generic-remote refusal (src/commands/invite.ts:20-21 @ b5c0507 via `hostOperationAllowed`, src/lib/remote.ts:266-272 @ b5c0507), the gh-failure explanation (src/commands/invite.ts:32 @ b5c0507) and the login validation (src/commands/invite.ts:28 @ b5c0507). `invite` is the right home precisely because it is the LOGIN-keyed verb (spec:339: "Takes GitHub logins, not team handles"); `team remove` is handle-keyed and people-file-gated. Against the bar: no owner-file field, one flag on one existing verb, no guard change, tests in the existing src/commands/__tests__/invite.test.ts, ONE paragraph to ONE section, one approver. Two mechanical notes that break no bar condition: `paginatedItems` is module-private at src/commands/team.ts:147 @ b5c0507 and must be exported or lifted to a lib; and the listing+DELETE block currently sits inside `remove()`'s admin-gated branch, so it is lifted, not copied.
- **App side:** NA (D20 ok) — The app calls the flag; it does not reimplement it. It could shell `gh api -X DELETE repos/{owner}/{repo}/invitations/{id}` itself and D20 would not bar it - nothing is invented and no truth is stored - but spec:59's library-first rule sends it through the same function the CLI calls, so the UI's Cancel and `invite --cancel` are provably one code path.
