## 3. The SIMPLE set — app-side (46 rows) and design-side (58 rows: 57 design + 1 cut)

### 3.1 App-side: what the app does alone, and why D20 allows it

- **AC-01** Theme toggle (dark / light / system); FigmaDark is not a theme — D20 ok. One stored value in the app's own preference store (tauri-plugin-store, tauri-feasibility.md:567+; window geometry rides tauri-plugin-window-state, tauri-feasibility.md:563-564). Four sentences in the spec: (1) storage - app-owned, per machine, not synced, not in ~/.terum; (2) domain - exactly System / Light / Dark (build.py:3885), System live via prefers-color-scheme; (3) pre-paint - a synchronous root-attribute stamp before first paint, plus one tauri.conf background_color default AND a runtime setBackgroundColor call for the other theme (the config field is build-time only; the runtime setter is verified on the JS Window class); (4) FigmaDark is generator-only. Resolve T-D as gap-8 recommends (b). Tests (app repo): theme.test.ts: the stored choice survives a restart; theme.test.ts: System repaints when prefers-color-scheme flips; theme.test.ts: the root attribute is set before first paint; theme.test.ts: figma is not a selectable value; theme.test.ts: System resolves to a concrete theme under the gate. *Why:* D20 (.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:133 @ b5c0507) binds roster, profiles, skills, eval results and usage selections. A palette is none of those and is per-machine chrome by nature, so D20 does not reach it. No count, membership, follower, last-seen or favourite value is invented. The design reaches the same conclusion independently at build.py:3751.
- **AC-02** Hide overview / remembered panel and rail state — D20 ok. One app-owned preferences record (tauri-plugin-store) plus tauri-plugin-window-state for geometry. Fields: overview_open (bool), rail ('auto'/'open'/'closed'), open_at_start (enum), sidebar_counts (bool), footer_machine_name (bool), and a decision on the panel-left glyph (build.py:247) - wire it as sidebar_collapsed or drop it from the design. Three rules: per-machine and never in ~/.terum; every value is a bit/enum/geometry and never a cached number; an unreadable store yields the drawn defaults. Tests (app repo): prefs.test.ts: an unreadable store yields the drawn defaults; prefs.test.ts: an explicit rail choice survives a narrow window and a restart; prefs.test.ts: rail='auto' collapses below 1400 and re-opens above it; prefs.test.ts: sidebar_counts hides the count and does not persist the number. *Why:* Collapsed panels, a remembered start view and a footer toggle are exactly the 'per-machine chrome (unread dots, collapsed panels, theme)' the bar names as app-side by nature. The one place this brushes team truth is the counts behind build.py:3747, and the bit/number split keeps them derived, so no second source of truth is created (.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:133 @ b5c0507).
- **AC-03** Bell / desktop notifications — D20 ok. Four app-local values in the AC-02 preferences record (badge_on_bell, kinds_off, sidebar_rows, a per-machine seen set) and one derived count. Refresh is a read, never a sync. The 'review' kind is displayed without a count until a source exists. Tests (app repo): bell.test.ts: the badge counts only kinds that are on; bell.test.ts: marking an item seen decrements the badge and persists; bell.test.ts: an item that vanishes from the clone leaves no phantom unread; bell.test.ts: the review kind renders with no count and no network call; sync.test.ts: no code path calls sync on a timer. *Why:* An unread dot is the bar's own named example of per-machine chrome, so D20 (.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:133 @ b5c0507) does not reach it; the items and their facts stay derived from committed files and nothing team-wide is invented. The honesty risk is calling this 'unread by the team' when it is 'unseen on this machine' - the design already sets the precedent for saying so at build.py:3697 ('Follows are kept by this app on this machine...').
- **AC-05** i18n: none, recorded explicitly — D20 ok. One paragraph (D-W3 verbatim) plus one attribute plus one small module. Concretely: <html lang="en"> on the shell; one relative-time formatter (Intl.RelativeTimeFormat, baseline since Sep 2020 per gap-8.md:347+) taking an injectable clock, used by every list row and report; a rule that no user-facing string is assembled from fragments; and a note that iso()'s ISO-8601 dates stay as they are. strings.ts extraction is explicitly NOT the ask. Tests (app repo): time.test.ts: every relative time comes from the formatter and none is re-parsed from a rendered string; time.test.ts: the fixture clock is injected and the boards render deterministically at a fixed instant; a11y.test.ts: the shell carries lang="en". *Why:* Vacuously D20-honest - nothing is presented as team truth. The value is that an unrecorded non-goal reads as an oversight and gets re-raised every review; recorded with D-W3's three obligations it also fixes a WCAG Level A failure and a formatter that cannot survive its own inputs.
- **AC-10** No route table, history stack, deep links or keymap behind the drawn navigation — D20 ok. Publish the six decisions in the spec_delta above: route inventory (specimen sheets excluded), per-window history stack, focus-scoped keymap including the wizard, versioned route-id vocabulary with a fallback, no deep links in phase 1 per D25, and the CMD-R / D-R4 rule. *Why:* All six are app rules over per-window or per-machine state; none presents team-wide truth, so D20 (.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:133 @ b5c0507) does not reach them. The one thing that is NOT chrome is CMD-R: it is a D-R4 mechanism (gap-4.md:59), and the rule that keeps it honest - call the verb, user-intent only, no repeat, no timer - is written into the same decision rather than left implicit.
- **AC-12** user-select: none on the whole frame - hashes, skill names and commands cannot be selected — D20 ok. One app rule, and it is negative work: do not port the frame-root declaration; scope it to chrome. Nothing is built. *Why:* A rendering rule with no stored value, explicitly admitted as SIMPLE by the bar ('an app rule'), and it agrees with gap-8.md:439 word for word.
- **AC-13** The generator's contrast gate must be ported as a test, or hover and dim states break AA silently — D20 ok. One test file over the app's token values, asserting the 80 pairs x 2 themes and growing with every invented state. A cheaper variant is legitimate and should be weighed: point the EXISTING Python gate (build.py:4266) at a single exported token file so there is one implementation rather than two - strictly less code, at the cost of coupling the app's CI to the design generator. Either way the input must be the app's tokens, never a duplicated palette; that is the load-bearing half. *Why:* No value is displayed to a user, so D20 does not engage. It is one test over token values, which the bar admits as an app rule / app-side derivation.
- **AC-14** Font pipeline: Inter is fetched from Google Fonts at render time, and Berkeley Mono is commercial and never loaded — D20 ok. One bundled variable woff2 plus a local @font-face, the CDN link dropped, and one mono-stack decision that becomes normative. The "sequence it BEFORE baseline capture" advice (gap-8.md:465-467) is spent: the `.shots` were re-rendered under patch_aa and boards are already being locked against them. *Why:* A bundled font file is app-local by nature and displays no team value, so D20 does not reach it; it positively satisfies D21's offline clause. *(Premise superseded after the snapshot, on BOTH halves: `MONO` is now JetBrains Mono, loaded from Google Fonts like Inter, at `build.py:175` — patch_aa, `BRIEF.md:13`; and the app's bundling is pinned by `BRIEF.md:44` to `@fontsource-variable/inter@5.3.0` (importing `standard.css`, not the default import) plus `@fontsource-variable/jetbrains-mono`, i.e. bundled from npm with no Google Fonts CDN link — which is exactly what this row's app side asks for. Nothing to decide; row and verdict kept as the evidence record.)*
- **AC-15** Icon set: adopting a Lucide package silently redraws a large minority of the drawn glyphs — D20 ok. One typed constant file (53 paths, 6,911 bytes) plus a one-file ISC notice. Do not take the library. *Why:* No value is displayed, so D20 does not engage. The cheap-looking alternative is the expensive one: the library silently redraws 21 glyphs including chrome visible on nearly every board, breaks fidelity against the .shots acceptance set, and - per gap-8.md:465-467 - a different icon set is one of the three things that can make the score row collide at 1440 on a design with 16 px of slack.
- **AC-16** The fixture clock is hard-coded (TODAY = 2026-09-06); every relative time is computed against it — D20 ok. One now() provider plus one relative-time module (D-W3 obligation 2), frozen at 2026-09-06 in fixtures, tests and every ported gate; relative strings formatted from ISO instants and never stored. *Why:* A formatting function over an instant the CLI already supplies invents no value, so D20 does not engage - provided the three carve-outs above stay carved out. The honest urgency: the generator is already frozen and time-invariant (no live clock anywhere in it); the hazard is created BY THE PORT, the moment a mock layer formats pinned 2026-08/09 fixture dates against the real system clock and '8 days ago' silently becomes 'n months ago', or a gate ported with a live clock goes red on a date no one changed.
- **AC-17** The desktop app's own version identity, and an update channel drawn as absent — D20 ok. Two things, and only two. (1) The app reads its OWN version from its own bundle at build time (the Tauri config version surfaced through the app handle) and renders it in the two drawn rows — build.py:3775 (About, value column) and build.py:3671 (Updates, caption). (2) The "The app" group on SettingsUpdates ships exactly as drawn: caption "<version> · no update channel yet.", value "—". No updater, no channel, no check, no background request. Zero owner files, zero verbs, zero guard changes. SCOPE CORRECTIONS applied to the coster's change list, both found independently by refuters A and B and verified here: (a) the About/Updates CLI version pair (build.py:3667 "0.1.2 installed · 0.1.3 available", :3776 value + Check, :3673/:3784 the notes) is NOT AC-17's — it is RM-08's, and corrections.settings.json's RM-08 entry names exactly those two drawn consumers. Recorded here so the seam is not lost: the honest route is the exported library, not update's stdout — createReleaseState (src/lib/update.ts:36 @ b5c0507), describeUpdate (src/lib/update.ts:159 @ b5c0507) and packageVersion (src/lib/package.ts:25 @ b5c0507), which is what src/commands/update.ts:15/:19/:20 @ b5c0507 themselves call; update's output is unstructured prose composed at src/commands/update.ts:24-33 and printed line by line at :34, and no verb has a --json flag at b5c0507 (grep -rn -- '--json' src/ returns only gh sub-invocations at src/commands/publish.ts:200 and src/commands/team.ts:262 plus test fixtures). (b) The Agent CLI row (build.py:3777) is not free either: the only producer at b5c0507 is preflight() (src/lib/evals/agent.ts:208-223 @ b5c0507), which reads ccVersion at :216 and then runs a real paid agent task at :219, inside the eval-gated engine — so the app must spawn `claude --version` itself. Per-machine and D20-clean, but not AC-17's row. *Why:* D20 (.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:133 @ b5c0507) asks whether the value is presented as TEAM-WIDE truth. It is not: the app's version is a fact about this binary on this machine, the same class as Platform (build.py:3778, MACHINE["os"]) and Agent CLI (:3777) — per-install chrome, which the bar puts app-side by nature. There is no repo field it can contradict and no second source of truth it can create, because the team repo has no notion of the shell. The honesty risk is not D20 but src/lib/package.ts:7's rule: the drawn string carries a build number ("(build 12)", build.py
- **CP-16** Hash length: app renders 12 chars; README shows 8; both binding — D20 ok. One app rule with one corollary, and the corollary is free. The app renders twelve characters everywhere and never normalises a hash it received as text: it derives its own short form by slicing the forty-character value it holds — placements[].version / installed[].version (src/lib/schema.ts:100, :38), the receipt's version, or latestTree(runner, clone, name), which is an EXPORTED library function returning the full forty characters from git rev-parse HEAD:skills/<name> (src/lib/readme.ts:119-122, imported by src/commands/ls.ts:12). Corollary: the app must not source a version from ls or search OUTPUT, because those have already been through shortHash (src/commands/ls.ts:60, src/commands/search.ts:58) and eight characters cannot be widened back to twelve — including for a marketplace row on a skill this machine has not installed, where no ledger or people-file value exists. Scope honestly: twelve on the eval card is §12's (.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:491, scoped by :481-482); twelve on the Inbox row, the rail command and the person page is Teddy's rule, adopted here for consistency. *Why:* A derivation over committed data with one source of truth (the forty-character hash), rendered at the length the spec that owns the card already fixes. Nothing per-machine and nothing invented — and the un-installed case needs no CLI change, because latestTree is already exported.
- **CP-20** Staleness disclosure on the happy path — the app inherits the CLI's sentence; the last-sync VALUE is not this row's — D20 ok. One rule, needing zero new reads: every surface derived from a team clone carries the staleness disclosure on its HAPPY path, not only when it errors, and it inherits ls.ts:150's sentence verbatim rather than writing new copy — 'Team status is from local clones and may be stale; open endorsement requests are not checked.' *Why:* A copy rule over data the app already renders; nothing is derived and nothing is stored, so D20 is not engaged — the rule exists precisely to keep the app from presenting clone data as live team truth. Two cautions to write into it, for whenever the NUMBER arrives (RM-48): freshness and display are different values — the hook gates on the stamp file's mtimeMs (src/lib/hook.ts:166) while its content is the ISO instant (src/commands/sync.ts:347), and they diverge when run/ is copied or the clock steps (src/lib/hook.ts:160-164) — and an absent stamp means 'no fully successful sync recorded' (src/
- **CP-21** The Install dialog's one confirm hides the create-or-join wizard on a zero-team machine — D20 ok. One routing rule plus one relabel, as gap-1 §5.3 already ruled: the Install button must NOT trigger the inline bootstrap; on a machine with zero teams an install action routes to onboarding, carrying the requested skill as the flow's completion intent. The predicate is the CLI's OWN conjunction, not a team count: zero configured teams AND a ref that names a remote this machine has not joined (src/commands/install.ts:67 with :203-206) — a bare or two-part ref on a zero-team machine is a different error and must not be routed to onboarding. *Why:* A branch over machine-local config (src/lib/schema.ts:91-101) plus button copy. It asserts nothing team-wide, so D20 is not engaged. Its cost is one condition and one label — PROVIDED the destination exists, which it does not yet (see design_consequence). Two things the rule must state rather than discover later: gap-1 §5.3's own text also requires the completion intent to survive the flow (cross-screen state plus a post-onboarding install action), and the app must supply the questions itself, because it cannot run the inline bootstrap headlessly at all — PromptClosedError fires before questio
- **CP-25** Share divergence "Keep mine" / "Keep the team's" moves the user's source folder, with no confirm on either side — D20 ok. Both ghost buttons open a dialog() (the component exists, build.py:1546-1553, and its docstring calls it "the desktop rendering of a Prompter confirm") before the app runs the verb. "Keep the team's" names the exact source folder — read from config.shared[<id>].source (src/lib/schema.ts:97) — and the destination ~/.terum/skills/quarantine/<timestamp>/<name> (src/lib/placer.ts:170-172), and says nothing is deleted and nothing restores it (only sync prune empties quarantine, .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:369). "Keep mine" names the team repository and says it commits (src/commands/connect.ts:357, :359). After the verb returns the app shows the verb's own line (src/commands/connect.ts:364) as the receipt — not as an undo. *Why:* Per-machine confirmation over two paths the app reads from its own config.json plus one constant path; no team-wide value is asserted, so D20 does not reach it, and it needs no CLI change. One dependency the row must not hide: the app can only draw the Diverged row at all if it can compute the §5.3 three-state, and no read-only verb exposes it at b5c0507 — ls --local returns tracking prose (src/commands/ls.ts:94-109) and status carries no per-skill health (src/commands/status.ts:17-22); the comparison lives inside connect's reconciler (src/commands/connect.ts:273-278). That is RM-39's cost, no
- **CP-26** "Re-place from team" maps to no exact verb, and cannot repair the case it is drawn for — D20 ok. One app rule, and it is the whole of the remaining work. The button runs plain `sync`, never `install <ref>`. It must disclose two things the drawn report does not: (a) the repair is MACHINE-WIDE — sync re-places every drifted placement in the ledger loop (src/commands/sync.ts:160-242), not just this skill; (b) an app-initiated sync writes run/<team>.stamp (src/commands/sync.ts:347) and thereby suppresses the SessionStart hook's own sync for the next hour (src/commands/sync.ts:106; D-R4, gap-4 §12). It must also carry sync's own tools y/N through (src/commands/sync.ts:189-190) and must NOT claim the re-place will restore a file the team copy never had — that is unknowable at b5c0507 and build.py:2068 is the correct hedge. *Why:* Pure command routing plus a disclosure; every value comes from the machine's own ledger, so D20 does not reach it. The alternative routing the coster proposed (install <ref>) is forbidden here, not merely dispreferred: it commits to the team repository (src/commands/install.ts:128-137) and moves the placement between "Follows the team" and "Pinned" (:89, :122-124) — the exact pair Settings ▸ Updates draws as the user's choice (build.py:3662-3663).
- **CP-28** The Library toolbar's Filter has no facet set, and the card ⋯ menu is drawn on no board — D20 ok. Define the Library's own facet set over what a placement plus the clone actually carries: Scope (global / each project, src/lib/schema.ts:26-29), Team, Updates (Follows the team vs Pinned, from version !== null, src/lib/schema.ts:100), Category and Author (frontmatter, src/lib/schema.ts:107-113), Verdict (committed receipt, carrying src/lib/readme.ts:196-199's rule that a partial receipt is never promoted), Placed (a range over placed_at), and Installed-by ≥ N teammates (installCounts over the clone's committed people files, src/lib/readme.ts:40). Define the ⋯ menu as verbs that exist: Open in editor (app-local), Copy install command (src/cli.ts:122), Re-place from team (plain `sync`, src/cli.ts:125, per CP-26), Run eval (src/cli.ts:116), Publish (src/cli.ts:108), Remove (src/cli.ts:123). EXCLUDED by name, each for a different reason: State / In sync / Local edit / Missing files (no read-only classifier and no reference scanner); Favorites (0 hits in src/); Disabled (no mechanism). And "Pin to this version" / "Follow the team" must not be offered as metadata toggles — install <ref>@<version> and install <ref> both re-place the folder (src/commands/install.ts:121) and commit the actor's people file (:128-137) and may re-ask consent (:96); either label them for what they do or leave them out. *Why:* Every facet in the reduced set reads a committed file or the machine's own ledger, so D20 is satisfied. Installed-by is the one that needed settling: it is not an app-local count — it is installCounts' own rule (src/lib/readme.ts:40) re-read from the same committed people files the README is generated from, and the Library card already draws the number (build.py:685). Filtering by a number you already display, by the same rule, is one source re-read, not a second source of truth. The excluded facets fail for lack of a source, not for D20.
- **CP-41** No board for “the item you were reading is no longer here” in the Inbox master/detail — D20 ok. On any refetch that invalidates the Inbox query, if the selected id is absent from the new derivation, keep the detail pane mounted and swap its body for one centred state; do not move the selection and do not open another row. Copy states the observation, never a cause — title “This item is no longer here”, body “It is not in the list any more. Nothing here was changed.” ONE action, “Back to the list”. No “Sync now”: an app sync writes run/<team>.stamp and silently suppresses the SessionStart hook for an hour (gap-4.md:59 D-R4; hook.ts:157/:165 @ b5c0507), and D-R5 would then require rendering the SyncResult (gap-4.md:60) — all of that for a state whose trigger is a purely local re-read. The rule composes with gap-4 §9.2 guard 1: a dialog open over the pane holds the invalidation until it closes, so the state renders only once no dialog holds a snapshot. *Why:* D20: the value is “the row you had selected is not in the list I just re-derived” — this window's last render versus the current read of committed files. No count, membership, follower, last-seen or favourite; nothing team-wide is asserted and no second source of truth is created. Per-window view state, like collapsed panels and unread dots. Passes. Note the cause is genuinely unknowable to the app: the same absence is produced by a terminal `install`, a terminal `team leave` (gap-4.md:720 G4), or the user's own Settings ▸ Inbox kind toggles (INBOX_KIND_TEXT build.py:3675-3683, toggles :3687) 
- **CP-43** No “this cannot be installed by command” state for generic-git remotes and non-conforming names — D20 ok. One DEGRADE-ON-DOUBT rule over committed files, no new data: render an install command only when the app can confidently read `config.teams[<team>].remote` (src/lib/schema.ts:72) as a github.com owner/repo AND the folder name matches /^[a-z0-9]+(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*$/ with length ≤ 64 (the whole of src/lib/schema.ts:19). Anything it cannot parse gets the no-command state. The two reasons are shown distinctly because the remedies differ: a non-GitHub remote is a permanent property of the team (nothing the user can fix in the app); an illegal folder name is a repo defect someone can rename. The predicate is RE-IMPLEMENTED app-side, not imported (see cli_side): `isSkillName` is a one-line regex; the GitHub test is a prefix check the app must make conservative, because `githubOwnerRepo` composes `normalizeRemote` (remote.ts:148-155, the module's own “one place that decides what a remote is”, remote.ts:1-8) across scp-form, `file:`, credential stripping and host case-folding — so any remote the app cannot normalise with certainty degrades rather than guesses. *Why:* D20: the value is “there is no command for this skill”, derived from two committed inputs — this machine's own config.json remote and the skill's committed folder name. No count, membership, follower, last-seen or favourite; no second source of truth, since the app computes the same predicate from the same bytes the CLI reads. Degrade-on-doubt bounds the drift the re-implementation introduces: a false negative shows no command (recoverable and honest), and the dangerous false positive — a printed command that resolves to a repo this machine never joined and dies in NotJoinedError — cannot be p
- **EV-04** Report prose (abstract / results / coverage / trigger_text / efficiency_text) — D20 ok. Five string templates in the app, one per drawn paragraph, each a pure function of one parsed Receipt (plus per_case when EV-03 lands). Every slot is a receipt field printed at the receipt's own precision - sign_p formatted with toFixed(3) exactly as summarize() does (src/lib/evals/stats.ts:43-49, which the design already mirrors at build.py:493-498) - and a template whose field is absent drops its clause rather than inventing a number. Three explicit template rules: no arithmetic (no product, no argmax, no ranking); a comparison between two stored values is allowed and is the only inference permitted; and a hygiene sentence says 'no hygiene errors' plus the warning count, never 'all screens passed'. *Why:* D20 asks whether the app invents team-wide truth. It does not: every number in the rendered sentence is a value some teammate's committed receipt asserts, and the sentence is a presentation of it. The failure mode D20 exists to prevent - the app and the CLI disagreeing about a skill - is impossible when the template has no arithmetic. This is precisely the bar's 'purely app-side derivation over committed files that passes the D20 honesty test', and it is cheaper and more maintainable than five redacted prose fields in the receipt.
- **EV-06** Size row: tokens vs characters ('4.2k tokens - cap 5k' vs HYG6 20,000 characters) — D20 ok. The app computes the label from the committed SKILL.md bytes it already has, using the spec's own method and the CLI's own vocabulary: units = decodeUtf8(skillMd).length - literally JS String.prototype.length on the decoded string, NOT Rust str::len() (UTF-8 bytes) and NOT chars().count() (code points), either of which would silently disagree with CI; tokens ~ Math.round(units / 4); guideline 20_000 / 4 = 5_000. Rendered with an explicit approximation marker and without the word cap - 'SKILL.md ~4.2k tokens - 20,000-code-unit guideline' - with the exact code-unit count available on hover, since that is the number HYG6 measures. Second rule, for the Marketplace facet: a threshold cannot be labelled approximate away. The 'Context - at most 5k tokens' facet (build.py:2764) evaluates tokens_of(s) inside filter_matches (build.py:2430-2434) and therefore REMOVES skills from the catalog view; it must filter on the exact code-unit count (with the slider labelled in code units, or in tokens but evaluated on units/4 of the same exact count), never on a parsed display string. *Why:* This is the bar's 'purely app-side derivation over committed files'. D20 holds structurally, not by promise: the input is the committed skills/<name>/SKILL.md in the team clone, identical on every machine; the conversion factor and the counting method are the spec's own (.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:446); and the CLI's own user-facing string already speaks both units ('N characters, M over the 20,000-character guideline (~5k tokens)', src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:95). So every viewer computes the same value and it can never disagree with what the CLI measures. The honesty requirements
- **EV-14** Derived vs stored lift and verdict (design derives from W/L/T; the receipt stores net_lift and verdict) — D20 ok. An app rule, not app code. STORED WINS: render receipt.verdict and receipt.comparisons['candidate-vs-baseline'].net_lift; never recompute either from W/L/T. Port build.py:588-589's gate as a FIXTURE INVARIANT in the app's test suite — for every mock receipt, derived-from-W/L/T must equal the stored verdict and net lift, or the fixture is wrong — so the mock stays faithful without the derivation reaching production code. Four sub-rules, three of them found by the refuters: (i) net_lift is a fraction, and to agree with the CLI report the percent must be formed as (100 * net_lift).toFixed(0) (src/lib/evals/stats.ts:46-47 @ b5c0507), which differs from the design generator's Python round() (build.py:486) at an exact half — 1W/0L/7T gives 12.5, Python banker's rounding gives 12 and toFixed gives 13; (ii) src/lib/evals/stats.ts:47 emits an ASCII hyphen for a negative lift while lift_number (build.py:491) emits U+2212, so the CLI report and the app must not be compared glyph-for-glyph and the app should keep U+2212; (iii) the verdict is the receipt's, including on a partial run, where it is banded over scored rows and must be greyed with the scored/expected suffix (src/lib/evals/receipt.ts:58-60 @ b5c0507, .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:255-256 @ b5c0507); (iv) NO-HEADLINE CASE — src/lib/evals/results.ts:85 @ b5c0507 is `headline ? verdictBand(...) : 'NEUTRAL'`, so `comparisons` may be EMPTY while `verdict` is a real enum value, and that receipt is committable today: `eval <skill> --triggers-only --commit` is permitted (only --working --commit is refused, src/commands/eval.ts:56 @ b5c0507), expectedRows stays 0 (src/commands/eval.ts:126-127 @ b5c0507) and src/lib/evals/results.ts:83 @ b5c0507 maps expectedRows === 0 to execution_status 'complete'. The app must therefore render: no candidate-vs-baseline comparison -> no lift number, no Figure 2, no arm-score or efficiency figures; the stored verdict still shows, labelled as a triggers-only run. *Why:* Rendering the committed value is the opposite of a second source of truth, so D20 is satisfied by construction; re-deriving would be the D20 violation — two places computing the same team-visible number, free to drift.
- **EV-18** Per-arm turns / duration / cost are individually nullable; no board renders a missing one, and the estimate multiplies by them — D20 ok. Four render rules, three of them mirroring results.ts:145-150, and one deletion. (1) Table 2 (build.py:1372): render '—' for any absent value; drop a row that is absent for every present arm; if all three rows drop, replace Table 2 with one sentence ('The agent CLI reported no efficiency figures for this run.') rather than an empty table. (2) The detail header's ROI bar (build.py:1114-1118): the bar LENGTH is a pure cost ratio, so a null cost means no bar — fall back to the '—' bar pair the no-receipt branch already draws at build.py:1109, keeping the duration in the label if it survives; and guard the division (mx > 0) before cc / mx even when both costs are present, because two zero costs divide by zero. (3) The Run-eval dialog (build.py:1606) keeps the countable half and DELETES the priced half: '6 cases × k=3 reps × 3 arms, 54 agent runs on sonnet from this machine' is a recitation of provenance.cases (receipt.ts:82), provenance.k (:81) and provenance.arm_skill_lists (:83) describing work the app is about to trigger; 'roughly N minutes and about $M of API spend' is a number derived from receipt means and goes. (4) The Run-eval button tooltip (build.py:1737, eval_estimate_tip) loses the same two figures. *Why:* Rules 1 and 2 are pure suppression — rendering an honest absence is the opposite of inventing a value, so D20 (2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:133) does not bite, nothing app-local is stored, and the rule is a copy of the CLI's own (results.ts:145-150). Rules 3 and 4 remove a claim, which never needs anyone's permission. Deleting the priced half is what makes the row SIMPLE: the coster's version kept minutes and dollars and gave them a null branch, which cannot be written without first settling whether the UI may compute them at all — and §12:480 says it may not derive new statistics, with no
- **IB-01** Inbox production model: recomputed-at-sync vs append-only local log; stable ids; 'acted on' — D20 ok. The app recomputes the whole item set from the clone + config.pending + config.placements + evals/ on open, on clone file-watch, and after a user-initiated sync. It never syncs on a timer (D-R4, gap-4.md:59) and never calls `sync --hook` (a fresh stamp makes hook mode return changed:false, notices:[] with no fetch — src/commands/sync.ts:106 @ b5c0507). It persists exactly one app-owned file in the Tauri app-data directory: { "<identity-id>": { "seen_at": "<iso>", "evidence": "<hash>", "dismissed": true/false } }, plus the per-kind toggles and the sidebar-grouping choice Settings already draws (build.py:3687, :3690). Entries whose id is absent from the recomputed set are garbage-collected. *Why:* The stored values are unread, dismissed, kind toggles and sidebar grouping — per-machine chrome, which the bar's D20 test explicitly does not reach. Nothing team-wide is stored: no counts, no members, no last-seen, no favourites. The design already ships the honest wording for exactly this shape at build.py:3697 ('Follows are kept by this app on this machine. The team repo has no place for them yet, so another machine starts from nothing.'), so the same sentence covers unread.
- **IB-04** Activity feed (pushes, installs, evals, favorites) — D20 ok. One read model per skill, merged and sorted by timestamp: (a) `git log --follow --format=%H%x09%aI%x09%an -- skills/<name>` on the clone, subjects classified as ^<handle>: (connect/share/update/rename/eval), with `git show --stat` for the changed filenames the design prints -> push/connect events; (b) `git log -p -- people/*.json` on the clone -> install and uninstall events at commit precision (NOT a scan of the current installed[] array, which is a snapshot); (c) every receipt under evals/<id>/ -> eval events by provenance.runner_handle at provenance.timestamp with verdict, and net_lift/sign_p read straight off `comparisons` (src/lib/evals/receipt.ts:37-38 @ b5c0507) rather than recomputed. All read-only, all against committed content. The 30-day window (build.py:2112) is a filter, not stored state. *Why:* Passes D20 by being a derivation over the repo, not a second store: an app-local log of events the app itself noticed would be forbidden (two machines would disagree about team-wide history), while git history, the people files and the receipts ARE the first source. Every event class has a durable append-only home already — git history for pushes, the people-file commits for installs, immutable timestamped receipts for evals (.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:248-252 @ b5c0507). Nothing is persisted by the app.
- **IB-07** 'Eval finished' Inbox kind — D20 ok. On each open (and on clone file-watch), enumerate evals/**/*.json in the clone; every path absent from the machine's watermark set is a finished eval. Parse it against the receipt schema, drop it if invalid, and render the report from the receipt alone, per §12. The item's stable id is the receipt path itself — immutable by construction — so IB-01's identity-id rule needs no hashing here. For an app-invoked run the path comes back directly: `EvalResult.receiptPath` (src/commands/eval.ts:50, :202 @ b5c0507). LOCAL runs are shown ONLY if `--write-receipt` lands; otherwise they are out of the kind, because the app must not re-aggregate run.jsonl. *Why:* The content is committed team truth read straight from the repo — the strongest D20 position in the batch, and .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:480 @ b5c0507 makes it the sanctioned one. The only stored value is the per-machine watermark of which receipts this machine has already shown, i.e. unread state, which the D20 test does not reach. A reader cannot race a writer: receipts are append-only single-file writes through safeWrite and the guard proves it (src/lib/guard.ts:63 @ b5c0507 refuses any eval mutation that is not exactly one brand-new path), so a reader either sees a complete
- **IB-09** Inbox list header draws search / filter / sort with no menus and no item query model — D20 ok. Three pure in-memory operations over the composed item list (IB-01's model), plus one written rule. (1) SEARCH — case-insensitive substring over each item's title + fact + state + skill name, the three lines `inbox_row` already draws (build.py:1869, :1882-1885). (2) FILTER — multi-select over facets that already exist as keys on the item dicts: kind (`KINDS`, build.py:1761-1769), alert `sub` (:1792/1795/1812/1822), unread (:1898), acted-on (:1894/:2281), team/scope (:1792, :1812), date (:1832). (3) SORT — newest-first (the drawn default, docstring :1896) or oldest-first over `it["date"]`, plus optionally unread-first. Facets and sort order are per-viewer view state kept by the app on this machine; nothing is written to the clone, `config.json` or `team.json`. TWO HARD APP RULES. (a) The SORT menu offers date / unread / kind only — never verdict, net lift, W/L/T or any receipt-derived order: `.planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:495` @ b5c0507 forbids ranking any skill list by a receipt number, and `:481-482` forbids merging receipts. FILTERING by verdict is NOT forbidden and the design already does it on the Marketplace (`filters_popover` verdict counts, build.py:2759-2761), so state the rule as sort-only, precisely. Do not let a filter combine numbers from different `model`/`cc_version` in one surface (:497-498). (b) NEVER run `sync` to freshen the list. `writeStamp` (`src/commands/sync.ts:347`, called for every fully-walked team at :291) writes `run/<team>.stamp`, and the SessionStart hook skips a team whose stamp is fresh (`src/commands/sync.ts:106` against `STAMP_FRESH_MS = 60 * 60_000`, `src/lib/hook.ts:149`), so any app-initiated sync silences the hook for an hour (D-R4). Label staleness with the existing `staleLine` (`src/lib/hook.ts:171`), which is exactly what `search` and `status` print for this purpose (`src/commands/search.ts:62`, `src/commands/status.ts:85`). *Why:* D20 is not reached: a query control presents no new value, it re-presents items whose truth already came from the clone / `config.pending` (`src/lib/schema.ts:99`) / `config.placements` (:100) / `evals/`. Filtering a list is not a second source of truth about the team — it is per-viewer chrome, the class the ledger carve-out names, and the design has already written the sentence for the neighbouring row: "Follows are kept by this app on this machine. The team repo has no place for them yet, so another machine starts from nothing." (build.py:3697). Refuter A is right that "no state has to be pe
- **IB-10** The rest of the indicator system: nine flags from Teddy's sketch dropped with no written reason — D20 ok. The app adds three keys to the flag vocabulary and computes them itself — no CLI change, no write of any kind: • DUPLICATE — scan `config.placements` (`src/lib/schema.ts:100` @ b5c0507) for two path keys sharing one `id`; the hover names both paths and points at the existing `uninstall-skill` (`src/cli.ts:123`). • COLLISION (the honest name for "shadowed") — a folder name present in both `LsResult.local` sections (`src/commands/ls.ts:19-20`), or equivalently in `localSkills` over both `localSkillRoots` (`src/lib/local-skills.ts:29-61`, :70). The string names the collision and NEVER the winner (`.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:343`). • OVERSIZED — the HYG6 size warning off the exported pure inspector. FOUR THINGS THE APP MUST NOT DO, each a plausible cheap win that is wrong: 1. Do NOT light Oversized from `ValidateResult.warnings > 0` (`src/commands/validate.ts:12`, set at :48-50). It works today only because HYG6-size is the single `warnings.push` in the whole file (`src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:95`; every other push in :38-99 targets `errors`, at :46, :48, :53, :65, :71, :79, :90, :97) — an undocumented accident that mislights the moment a second warning code lands. 2. Do NOT key on `code === 'HYG6'` alone. **HYG6 names two different findings**: the size WARNING at :95 and the empty-description ERROR at :97 (the CLI's own help text conflates them — "HYG6 description (size over 20,000 is a warning)", `src/cli.ts:114`). The safe key is membership in `assessment.warnings` (errors and warnings are disjoint arrays by construction, `src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:9`, comment :8: "errors gate (fail-closed); warnings are printed by every caller and gate nothing") — refuter A caught this and refuter B's own recommended route inherits the trap unless it reads the array rather than the code. 3. Do NOT call `assessHygiene` (`src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:120`) bare, as refuter B proposed: it THROWS `HygieneRefused` when there are any errors (:123), so a skill with any hygiene error would never yield an assessment. Copy the CLI's own call site verbatim — `try { assessment = assessHygiene(...) } catch (error) { if (!(error instanceof HygieneRefused)) throw error; assessment = error.assessment; }` (`src/commands/validate.ts:40-41`, the class at `src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:110-117`) — or call the pure, never-throwing `inspectHygiene` (:38) directly. Neither refuter caught the throw. 4. Do NOT run `sync` to freshen the clone before deriving. `writeStamp` (`src/commands/sync.ts:347`, called for every fully-walked team at :291) writes `run/<team>.stamp`, and the hook skips a fresh team (:106 against `STAMP_FRESH_MS = 60 * 60_000`, `src/lib/hook.ts:149`), so an app sync silences the SessionStart hook for an hour (D-R4). Derive over the clone as it stands and label freshness with the existing `staleLine` (`src/lib/hook.ts:171`). EDGE CASE neither the coster nor refuter B handled: `decodeText` can return `undefined`, so an undecodable SKILL.md scores `length = 0` (`src/lib/evals/hygiene.ts:92-94`) and never warns. An unreadable file must render as unknown, never as "not oversized". *Why:* D20 holds for all three. `duplicate` and `collision` are facts about THIS MACHINE's placements and THIS MACHINE's skills directories — there is no team-wide claim to contradict, and `config.placements` is the only record of them that exists, so there is no second source of truth. `oversized` is a derivation over a committed file that the family brief blesses, and it reads the same bytes through the same exported inspector the CLI uses, so it cannot diverge. None of the three is a count, follower, member, last-seen or favorite.
- **MC-02** Unread state for Inbox items — D20 ok. An app-owned per-machine store (the OS app-config directory of MC-08, not ~/.terum/skills) holding a set of seen item keys plus a last_opened timestamp per key. Unread = 'derived item not in the seen set'. The dot (build.py:1876), the 'N unread' header (build.py:1898), the sidebar per-kind counts (build.py:254-256) and the bell badge (build.py:3691) all read that one set. TWO boundaries, both load-bearing. (i) `acted` is DERIVED, never stored: inbox_row's acted line asserts a repo action ('Installed to Global', build.py:2281, :1884), and the truthful sources for that are config.placements (src/lib/schema.ts:100 @ b5c0507), config.approvals (src/lib/schema.ts:98 @ b5c0507) and people/<you>.json installed/declined (src/lib/schema.ts:62-63 @ b5c0507). An app-local `acted` flag would be a second source for a placement fact and fails D20. (ii) The seen-set key is only as stable as the item id the Inbox derivation produces (family IB); a key derived from mutable content - a title, a rendered timestamp - marks rows unread again on the next render, so it must come from committed identity (skill UUID + tree hash + kind). That is an IB-family obligation this row inherits and cannot settle. *Why:* D20 lists what must live in the repo - 'roster, profiles, skills, eval results, and usage selections' (.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md ledger D20). Whether I have looked at a row on THIS laptop is none of those and is not team-wide truth: two people opening the same push each see their own dot, and my second laptop legitimately starts unread. It is per-machine chrome, which the bar hands to the app by nature, and the design already applies the same standard to the neighbouring case: 'Follows are kept by this app on this machine. The team repo has no place for them yet, so anot
- **MC-03** Machine name / label in the footer — D20 ok. The Appearance row (build.py:3748) is one boolean in the app-owned preferences file of MC-08 - hide/show the footer's second line, no naming involved. *Why:* A host name is an OS fact about the machine the app runs on, not team truth - and the design says so on screen: 'Terum keeps no list of your machines and nothing here is committed.' (build.py:3632). D20 does not reach it. The coster wrote that no verb prints it; that is false - `login` prints `Identity saved: ... (@<github>) ...` (src/commands/login.ts:27 @ b5c0507). It remains true that no READ verb surfaces it (logi
- **MC-07** Default team / persisted team switcher — D20 ok. An app rule plus one preference key. RULE: the app always passes `--team <name>` explicitly on every verb that accepts it, so selectTeam's ambiguous branch (src/lib/config.ts:29 @ b5c0507) is never reached. The rule is provably sufficient at b5c0507: every caller of selectTeam belongs to a verb that accepts --team (validate.ts:22 / cli.ts:114; ls.ts:29 / cli.ts:93, :95, :96; uninstall.ts:27 / cli.ts:123; team.ts:76 / cli.ts:75; invite.ts:19 / cli.ts:91; status.ts:33 / cli.ts:97; install.ts:42, :52, :217 / cli.ts:122; eval.ts:63 / cli.ts:116; connect.ts:67 / cli.ts:119), and the two team-touching verbs with no --team never call it - sync iterates every configured team (src/commands/sync.ts:288 @ b5c0507) and search iterates every team (src/commands/search.ts:31 @ b5c0507). PREFERENCE: the last-selected team key, kept in the app-owned store of MC-08 and validated on every launch against the configured set (from `status`, which enumerates all teams at src/commands/status.ts:33); an unknown key falls back to the single configured team, or to the picker. *Why:* Which team I happen to be looking at on this laptop is per-machine chrome, not team-wide truth - D20 does not reach it - and the bar admits both halves explicitly ('ALSO SIMPLE: ... an app rule'). The launch-time validation is what keeps it honest after `team leave` (src/cli.ts:78-81 @ b5c0507) removes the entry.
- **MC-08** App preferences home (theme, startup page, date format, hide overview, bell) — D20 ok. Every value is per-machine, defaults are the drawn ones, and a missing or unparseable file reads as defaults. CARVED OUT, not included: the four eval defaults (build.py:3701-3704) and 'Commit receipts' (build.py:3711) - see condition_broken. *Why:* Per-machine chrome by nature; D20 explicitly does not reach it, and the design says so on screen (build.py:3751). The one real constraint is the file's LOCATION, and it is verified rather than assumed - though not by the coster's mechanism. Bare uninstall creates backups/ ITSELF at src/commands/uninstallMachine.ts:60 @ b5c0507, before the sweep at :120, and backups is not in the sweep list, so rmdir(store.root) ALWAYS raises ENOTEMPTY and the root is ALWAYS skipped silently at :126, app file or not. The argument that survives is narrower and still decisive: an app preferences file is not recov
- **PF-06** Joined date on the roster — D20 ok. the app runs `git log --diff-filter=A --reverse --format=%aI -- people/<handle>.json / head -1` in ~/.terum/skills/teams/<team> (or one `git log --diff-filter=A --reverse --name-only` pass over people/ for the whole roster) and renders the date. *Why:* A derivation over the repository's own committed history is repo truth, not a second source of it, so D20 (team-skill-sharing.md:133) is satisfied in full; it is available on every machine because the clone is full (src/lib/teamRepo.ts:348, `git clone -q --branch main` with no `--depth`); and it survives safeWrite's hard reset to origin/main (phase-1-build.md:303, :309), which rewrites the working tree and never the history. The command form matters: `-1` alone returns the NEWEST add, not the first, and is only accidentally right because departures archive rather than delete (team-skill-sharin
- **RM-04** Categories with counts — D20 ok. Group the skills the Result already carries by their `category` field for the tile and index counts; call the exported `readTeam(clone).categories` (or read `team.json`) for the declared list and for categories that have no skills. *Why:* The counts are a group-by over values the CLI produced, so the app is re-using the CLI's own answer rather than minting a parallel one -- D20-honest by construction. The declared list is repo content read through the CLI's own exported reader.
- **RM-05** Quarantine read-only view — D20 ok. readdir `~/.terum/skills/quarantine` for the timestamp directories, readdir one level deeper for the skill folder names, and a recursive stat for the sizes; the moved-at instant comes from the directory name (reversing the [:.] -> - substitution) with the folder mtime as a cross-check. *Why:* D20-honest and not near the line: this is per-machine recovery data that is never committed (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:102 @ b5c0507), so there is no team-wide truth to fork, and the sizes are a filesystem fact rather than a minted value. Preferring this over a CLI flag is also the honest engineering call: putting a recursive `du` inside a verb to satisfy a Settings card is worse than the app measuring its own disk.
- **RM-06** Placements view — D20 ok. Read `~/.terum/skills/config.json` (through the CLI's `ConfigStore` under the sidecar, or the file directly while mocked), render one row per `placements` key, map `version === null` to 'tracking' and otherwise to a fixed-width prefix of the hash, and derive the footer counts by `scope.kind` over the ledger itself. *Why:* Placements are per-machine local state that is never committed (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:102 and :234 @ b5c0507), so there is no team-wide truth to fork and D20's prohibition does not reach them; and the app is not minting a value, it re-renders the one file that IS the source of truth. Reading it without a lock is safe because config writes are atomic (src/lib/config.ts:97-111 @ b5c0507).
- **RM-12** Combined search over skills, people, projects (⌘K) and the undrawn no-results surface — D20 ok. The sidecar builds one in-memory ⌘K index over the clone from three already-exported readers — `skillRecords(clone, team)` (src/lib/skills.ts:20), `readPeople(clone)` (src/lib/readme.ts:142) or `readRoster(clone)` (src/lib/skills.ts:69), and `readTeam(clone).projects` (src/lib/skills.ts:63) — tags each entry with its kind, and ranks by substring match on the fields the CLI already matches (name, description, category) plus handle/display_name for people and the key for projects. Zero CLI change; the three readers are public today and RM-11 makes them importable. SCOPE LIMIT: the skill row (build.py:4147) and the project row (build.py:4149) are derivable as drawn; the PERSON row's meta as drawn (`{handle} · {role}`, build.py:4148) is NOT — `role` has no committed source (src/lib/schema.ts:54-64, src/lib/skills.ts:66). The app renders `handle · display_name` (src/lib/schema.ts:55-56) or `handle` plus the first clause of `bio` (src/lib/schema.ts:61) instead. *Why:* D20-honest: every row is a rendering of a committed file (skills/*/SKILL.md, people/*.json, team.json), rebuilt on every read, with no app-local store and no second source of truth — the bar names 'a purely app-side derivation over committed files' as SIMPLE explicitly, and ledger D20 (.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:133) forbids a second SOURCE, not a derived view. No sync interaction: the index reads the clone as it stands, exactly as `search` does (src/commands/search.ts:62-63), so D-R4 is not engaged. An app-local `role` value WOULD fail D20 (it is team-wide truth about a 
- **RM-17** Progress events and cancellation on long verbs (sync, eval) — D20 ok. Drive the four drawn phases by polling three machine-local artifacts FOR THE LIFETIME OF THE SYNC THE USER STARTED, with no CLI change. Phase 1 ('Team terum found · @teddy') is a config read taken BEFORE the call - config.teams[<team>].handle (teamConfigSchema, src/lib/schema.ts:72 @ b5c0507) - and is already true when the sync begins. Phase 2 ('Fetched … · main') is the weakest: it is inferable only from the clone's FETCH_HEAD/HEAD mtime under <store.root>/teams/<team> (src/lib/config.ts:91-93 teamClone @ b5c0507), refreshed at src/commands/sync.ts:117. Phase 3's counter is exact and durable: installOne rewrites config.placements through store.update after EACH placement (src/commands/install.ts:122-124 @ b5c0507). THE NUMERATOR IS NOT Object.keys(config.placements).length - placements is keyed by absolute path and is machine-wide across every team and both scopes (src/lib/schema.ts:100 @ b5c0507), so on a two-team or project-scoped machine that count is already past the denominator before the first Global skill lands. The honest numerator counts entries whose .team === team and .scope.kind === 'global' and whose .id is in team.json.global (src/lib/schema.ts:47 @ b5c0507); the denominator is team.json.global's length, which is what the board's '4 of 7' means (build.py:4035, GLOBAL_SET at build.py:3886). Phase 4 flips when run/<team>.stamp appears - path from the EXPORTED stampPath (src/lib/hook.ts:157 @ b5c0507), written at src/commands/sync.ts:291. *Why:* D20 is not engaged: none of these is a team-wide count, follower, member or last-seen - they are per-machine facts, and the files read ARE their source of truth (.planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:133 @ b5c0507). Refuter B's refutation of the coster's RM-23 dependency is correct and I adopt it: a bounded poll inside ONE user-started mutation's lifetime is not RM-23's watcher - it re-stats by path each tick, so writeAtomically's rename (src/lib/config.ts:107 @ b5c0507) is invisible to it; there is no four-source invalidation matrix (gap-4.md:57), no >=250 ms debounce, no .lock/.tm
- **RM-28** Endorsement is a lossy display string on the only verb that carries it, and it masks project membership — D20 ok. An APP RULE with zero CLI lines, in three clauses. (a) The badge on a card or detail header is the CLI's badge — `skillEndorsement`'s output, global-first — so what the app shows equals what the README a teammate reads shows. (b) Project membership is expressed by WHICH SURFACES a skill appears on, never by the badge: a project page's contents come from `ls project <name>` (src/cli.ts:96) or from `team.projects[<name>].skills` in the committed team.json, which is exactly what `skills_in` already does in the design (build.py:2420-2421). (c) A person page's bucket order, which the design takes from `LIST_OF[...][0]` (build.py:2426, i.e. the fixture's tuple order), is global-first-then-alphabetical — the badge's own precedence (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:343). Every multi-entry tuple in the fixture is `("Global", ...)`, so the rule reproduces the mock exactly. *Why:* Honest under D20 — read from the committed team.json, the same bytes teamSchema parses, no app-local store and no second source of truth — and the bar names “an app rule” as SIMPLE outright. It is also more faithful to D17 (“scope is which team.json lists reference its ID”, .planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:125) than any badge, because it never collapses the lists at all. The rule costs nothing and, unlike a second endorsement implementation in the app, cannot drift from `skillEndorsement`'s precedence (src/lib/readme.ts:48).
- **RM-33** No serialization for mutating verbs: four lock families either throw at once or skip in silence — D20 ok. One single-flight mutation queue inside the Backend adapter, FIFO, at most one mutating verb in flight. The verb list is every caller that takes a lock or reaches safeWrite: install, uninstall-skill, uninstall, connect, publish, sync, team create, team join (src/commands/team.ts:335), team remove (src/commands/team.ts:123), team leave, eval --commit — and setup, which chains several of them and, since c13d0e5, connects several local skills in one run (src/commands/setup.ts:28 offerConnect, :191). invite is correctly absent (gh only, no safeWrite). Three clauses beyond 'one at a time': (1) a ChangeEvent may invalidate queries but must NEVER start a verb, or the app races the hook that fired it (gap-4.md:667-670); (2) no timer sync — an app sync writes run/<team>.stamp on success (src/commands/sync.ts:291) which suppresses the hook's sync for an hour (src/lib/hook.ts:149 STAMP_FRESH_MS, :165-167; gate at src/commands/sync.ts:106), so a timer would silently disable the SessionStart hook for as long as the app is open (D-R4, gap-4.md:59 and :377-390); (3) the queue needs a DEADLINE shorter than LOCK_STALE_MS — a queued mutation parked on a modal question for more than ten minutes has its team mutex reclaimed on age alone (src/lib/hook.ts:224, :153) and two syncs then run into one clone. Also: an in-process sync caller must set noUpdateCheck in SyncArgs deliberately (src/commands/sync.ts:25, env-only at src/index.ts:37), or every app sync makes an outbound git ls-remote to the approved upstream. Reads take no lock and need no queue, but must degrade per row rather than per surface (src/commands/ls.ts:59; gap-4.md:671-677). *Why:* A mutation queue is per-machine chrome over the app's own outbound calls; it publishes no value, so D20 cannot be violated by it. gap-4.md:663-666 already states the requirement in the same words ('A single in-app mutation queue, one at a time, is mandatory').
- **RM-34** Prompts must cross the seam as a round trip, and the questions are untyped English strings — D20 ok. The app implements Prompter itself — exactly the role .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:59 assigns it — and renders a GENERIC question dialog from (question, choices?) with Cancel plus the affirmative, upgrading recognised questions to the drawn bespoke dialogs (build.py:1546). Four clauses the rule must carry. (1) The recogniser is a PREFIX/PATTERN table, not exact match: only four confirms and one select are constant literals (auth.ts:43, auth.ts:84, uninstallMachine.ts:58, team.ts:493, setup.ts:100 ROLE_QUESTION); fourteen interpolate, and team.ts:121 is one of two runtime variants (team.ts:120). Anything unmatched falls through to the generic dialog. (2) The dialog BODY is not in the question: install.ts:152 and sync.ts:190 are each preceded by an io.print of the grant text (sync.ts:189) and connect.ts:200 by the 'Will add: ...' block (connect.ts:199), so the adapter must accumulate print lines since the last question and use them as the pending dialog's body — that is how dialog()'s body/extra slots get filled. (3) Cancel must reject with PromptClosedError (src/lib/prompt.ts:29), not a plain Error: sync.ts:244 and :270 rethrow only that class, and any other rejection becomes a per-placement 'Blocked <path>: ...' line while the loop keeps asking, so a window closed mid-sync would produce N spurious Blocked notices and leave the team unstamped (sync.ts:73, :291). (4) text() is not optional: askUntilValid (auth.ts:120-129) re-asks with a rule string until the value validates, driving four identity questions (auth.ts:87-95), so the generic dialog needs a validating text field with a default. One entry in the table is a deliberate, permanent false: auth.ts:43's gh offer, because auth.ts:44 would hand the app's stdio to an interactive gh in a window with no TTY. The design already draws the honest replacement (build.py:3539-3540). *Why:* No team-wide value is involved; a dialog is per-machine chrome over a round trip the spec already assigns to this side of the seam, so D20 is not engaged. The honesty risk is silence, not D20: the false for auth.ts:43 must be a written rule with a test, because the fall-through default for an unrecognised confirm is cancel and cancel happens to be the right answer there — an app that got it right by accident would get it wrong the moment the default changed.
- **RM-35** `interactive` is a semantics switch, not a formatting flag, so the app cannot run any verb quietly — D20 ok. A recorded app rule with three clauses: (1) the app's Prompter supplies interactive: true — exactly what .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:59 says the phase-2 channel does — and holds a live question channel open for the whole duration of every verb (RM-34); (2) it answers auth.ts:43's gh offer with a deliberate false, backed by a test, because auth.ts:44 inherits stdio into a window with no TTY, and points the user at the drawn Sign in row instead (build.py:3539-3540); (3) it sets noUpdateCheck in SyncArgs deliberately (src/commands/sync.ts:25; the value is env-derived only at src/index.ts:37), because interactive:true is also what arms the release probe at src/commands/sync.ts:45-48. With those three, Prune and Connect work as drawn. *Why:* No team-wide value; the flag governs this machine's channel only, so D20 is not engaged. The rule is honest only if clause (2) is explicit — an app that supplies interactive: true and lets auth.ts:44 run does not hang gracefully, it hangs. That is why the CLI split is the correct fix and the app rule is the cheap one: the rule is a line of policy plus a test, the split makes the mistake structurally impossible.
- **RM-36** Per-file change list with added/removed line counts (update items and the local-edit alert) — D20 ok. Two git invocations against the team clone the CLI already maintains: `git -C <clone> diff --name-status <treeA> <treeB>` for FILE/CHANGE and `git -C <clone> diff --numstat <treeA> <treeB>` for LINES, where treeA is the placement's ledger version (src/lib/schema.ts:100) or the incumbent version named on the Inbox item and treeB is the candidate tree. Measured at git 2.53.0 on a two-commit skill-tree fixture: the combined `--numstat --name-status` prints ONLY the name-status block, so one call cannot produce the drawn table; a rename prints as `R100 run.sh run2.sh` / `0 0 run.sh => run2.sh` with rename detection ON by default; a binary file prints `- - blob.bin`; and a `-- skills/<name>` pathspec against a skill tree returns nothing (exit 0). For the local-edit alert (build.py:1812) the two sides are the clone's tree and the placed folder, and this half is CONDITIONAL: the materialized cache entry (~/.terum/skills/cache/<team>/<tree>/<name>, src/lib/version.ts:36) exists only when the placement is pinned — src/commands/sync.ts:184 materializes only if entry.version, and a bare `install <name>` records version: null (src/commands/install.ts:89), which is the default. For an unpinned placement the app must either write into ~/.terum (a second writer of the tool's state root, which gap-4.md:707 OD-4 argues against) or materialize into a temp directory of its own; the honest degradation is that the 'edited locally' flag still renders and the +9 −2 does not. *Why:* D20-clean. A diff between two committed trees is a recomputation of the team repo's own content — every teammate running the same two hashes gets the same table — not a second source of truth, which is what D20 forbids. That is materially different from an app-local follower count, which has no committed source at all.
- **RM-46** ls --local sees only the global root unless the app passes a cwd, and the nearest-repo rule under-reports what Claude Code loads — D20 ok. One `ls --local` call per project root the app knows about, each with that root as cwd, merged with the global-root-only call under the CLI's own precedence rule; plus one caption stating the nearest-repository narrowing. The root set comes from MC-01's already-reconciled app-side derivation: every key of config.placements is an absolute placed path carrying scope {kind:'project', project} (src/lib/schema.ts:100), so the checkout root is the key with /.claude/skills/<name> stripped (cost-MC1.final.json, MC-01 app_side (a), final_verdict SIMPLE). *Why:* The list is paths on this laptop - machine scope, not team-wide truth - so D20 does not forbid an app-side merge. Each ROW is returned verbatim by a CLI call; the MERGE is a small derivation the app must do honestly, because every run re-emits the global root (src/lib/local-skills.ts:30 seeds it unconditionally and the realpath dedupe at :57-59 is within ONE run only) and spec :343 sets the precedence. THE LIVE DEPENDENCY, recorded rather than papered over: that derivation requires the app to read config.json. gap-4.md:707 (OD-4) rejected option (c), "the app reads run/<team>.stamp and config.
- **RM-50** The Markdown link-label rule has to be re-applied wherever the app renders repo text — D20 ok. One components map plus a site-selection rule, both inside the app repo. Site selection: the card description (build.py:683), the tabs_row file names (:1161-1169) and the frontmatter block's contents (:1194-1195, rendered at :1202) are React text nodes, never routed through Markdown — and inlineText must NOT be applied to them, because a text node does no backslash-escape processing and would print a literal backslash. Only the SKILL.md body is parsed, with four map rules: no rehype-raw; a urlTransform allowing only http/https/mailto; no images; and a visible destination for every link. One unit test per rule under the app's vitest + testing-library setup (frontend-stack.md:35), seeded with the CLI's adversarial strings (src/lib/__tests__/readme.test.ts:39, :45), plus one test confirming whether the components map filters at all or whether allowedElements/disallowedElements is required. *Why:* A render rule is not a value, so D20 is not engaged — nothing is being presented as team-wide truth. The image rule is not a new privacy call either: frontend-stack.md:33 already decides the app fetches nothing at runtime, so dropping images applies an existing app decision rather than making a new one. The work is a components map decision 7 already budgets for plus roughly four lines of policy, entirely in the app repo Teddy owns.
- **RM-51** The author-to-person join is a normalized 'Display Name <email>' string, not a handle — D20 ok. One derivation, written once in the Backend adapter and used by MarketplacePerson, MarketplacePeople and SkillDetail's author block: enumerate with skillRecords (RM-49's path, which also yields description), read every people/<handle>.json, and bucket each skill by normalizeAuthor(metadata.author) === normalizeAuthor(`${display_name} <${email}>`) — the exact predicate at src/commands/ls.ts:68, with normalizeAuthor copied verbatim from src/lib/guard.ts:146-148 (one line; there is no package export to import). The join is over ALL people files, not only active members: the CLI attributes across every people file (ls.ts:66-68) and the README's per-author sections carry no roster filter (src/lib/readme.ts:62-66), so an active-only rule would make the person page, ls member and the committed README disagree about the same archived teammate. A skill matching no people file goes into an UNATTRIBUTED bucket and its author block shows the raw metadata.author string with no avatar, handle or role, rather than being dropped or guessed at. MarketplacePeople's per-person counts derive from the same buckets, so those three screens cannot disagree. *Why:* Authorship IS team-wide truth, so D20 bites — but this derivation reads only committed files and applies the shipped predicate, so it is the same source of truth, not a second one. D20 is satisfied precisely by the discipline of never inventing a fallback join: no email-local-part matching, no handle written into frontmatter (which schema.ts:111's passthrough would silently accept), no 'closest name', and above all not search --author's substring (search.ts:44), which would attribute skills the write guard (guard.ts:121-139) would refuse to let that person touch — confidently wrong, and worse

Use them at :852 and substitute at the five other occurrences of the old literal — :880, :898 (activity PROSE inside DETAIL's own record, unreachable by any dict-key gate, the miniature of the coverage problem), :1796, :1818, :1819. `check_history`'s `history[0]["version"] == d["version"]` assert (:538-539) keeps passing by construction. (2) FIX THE HARD-CODED ID in the same function: build.py:1194 becomes `id: {s.get("id", SAMPLE_SKILL_ID)}`, and DETAIL_NO_RECEIPT / DETAIL_PARTIAL get their own `id=` values so no board shows one skill's id over another's version. (3) WIDEN THE GATE OVER build.py's OWN SOURCE TEXT, at the call site it already has:     HEX12 = re.compile(r"(?<![0-9a-f-])[0-9a-f]{12}(?![0-9a-f-])")   # a UUID's blocks are hyphen-adjacent and are skipped     VERSIONS = { … the 16 declared version strings … }     def check_versions(details, source: str) -> None:         problems = [ … the existing three-rule check, unchanged … ]         dashless = SAMPLE_SKILL_ID.replace("-", "")         for token in sorted(set(HEX12.findall(source))):             if token not in VERSIONS: problems.append(f"hash-shaped string {token} is not a declared version (add it to VERSIONS or fix the copy)")             if token in dashless:     problems.append(f"version {token} is a slice of the sample skill id; a tree hash and a UUID are unrelated (schema.ts:21 vs readme.ts:119)")         if problems: raise SystemExit("versions inconsistent:\n  " + "\n  ".join(problems)) called as `check_versions([DETAIL, DETAIL_NOT_INSTALLED, DETAIL_NO_RECEIPT, DETAIL_PARTIAL], Path(__file__).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))` at build.py:4513 — same line, before a single board is written. I verified the three properties this rests on, at the snapshot: (a) that regex yields exactly 16 distinct tokens over 36 occurrences and drops only `8a6d0c3e9b47`, so the UUID false positive is gone; (b) `grep -n 'version_full\[/\[:12\]/version\[:'` returns NOTHING — no hash is ever sliced or composed, so every hash reaching the HTML is a literal in the source and a source scan is a strict SUPERSET of a scan of the rendered boards; (c) the collision test is two-directional (`token in dashless`), unlike the coster's `id.startswith(token)`. *CLI side:* NA — The CLI cannot police a mock's fixtures and nothing it does is wrong here — skillIdSchema and latestTree are already unambiguous about what each value is. No CLI-side alternative exists. This is generator work in Teddy's own file.

(2) Rewrite the item state string at build.py:1811 from "Approve and merge, or request changes" to "Review on GitHub". (3) Keep build.py:2095 (the abstract is accurate about what a merge does) and promote build.py:2102's terminal hint from footnote to second affordance. Boards to re-render: Inbox, InboxPanes, InboxStates. *CLI side:* DEFERRED — The coster recorded HARD on the grounds that a host merge 'routes around both guards'. That reasoning does not survive the pin. The write guard runs inside safeWrite on the staged diff (src/lib/guard.ts:43 @ b5c0507) and the push guard in the clone's pre-push hook (src/lib/guard.ts:88-90 @ b5c0507 via src/lib/teamRepo.ts:410-419 @ b5c0507), but the CONTENT a merge lands was already authorized by r
- **TJ-12** 'The list is admin-extendable': no verb writes team.json categories, policy or projects, and two guards refuse a hand edit — Three string- or data-level edits on SettingsTeams (and its SettingsStates specimens): (1) STRIKE "the list is admin-extendable" from build.py:3575. No mechanism exists behind it and the field is inert - src/commands/team.ts:523 @ b5c0507 is its only writer and nothing in src/ reads it, so extending it would change no behaviour even if a verb existed. Honest replacement: "From SKILL.md frontmatter. team.json lists the team's own; nothing enforces it." (2) THE ROW RENDERS THE WRONG LIST. build.py:3573 feeds it the catalog's frontmatter-derived `CATEGORIES` (build.py:2372-2375, ten keys) under a card labelled "Team policy", while `team.json.categories` is the seven at src/commands/team.ts:42 @ b5c0507 - only four overlap (infra, docs, testing, research) - and the tool writes an eighth default, `misc`, into skills that declare none (src/lib/skills.ts:118,153 @ b5c0507). Three lists, none governing another. Either render team.json's array here, or - equally cheap and arguably more honest, and one edit rather than a rewrite - DELETE the Categories row from the read-only Team policy card entirely, since the Marketplace already frames the frontmatter chips correctly twice (build.py:2999, :3004) and `TEAM_POLICY` (build.py:3368) carries no categories key. (3) OPTIONAL tightening of the note at build.py:3580: "It changes by a pull request on GitHub" is true only of GitHub's web editor - a branch carrying that change and pushed from the clone is refused by the pre-push guard (src/lib/guard.ts:90 @ b5c0507 via src/commands/guardPush.ts:56-60 @ b5c0507). "Edit team.json on GitHub" is the precise phrasing. *CLI side:* SIMPLE — As drawn the row is already read-only and the note beneath it already names an out-of-band route, so no code is wanted. The only CLI-repo work is one clarifying paragraph in one section with one approver - the spec-delta shape the bar names SIMPLE. A VERB would be HARD and is worth stating so nobody proposes it: a new section 6.0 permitted-writes row (the table at spec:317-324 @ b5c0507 is closed,
- **TJ-13** Archived people are counted: 'N teammates use this' claims current membership over a total that includes departures — Change the TENSE, keep the noun, at five sites; leave four alone. CHANGE: build.py:1513 (`{n} teammate{s} use{s} this` -> `{n} teammate{s} ha{s/ve} installed this`); build.py:1010 (popover header "Used by {installs_n} teammates" -> "Installed by {installs_n} teammates"); build.py:1480 (the Details row is already labelled "Installs", so its value should be the bare number, not "{n} teammates"); build.py:1715 (the hover specimen's literal "12 teammates use this"); build.py:4143 (Onboarding's Share panel - the catalogue row and the coster both cite :4112, which is blank space inside `basics_panel`). ALSO fix the stated invariant at build.py:2309 and the comment at build.py:690-691 - "install counts come from their people files, so none exceeds 12" is false against src/lib/readme.ts:35 @ b5c0507, because a departed installer's people file is still counted; and build.py:2765's filter track divides `installs_min` by `TEAM_N`, so it needs a denominator that is not the active roster (or a clamped ratio). LEAVE ALONE: build.py:685/:694 (already "installs"); build.py:2851 ("{members} teammates use this project" is a roster count, and check_market:2482-2485 proves it equals the active members on that project); build.py:2101 ("{installs_n} teammates installed it ... none declined it" - already the perfect tense over a historical total). OPTIONAL, and the only way the mock can ever show the difference: relax `check_market` (build.py:2445, :2447-2449) enough to admit one archived installer and add a board state for it. *CLI side:* CLOSED — The decision is made at b5c0507 and documented in the code that makes it: src/lib/readme.ts:35 states that totals include archived people on purpose, and src/lib/readme.ts:51-57 supplies the active-membership filter separately for the callers that want it. The CLI never launders the total into a present-tense membership claim (src/lib/readme.ts:77, src/commands/ls.ts:84 @ b5c0507). Nothing is want
- **TJ-O2** The joiner's invitation-acceptance gate — a browser hand-off and a blocking confirm — is drawn nowhere — Three edits, all design-side. (1) Two captions become conditional, in the CLI's own already-shipped words: build.py:4178 (f"{INVITE_TIP}, which accepts the invitation and offers the Global set.") and build.py:3167 ('… which accepts it and offers the Global set.') -> the sentence at invite.ts:54, which phase-1-build.md:339 mandates verbatim, so this is spec conformance rather than taste. INVITE_TIP itself (build.py:3080) is fine; only the appended clause over-promises. (team.ts:492) and 'Continue after accepting the invitation?' (team.ts:493) with its two answers and the decline error. NOT owed: the coster's third edit (a provenance sentence on OnboardingBoot, build.py:4034) — the Boot board correctly presumes a completed join for the terminal route, and the app-driven route belongs to the Settings surface in (3). *CLI side:* CLOSED — Every string and every branch the app needs is shipped and asserted at b5c0507: the URL print (team.ts:492), the blocking confirm and its decline error (team.ts:493), the auto-accept branch (team.ts:496-502), the pre-notices (setup.ts:114-116), the conditional sentence (invite.ts:54, echoed verbatim in phase-1-build.md:339), the <org>/<repo> precondition (team.ts:322, :473-481) and the interactive

## 4. MEDIUM and HARD (16 rows): the bar condition broken, and what the app ships meanwhile

### IB-03 — Off-target telemetry (alert · offtarget: sessions, prompts, precision) *(HARD; recommended: cut)*

- **Condition broken:** All of: a collector; an event log; a privacy call over the user's literal prompts (build.py:1797-1798, :2021); and a mechanism that does not exist. Forcing citations: .planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:228 @ b5c0507 (the binding one — it blocks the work before engineering starts), src/lib/hook.ts:12-13 @ b5c0507 with .planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:431 @ b5c0507 (the single SessionStart entry the settings contract admits), .planning/specs/2026-09-04-eval-engine.md:346 @ b5c0507 (trigger measurement is sandbox-only by design). Refuter B's alternative label is recorded rather than silently taken: under the bar's taxonomy DEFERRED ('decided elsewhere') is the more precise reason this row is not costed, since :228 is a scope non-goal rather than an unbuilt mechanism. HARD is kept because it is the number Teddy needs — what it would cost if the non-goal were lifted.
- **CLI side:** HARD — Four NOT-SIMPLE triggers fire at once, and a fifth gate stands in front of all of them. (1) A COLLECTOR — the family brief names it HARD by construction. (2) A NEW EVENT LOG — no such store exists and neither owner file can hold per-session rows. (3) A SECOND HOOK ENTRY: src/lib/hook.ts:12-13 @ b5c0507 installs exactly one SessionStart command under a settings-mutation contract (.planning/specs/2026-09-02-phase-1-build.md:429-436 @ b5c0507) whose idempotency key is 'a hook command containing terum-skills inside any SessionStart group' (:431); a differently-triggered hook is a new mechanism under that contract, not an increment. (4) A PRIVACY CALL: the design writes the user's literal prompts to disk and renders them (build.py:1797-1798, :2021). (5) The gate: .planning/specs/2026-09-01-team-skill-sharing.md:228 @ b5c0507 says no. Note one precision fix to the coster's write-up, from refut
- **Meanwhile, app side:** FORBIDDEN (D20 no) — The app would install its own Claude Code hook, keep its own session log, and present 'observed precision 0.64' beside the receipt's measured precision.
